# KnowFlow — Progress Tracker

**Single source of truth for project state.** Built from the actual record: git
commit history + [`PIVOT_PLAN.md`](./PIVOT_PLAN.md) + project memory. Update this
file at the **end of every step** so we never jump ahead on a deferred item or
skip a main phase.

- **Last updated:** 2026-09-24 - see [PROGRESS §7 Changelog](#7-changelog). This field carries the date only; the narrative lives in §7.
- **Active branch / last landed:** `feat/61b-account-deletion` (PR **#91**) - **FEATURE.** Closes register **#61(b)**: `DELETE /api/account`, the deletion orchestrator, the Settings card with a server-checked typed-email confirmation, and the privacy page corrected forward. **Both stores require this** (Apple 5.1.1(v); Google Play's requirement is a superset). Strings ship in **Arabic and English both**, enforced by `ar.ts`'s `: Translation` annotation and therefore by `tsc`. **No DDL and no migration.** **THE ORDERING IS NOW EXERCISED, NOT ARGUED** - Pass A ran it end to end on a throwaway with a real storage object and a real waitlist row, and the Paddle cancel branch ran against a real sandbox subscription (§7 2026-09-04). **The branch carries three commits and the third reverses the second:** `1c525f1` latched the 409 control off and was proven as behaviour; experiment (iii) showed the requirement was false; `000d2e2` reverses it and rewrites both locale strings, because **retrying costs the user nothing and completes the deletion**. The latch described in earlier readings of this PR **no longer exists**. **THIS FIELD IS SCOPED TO THE 2026-09-03/04 DELETION ARC AND IS NOT A LIVE POINTER — annotated 2026-09-11, not rewritten.** It is the same caveat the line below carries and this line lacked. `main` has since taken every PR through **#143** (`d9d6f40`, 2026-09-10), so `feat/61b-account-deletion` has not been the active branch for eight days. **§6’s maintenance list names *Last updated* and does not name this field**, which is why nothing caught it — and *Last updated* itself read `2026-09-09` on a file whose newest §7 entry was dated 2026-09-10, so the one field that does have a rule was also two days stale. The live answers are `git rev-parse origin/main` and `gh pr list --state merged --limit 1`.
- **Main tip at authoring (this PR's base):** `4d5f46a` (PR **#95** merge - the browser-side Paddle environment), on top of `b26ad57` (PR **#93** - the server-side switch) and `3598a8f` (PR **#94** - the `.env*` glob). All three landed during the 2026-09-03/04 deletion arc and `feat/61b-account-deletion` is rebased onto them. **This field is scoped to the moment of writing and is NOT a live pointer** - `git rev-parse origin/main` is.

> ✅ **RESOLVED 2026-07-04 — Supabase project back online (register #21).** The
> project **auto-paused** (free-tier inactivity) and then hit Supabase's free
> **active-project limit**, which is enforced **per Owner across every organization
> they own**. It was fixed **without any project migration**: the project was moved
> into a **new organization** owned by a second real account, and the old account's
> membership was removed from that org — clearing the limit and letting the project
> resume. **The project URL and API keys did not change, so no app code, env,
> re-embedding, or rebuild was needed;** verified by a successful login. The
> heavyweight move planned in [`supabase-migration-runbook.md`](./supabase-migration-runbook.md)
> was **never executed** and is now superseded. **Phase 2's paused Arabic/RTL
> visual verification can now resume.**
>
> ⚠️ This is a dev-time fix, **not** a launch foundation — see register **#22**
> (free tier is not production-grade; a stable, policy-compliant backend is a
> pre-launch decision).

Migration `20260708_quizzes.sql` **applied to the live DB and verified**. Design decisions → register **#28**; the leak-guard follow-up → register **#29**. <br><br>**P4.1 (quiz-generation route) ✅ DONE** — PR **#28**, merge `5338ffb` (`feat/phase-4-quiz-generate`; merge commit). Ships **`POST /api/quiz/generate`**: **document-scoped** generation via Haiku (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`), **strict-JSON** prompt + **assistant prefill**, **defensive parse + all-or-nothing validation** (→ **502**, **no partial persist**), **cache-existence check BEFORE `enforceLimit`** (a cached re-read never charges), **content guard before `enforceLimit`**, `enforceLimit('quiz')` in front of the paid call, **`is_partial` persisted on generate + read on cache-hit**, **`correct_index` server-only** (never selected in a read, never returned). Migration `20260708_quizzes_is_partial.sql` **applied to the live DB and verified** (`is_partial boolean NOT NULL default false`, confirmed via `information_schema`). **Live-verified on Preview `af2ccbe`** — evidence recorded in register **#29** (we prove, we don't assume). New: register **#30** (non-atomic two-insert creation, accepted) and register **#31** (quiz language permanently fixed at generation time). <br><br>**P4.2 (quiz grading route) ✅ DONE** — PR **#30**, merge `ad2b55d` (`feat/phase-4-quiz-submit`; merge commit). Ships **`POST /api/quiz/submit`**: **server-side grading**, **UUID shape-check before any DB query** (**400**, never `22P02` → **500**), **401**, **RLS-scoped 404** (not-found ≡ not-owned), **zero-item guard** (**409** — register **#30**'s orphan window), **200-answer cap**, **first-occurrence-wins** on duplicate `item_id`, **unanswered = incorrect**, **foreign `item_id`s ignored**, **no `enforceLimit`** (grading is unpaid; charging `'quiz'` would drain the **generation** cap), **fail-open `quiz_attempts` insert**, index on **`(quiz_id, created_at desc)`**. **Feedback model:** multiple attempts allowed; **`correct_index` revealed per item ONLY for items attempted with an IN-RANGE `selected_index`** (an out-of-range guess is **not** an attempt and earns no reveal). Migration `20260709_quiz_attempts.sql` **applied to the live DB and verified** (columns, three CHECKs, FK cascade, three-hop RLS policy). **Live-verified on Preview `9f26a53`** — evidence named in register **#29**. New: register **#32** (submission throttle, deferred) and register **#33** (`quiz_attempts` is write-only + best-effort). <br><br>**P4.3 (quiz-taking UI) ✅ DONE** — PR **#32**, merge `0910e63` (`feat/phase-4-quiz-ui`; merge commit). Ships **`QuizSection`** beside `SummarySection` on the document-detail page. The quiz is fetched via **`POST /api/quiz/generate`** (cached hit = **free**), and the UI makes **ZERO client-side reads of `quizzes`/`quiz_items`** — **register #29 closed STRUCTURALLY, not by discipline.** Two traps in **named helpers**: **`revealedIndex()`** tests **`'correct_index' in r`** (truthiness would make a correct answer of **option 0** permanently invisible); **`answeredIndex()`** returns `null` out-of-range, so `options[]` is never indexed with a raw echoed `selected_index`. **Error map read from the route bytes**, not copied from `summarize`: generate also returns **503** (`enforceLimit` fail-closed) and **400**; **submit's 409 = "quiz has no items"**, a *different meaning* from summarize's 409 "still processing" → **separate strings**. Correct-state styling reuses the **`--primary` tokens** (already emerald-700) rather than raw `green-700`, avoiding a second near-but-unequal green (the drift **P2.7** purged). **Live-verified on Preview `b8a9198`** — evidence named in register **#29**. |
| **5** | Streak & progress | ✅ **DONE** | **All four steps P5.0 → P5.3 are merged, and every emit site is now proven live — the last, `material_uploaded`, was exercised on 2026-07-11: a real upload produced exactly one `study_events` row `kind='material_uploaded'` at 10:57:30 with the document at `status='ready'` (`embedding_status='ready'`, `chunk_count=3`).** <br><br>**Why this was held at 🚧 until 2026-07-11 (Abo Jad, 2026-07-10 — do not re-litigate):** the disagreement was never about confidence in the ingest emit's correctness — it was about what the word **"done"** means. Phases 3 and 4 were flipped to ✅ only after **everything in them had been exercised live**. Flipping Phase 5 with one of four emit sites never having fired would redefine "done" as **"mostly proven"** — a drift in exactly the language that protects us. This very session caught real defects in three places that felt beyond doubt: a migration filename that sorted before the table it altered, a flat `AND` chain Postgres may reorder, and a non-null predicate that let `selected_index: 99` harvest the answer key. **"Least in doubt" is not evidence.** The cost of waiting is **zero** — the first real upload flips it. The live proof arrived on 2026-07-11: the first real upload flipped it, and the wait cost zero, exactly as predicted. <br><br>**EXIT CONDITION — MET 2026-07-11.** A real upload produced **exactly one** `study_events` row `kind='material_uploaded'` (10:57:30) **AND** the document reached `status='ready'` (`embedding_status='ready'`, `chunk_count=3`) — both assertions satisfied, the second by the observed result independently of code. The second-assertion hazard — ingest's final `documents` status `UPDATE` had a pre-existing **unchecked error** (documented as register **#38**'s risk (2)), which could have left a row `status='processing'` while the route returned `success:true` **and emitted anyway** — was **fixed in PR #41 (merge `c7336ce`)** and observed **not** to have fired on this upload, so it can no longer recur. <br><br>**"Merged"/"DONE" does NOT close the cross-cutting items P5 surfaced**, which stay open + tracked: registers **#35**, **#36** (both risks open — the `record_study_event` RPC grant to `authenticated`, and the client-asserted `current_streak` timezone, both Phase-9 triggers), and **#37**. The agent-emit teardown-loss risk (register **#38**'s risk (1)) also remains open. Register **#38**'s coverage gap is now **CLOSED** (see the emitter verification below) and its risk (2), the unchecked ready-write, is resolved by PR **#41**. <br><br>**P5.0 (drop `quiz_attempts`)** — PR **#34**, merge `f4b9c94`. That table cascaded `quiz_attempts → quizzes → documents`, so deleting one material erased every attempt beneath it — right for content-lifecycle data, **catastrophic for a streak** (an immutable claim about what the student did). Grading is now **stateless**; response `{ quiz_id, score, total, results }` unchanged. Verified live: table absent, quiz still graded 2/5 on preview `36fe60e`. <br><br>**P5.1 (create `study_events`)** — PR **#35**, merge `e9b3e27`. Migration `20260709_study_events.sql`: `study_events` (`id`, `user_id → auth.users on delete cascade`, `kind text` + fail-closed CHECK, `occurred_at timestamptz`), a **read-own SELECT policy with NO insert/update/delete policy**, the `(user_id, occurred_at desc)` index, and the **`record_study_event(text)` SECURITY DEFINER RPC** — the only write path. Live-verified: `pg_policies` shows **one** policy, `cmd = SELECT`; **three** constraints (PK, `kind` CHECK, `auth.users` FK, no FK to any content table); `prosecdef = true`, `proconfig search_path=public`. <br><br>**P5.2 (four emitters)** — PR **#36**, merge `11354da`. `src/lib/study-events.ts` is the single write path (RPC only, **fails open, loudly**); the four emitters fire on their **success paths only** — `quiz_submitted` (gated on ≥1 in-range attempted answer, sharing the hoisted `isAttempted` predicate with the answer reveal), `summary_generated` (after persist; **not** on the cached branch), `question_asked` (**inside the stream closure**, after the for-await drains — the route returns a 200 stream before Claude speaks, and the conversation-limit denial also returns 200), and `material_uploaded` (after `status='ready'`). <br><br>**P5.3 (streak query + timezone + placeholder)** — PR **#37**, merge `fb8f34d`; Arabic plural fix + preview-rebuild commits on the same branch. Migration `20260710_current_streak.sql`: **`current_streak(text)`, SECURITY INVOKER**, buckets `occurred_at` via `at time zone` into the student's local days and walks the `(user_id, occurred_at desc)` index newest-first, exiting at the first gap. Zone is an **IANA name** (never an offset — Ramadan reverts Morocco to UTC+0), supplied per request by the browser, **validated against `pg_timezone_names`**, falling back to `Africa/Casablanca`. **Grace decision:** newest study day must be today or yesterday; if yesterday, the streak survives until today ends and shows the run through yesterday. `null` (not measured) stays distinct from `0` (earned zero). `dashboard/page.tsx` **stays a server component** (a `<TimeZoneSync/>` client child writes a `kf_tz` cookie and refreshes once). Register **#14** resolved — the P2.2 placeholder took the real number with **no prop-shape change**, exactly as predicted. Live-verified on preview `537c7bc`, cold path: card rendered a real **1** (not the ghost — a green build alone would have shown the ghost), hint `بتوقيتك المحلّي` beneath it, plural reading **"1 يوم"** not "1 أيام". `current_streak('Africa/Casablanca')` from the SQL editor raised `P0001 not authenticated` — fail-closed with no `auth.uid()`, intended. <br><br>**EMITTER VERIFICATION — four of four proven LIVE, `study_events.kind` the only valid observable.** The HTTP response can **NEVER** prove an emit: `recordStudyEvent` fails open and silently, so a broken RPC still returns a perfect 200 with a correct summary or answer. Only the row proves it — assert on `kind`, never on status, body, or row count (ingest emits `material_uploaded` on its own success path, so counting rows after an upload would falsely confirm). **`quiz_submitted`** (P5.2 preview): empty submission → **zero** rows; ≥1 in-range answer → **exactly one** row. **`summary_generated`** (preview `537c7bc`): `summary`/`summary_generated_at`/`summary_model` nulled on an existing `ready` document via the SQL editor — `documents` is client-writable under a single `for all` policy, so no app-side regenerate path was needed and register #26's **structural no-regenerate stands**; `POST /api/summarize` returned 200 `cached:false`, and **exactly one** row `kind='summary_generated'` appeared (the cached branch precedes the ready check, so this path is otherwise unreachable). **`question_asked`** (same preview): `POST /api/agent` returned 200 **with an `X-Conversation-Id` header** (the only reliable discriminator — the conversation-limit denial also returns 200 with a text stream and emits nothing), a 582-char body, and **exactly one** row `kind='question_asked'` — proving the emit inside the stream closure fires after the for-await drains despite sitting past the header flush. **`material_uploaded`** (production, 2026-07-11): the first real upload produced **exactly one** row `kind='material_uploaded'` at 10:57:30 with the document at `status='ready'` (`embedding_status='ready'`, `chunk_count=3`) — the fourth and last emit site, now proven; register **#38**'s coverage gap is closed and its risk (2) (unchecked ready-write) is fixed by PR **#41**, while its risk (1) (the agent-emit teardown loss) remains open. **Cost consumed:** two Haiku calls + one Voyage query embedding; one `summary` (of 5) and one `query` (of 30) free counter ticked. No money. <br><br>**⚠️ THE THREE INVARIANTS THIS PHASE RESTS ON ARE LIVE IN REGISTER #35 — read it before touching `study_events`.** |

| B1 | Pro-buys-nothing (limits ignored subscription) | Phase 0 | ✅ done (`e203fda`) |
| B2 | Dual entitlement source (`profiles.plan` vs `subscriptions`) | Phase 0 | ✅ done — `getEntitlement` is sole source (`5cd499e`, `07230ef`). ⚠️ `profiles.plan` **dead column still present** (see register #11) |
| B3 | Paddle webhook gaps (past_due/paused/resume) | Phase 0 | ⚠️ **DONE IN THE CODE, NOT IN THE LIVE CONFIGURATION — CORRECTED 2026-09-11. Superseded status, quoted so it is not re-derived: “✅ done (`dff93fc`)”.** `dff93fc` (2026-06-28) grew the handler set from three events to eight. The active Paddle destination, read in the LIVE dashboard on 2026-09-11, still subscribes to **exactly the pre-fix three** — `subscription.created`, `subscription.updated`, `subscription.canceled` — which is `PIVOT_PLAN.md:373`’s own wording of the defect B3 was opened for. The five handlers this row paid for are not deliverable at that destination, and **no row in this file has ever named the subscribed set**. Grounds, exposure ranking and the fix in register **#86**, which is graded 🟠 **LATENT, NOT LIVE**: the route uses ONE shared handler for all eight events, so an undelivered event costs nothing unless it carries a transition no subscribed event also carries — and on 2026-09-11 none of the five does. **The code fix `dff93fc` shipped is still not reflected in the live configuration, which is what this row records; what it does NOT record is a live defect.** |

| B5b | Deep upload content hardening (magic-byte, bomb limits, scanning) | **Phase 7** | ⬜ not started (gates Phase 8) |
| B6 | Synchronous ingestion (holds HTTP connection) | **Phase 7** | ⬜ not started (gates Phase 8) |
| B7 | No rate limiting (cost abuse) | Phase 0 | ✅ done (`9c3b7ba`) — ⚠️ **code shipped Phase 0 but was NOT actually live** until the live-DB reconciliation on 2026-07-05 (`usage_counters`/`increment_usage` had never been applied, so `enforceLimit` failed closed). **Now genuinely active** (real 429s at caps) — see register **#23** |

Every tracked-but-not-done item found by sweeping git history, `PIVOT_PLAN.md`,
project memory, and per-step review debates. "Where" is the target phase/trigger;
🔎 marks items surfaced by this sweep beyond the original hand-off list.

| 1 ✅ **CLOSED 2026-09-10. Every limit message a student can read is now localized, in both routes this row named and in the shared guard behind them.** | **Server-side limit-message localization** — `/api/ingest` (“…limit of N materials”) and `/api/agent` (429 body, “…monthly limit of N conversations”) returned **English only**; no locale threaded into the API. | Was: presentation/i18n work outside each step’s scope; needed locale plumbed into API routes. | **DONE via the pattern register #27 established**, not a new one: `locale` arrives in the request (JSON body for `/api/agent`, a multipart field for `/api/ingest`, which takes formData), is whitelisted server-side to `'ar'|'en'` and never interpolated, and every string moved into ONE module, `src/lib/limit-messages.ts`. **Scope was wider than this row stated, and the sweep is recorded so the next reader does not re-derive it:** `enforceLimit` is shared by **four** kinds (`query`, `upload`, `summary`, `quiz`), so one fix reached all four, plus its burst guard and its fail-closed 503. **Two of the four walls could not have been fixed any other way without a contract change** — `/api/agent`’s monthly-conversation denial returns **200 and STREAMS its text as an assistant message** (register #8), so no status exists for a client to branch on. **Already localized before this change, and left alone:** `/api/summarize` and `/api/quiz/generate` 429s, whose clients map the status to dictionary copy (`SummarySection.tsx:53`, `QuizSection.tsx:101`) and never read the server string. **A separate falsehood fixed in the same pass:** every one of these messages said “try again tomorrow”, which is FALSE east of UTC — `usage_counters.day` defaults to `current_date` on a UTC database, so a student at 02:00 in the Gulf is cleared at 04:00 the SAME local day. The two dictionary strings carrying the same falsehood (`en.ts` / `ar.ts` summary + quiz) were corrected too. **NO PRICE APPEARS ANYWHERE IN THIS MODULE** (undecided, register #80), and a Pro user at a ceiling is never shown an upgrade line. <br><br>**AMENDED BEFORE MERGE (Abo Jad, 2026-09-10) — three rulings, all now shipped in this row's change.** **(1) `00:00 UTC` REPLACED BY TIME REMAINING.** The first draft named the instant, which is true everywhere and *usable nowhere*: Morocco is UTC+1 most of the year and UTC+0 during Ramadan, so a student in Casablanca must know what UTC is, what their offset is, and that it MOVES, before the sentence tells them anything. Copy now states the interval — **“It resets in about 3 hours” / “يتجدد الحد بعد نحو 3 ساعات”** — true in every timezone with no location knowledge. Rounding is **always UP** (`Math.ceil`): rounding down would state a time at which the student is still refused. Granularity **cascades minutes → hours → days** so one formatter serves both the daily reset (<24h) and the monthly conversation reset (~31d); minutes floor at 1, so it never reads “0 minutes”. **The per-subject materials cap keeps NO time clause at all** — a subject's shelf never empties on a clock — and instead gains the only remedy that exists: *add it to another subject*. It does NOT say “delete one”, because **no delete path exists for a document or a subject** (register #80(c)). **Known and accepted:** the string does not tick after render; it sits in a transient error surface and “about” absorbs the drift. The strictly better answer — render the reset in the student's OWN local time, client-side — needs a machine-readable timestamp in the response, which **neither wall has a field for** (200 text stream / `text/plain`). That is **register #8's** contract change, and this row now names #8 as the blocker for the better copy. **(2) EVERY PRO DAILY *AND MONTHLY* FIGURE REMOVED FROM STUDENT-FACING COPY.** No daily Pro figure reaches a student; the monthly conversation ceiling was removed on the SAME reasoning even though the amendment named only the daily ones, because register #80 records *“the monthly cap and every Pro ceiling”* as unclosed, and leaving it would have kept publishing the very number (2,000) the amendment objected to, just through a different wall. A Pro user reads *“You've reached today's limit for questions”* plus when it returns — everything they can act on. **Free figures are still printed** (settled product, and the number an upgrade decision needs). **The per-subject materials cap prints in BOTH tiers** — 10 / 200, ruled publishable: it is a shelf size, not a rate. **(3) THE TWO CLIENT-RENDERED 429s NOW READ THE SERVER'S STRING.** `SummarySection` / `QuizSection` mapped 429 to a dictionary line that **cannot say when the limit returns** — the reset instant is server state. They now prefer `enforceLimit`'s own localized message via `readServerLimitMessage`, falling back to the dictionary line if the body is unreadable; the two dictionary strings are correspondingly **time-free**, since a client with no clock reference must not claim one. This retires the two-voices problem the first draft left behind. <br><br>**ALSO CORRECTED IN THE SAME PASS, both introduced by the first draft:** the Arabic name of the paid tier was **“خطة Pro”**, a fifth spelling in Latin script — the shipped dictionary says **الباقة الاحترافية / الاحترافي** in four places and now so does this module; and tanween fatha was dropped (“مجددا”) against a dictionary that writes **مجددًا six times and مجددا never** (39 tanween marks in `ar.ts`). **Arabic is MSA with Western numerals 0-9. The claim that no CLDR plural category is needed is WITHDRAWN:** stating an interval makes the count govern the noun, so durations go through `pluralize` (`Intl.PluralRules`). Verified by execution across every MSA boundary — **1 ساعة (numeral dropped), 2 ساعتين (dual, genitive after بعد — NOT `streakUnit`'s ساعتان), 3-10 أيام/ساعات (plural of paucity), 11-99 11 يومًا/ساعة (singular tamyīz)**. The forms are whole PHRASES, not bare nouns, because CLDR's Arabic patterns drop the numeral at `one`/`two` and only a phrase can express that; `plural.ts`'s JSDoc was corrected to say it returns the declared FORM, not necessarily a noun. **Storage order PROVEN, not assumed:** the codepoints of بلغت read `U+0628 U+0644 U+063A U+062A` in file order, with **zero** bidi controls and **zero** presentation forms in either Arabic file — the reversed appearance in an earlier report was terminal rendering. <br><br>**SECOND REVIEW (Abo Jad, 2026-09-10) — ONE HARMFUL LINE FOUND AND REMOVED, ONE ALLEGED DEFECT DISPROVED.** **(A) “You can add it to another subject” IS WITHDRAWN. It was the obvious remedy and it was ADVICE TO DAMAGE YOUR OWN COURSE.** **Ask cannot span subjects, established from source:** `match_chunks` filters `where c.kb_id = match_kb_id` (`supabase/migrations/20260501_rag_pgvector.sql`), `/api/agent` takes ONE `kb_id` and 400s without it, and the Ask page makes the student choose a single subject through `KBSelector`. So splitting one course across two subjects puts half of it **permanently beyond every question** — and it is **IRREVERSIBLE**, because there is no delete path AND no move path for a document or a subject (#80(c)). The copy now names **no action at all**: it states the constraint that makes the obvious workaround a trap, at the moment the student is about to take it. Free users still get the upgrade line, which is a real remedy; **a Pro user at 200 has none, and the copy does not invent one.** **(B) THE “LOCALE LOCK-IN” THIS ROW WAS ACCUSED OF CREATING DOES NOT EXIST, and the accusation came from a careless sentence in the review report, not from the code.** What was meant was an API CONTRACT constraint (`locale` is now a JSON field on `/api/agent` and a multipart field on `/api/ingest`, so a third route must follow that shape) — it was written in a list of DEFECTS using the word “creates”, which is not what it is. **Verified from source: this change introduces no locale defect and widens none.** All four surfaces derive the locale identically — `locales.includes(params.locale) ? params.locale : 'en'` from the SAME URL segment the page is rendered from (`ChatBox:37`, `DropZone:21` (it read `:19` until #189 added two imports above it, 2026-09-23), `SummarySection:21`, `QuizSection:75`) — and `summarize`/`quiz` derive `lang` the same way (`summarize:66`, `quiz/generate:178`), both documented as “follows the APP UI language, never the document's language”. A limit message therefore always matches the page it appears on. **The change can only REDUCE wrong-language walls:** before it, a student on `/ar` met an English wall 100% of the time. **TWO PRE-EXISTING, UNRECORDED i18n GAPS SURFACED WHILE PROVING THIS — for the record commit, NOT for this row:** (i) **there is no language switcher anywhere in `src/`** (no locale-flip control exists), so the locale is fixed by the middleware's `Accept-Language` read at first entry and changeable only by hand-editing the URL; and (ii) **`defaultLocale` contradicts itself** — `middleware.ts:5` says `'ar'`, `lib/i18n/index.ts:6` says `'en'`, and every client and server fallback uses `'en'`. Register #17 covers hardcoded English copy and neither of these. |
| 2 | **Pricing page hardcodes "5 Subjects" / "10 materials" / "10 Subjects"** (`i18n` `pricing.*.features` arrays) — a static marketing list that cannot interpolate the real `FREE_LIMITS`/`PRO_LIMITS`. | Marketing copy is a static array; not wired to the limit constants. | Manual sync — **bump by hand if the limits ever change**. Revisit when pricing is restyled (P2.6) / a pricing pass. **2026-09-11: one of these strings is not merely unsynced — it is FALSE, and the manual-sync instruction on this row is the thing that failed.** “10 Subjects” (`en.ts:77`) and “10 مواد” (`ar.ts:79`) sit against `PRO_LIMITS.knowledge_bases = 50` (`limits.ts:18`), and `dashboard.newKb.errorLimitPro` interpolates the real 50 — so one app promises a Pro student 10 and tells them 50. The defect itself is owned by **#81**, not here; this row keeps the mechanism (a static array that cannot interpolate a constant). **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY: it was not “one of these strings” — it was THREE, and this row’s own “bump by hand” instruction failed on every one.** Superseded count, quoted: *“one of these strings is not merely unsynced — it is FALSE.”* The block was re-read whole rather than at the line #81 named, and it held **three** live defects, in **three different shapes**: **(i)** Pro *“10 Subjects”* **overstated downward** against `PRO_LIMITS.knowledge_bases = 50`; **(ii)** Free *“10 materials”* **understated 5×** because the cap is **per subject** (`limits-server.ts:53`), which this row never noticed because it only ever compared the STRING to the CONSTANT and never asked what the constant was counted over; **(iii)** Pro *“Priority support”* was **not a number at all** — a feature with no implementation anywhere in `src/`, which a row about *number* sync was structurally incapable of catching. All three fixed 2026-09-11 in both locales; the reasoning and the superseded remedy are in **#81**. **THE MECHANISM CONCLUSION THIS ROW SHOULD CARRY FORWARD:** *“bump by hand if the limits ever change”* is **the wrong trigger**. Not one of the three was caused by a limit changing — (i) and (ii) were wrong when written, (iii) was never true — so an instruction that fires on limit changes would have fired **zero** times in three defects. **The only thing that found them was reading the block against the code, whole.** <br><br>**ALSO ON THIS PAGE AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE — AN OMISSION, NOT A CONTRADICTION.** The free card publishes *“100 conversations/month”* and says nothing about the **daily question cap of 10** (`rate-limit.ts:42`, cut 30→10 by PR #141). Those are **two different meters**, and the tighter one is the one a student meets first. **It is not published now, and the reason is NOT “the cap may become monthly so we would edit twice”** — this project has ruled twice (**#61**, **#74**) that a defect is not deferred for the convenience of a future edit. **The reason is that publishing “10 questions/day” beside “100 conversations/month” without explaining how the two meters relate would ADD confusion rather than remove it**, and choosing which unit a student is told about is a product decision inside **#80(a)**’s open monthly-cap work, not a line edit. **It is recorded so the next reader inherits the finding and not the silence.** |

Before #194 the same case spent one credit and saved a broken summary for good (#26), so for the student the change is strictly better; but a failed generation is no longer only an accident. **Measured so far, one sample and not a rate:** the one post-fix Arabic summary used 1,262 of 3,330 output tokens (38%) and ended `end_turn` (#114); the `[summary-incomplete]` log tag counts refusals. **Why a refund is not a patch (ruled 2026-09-24, before #194 merged):** `usage_counters` has no update policy and `increment_usage` is its only write path (`supabase/migrations/20260629_usage_counters.sql`), so a refund needs a new SECURITY DEFINER function applied to production; granted to `authenticated` the way `increment_usage` is, a decrement would let any student reset their own cap from the browser, so it must be server-only or tied to a one-time reservation; and charging only after success would reopen the concurrency race the atomic increment closes. It belongs in its own PR across all four routes, never inside one route's fix. Ties to register **#23** (avoid another security-definer + live-DB cycle). |

Do not add a KB-scope or synthesis path as a silent addition — either needs its **own** deliberate design. |
| 29 ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-09 — all three surfaces live-proven. REFRAMED before closing (pedagogical integrity, NOT security)** | **`correct_index` must never be exposed by the GENERATION route, nor by any quiz-item read that PRECEDES an attempt** — a quiz whose answers the student can see before attempting it has no value as retrieval practice. The **GRADING route deliberately DOES return `correct_index`** — but **ONLY for items the student actually ATTEMPTED**, from the first submission onward: that is feedback, and feedback is where the learning happens. **"Attempted" means an in-range choice** (a non-negative integer indexing inside *that item's* `options`), **not merely a non-null `selected_index`**. The looser test would reopen the hole it exists to close: an unconditional reveal let `{"answers": []}` return the whole key for **zero** attempts, and a non-null test would let `selected_index: 99` on every item do the same in **one** request. An unattempted item comes back `{ item_id, selected_index: null, is_correct: false }` with **no `correct_index`**. Revealing the key is **the reward for an attempt, not the gift of a request**. ⚠️ **THIS REGISTER PREVIOUSLY MISFRAMED ITSELF AS A SECURITY CONTROL. It is not, and the original reasoning was unsound.** (1) **RLS makes cross-user exposure impossible** — every quiz read is scoped three hops up to `knowledge_bases.user_id`, so the only person who can ever see a quiz's answers is the owner of the document it was generated from. There is no second party to defraud; a student who extracts the key is cheating **themselves at their own study material**. (2) **Withholding `correct_index` never actually protected the key.** The per-item `is_correct` flags are **themselves an oracle**: with `MAX_OPTIONS` = 5, submitting `selected_index: 0` for every item, then `1`, then `2`, then `3`, reveals the entire key in **at most four submissions** (anything unmatched is `4` by elimination). Hiding the column moved extraction from one request to four; it never prevented it. The old wording shipped **the worst of both** — no feedback for the honest student, a four-request key dump for the dishonest one. **The concern is PEDAGOGICAL INTEGRITY, not security.** | Not fixable in P4.0 (schema + types only) — the control lives in each route that serves quiz items. **Also note the compile-time backstop is weaker than once claimed:** `ClientQuizItem = Omit<QuizItem,'correct_index'>` protects only where a payload is **constructed** against that type; `x as ClientQuizItem[]` is an **assertion, not a check**, and compiles happily over a `select('*')` result because the Supabase row is untyped at the boundary. **The explicit select list is the ONLY load-bearing control.** | **Binding rule — every PRE-ATTEMPT client-facing quiz-item read MUST use an explicit select list that omits `correct_index`.** RLS covers ownership; the type is documentation, not enforcement. **The single permitted exception is the grading route's internal fetch**, whose key reaches the client only *through graded results, after an attempt*. Live-verification must inspect the **actual response bytes**, never merely assert the type exists — the "verify the live artifact, don't trust the written intent" discipline of register **#23**. <br><br>**✅ P4.1 (`POST /api/quiz/generate`) — PROVEN on Preview `af2ccbe`:** both item selects list columns explicitly (`id, quiz_id, question, options, position`) and **omit `correct_index`**; the column is written **once on insert** and appears in **no read select**. Live evidence: (1) **5 items**, `is_partial` **false**, `model` `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`; (2) a second identical call returned **`cached:true`** with **no Claude call and no counter charge**; (3) `correct_index` **confirmed ABSENT from the raw network payload** and **PRESENT + correct in the DB** (all five verified against their `options`; `options_count` **4** each, within the 2–5 `quiz_item_valid` CHECK); (4) an **English document produced Arabic questions under `locale:'ar'`** (registers **#27**, **#31**); (5) a **nonexistent `document_id` returned 404 before `enforceLimit`** — no charge, no existence leak. **This half stays closed: the generate route must never start returning the key.** <br><br>**✅ P4.2 (`POST /api/quiz/submit`) — PROVEN on Preview `9f26a53`:** `correct_index` is read by exactly **one** select (the internal grading fetch) and reaches an outbound object at exactly **one** guarded line, inside `if (attempted)`. Live evidence, by name: (1) an **empty `answers` array → a truthful `0/5` with ZERO `correct_index` fields** (the bypass that made an unconditional reveal untenable is closed); (2) **`selected_index: 99` → graded incorrect with NO `correct_index` revealed** — an out-of-range guess is **not** an attempt, which is why the predicate is **in-range**, not merely non-null; (3) **five in-range answers → `3/5`, `correct_index` revealed on all attempted items**; (4) **three `quiz_attempts` rows persisted** (`0/5`, `0/5`, `3/5`); (5) live DB confirmed — `quiz_attempts` columns, the **three CHECKs** (`score >= 0`, `score <= total`, `total > 0`), **FK cascade** to `quizzes`, and the **RLS policy with the three-hop ownership chain**; (6) `/api/quiz/generate` **re-audited byte-identical on the merged tree** (blob `e7248510`) — **the key never leaves the generate route.** <br><br>**✅ P4.3 (the UI) — PROVEN on Preview `b8a9198`, and closed STRUCTURALLY rather than by discipline.** `QuizSection` performs **ZERO client-side Supabase reads** against `quizzes` or `quiz_items` (`grep -rn "from('quiz" src/components src/app/[locale]` → **nothing**): there is **no client query of the answer table to get wrong**, so no `select('*')` can leak the key. The quiz arrives from `POST /api/quiz/generate`; the only correct answers the UI ever sees arrive **inside a grading response, for items the student attempted**. Live evidence: (1) the raw generate response body's **`.includes('correct_index')` returned `false`**; (2) five in-range answers → **`1/5`**, the correct answer revealed under **each wrong item**, **no redundant reveal** under the correct one; (3) **zero selections → every item "not answered", with NO correct answer revealed anywhere** — the empty-submission bypass, proven **end-to-end through the UI**. <br><br>**🔒 CLOSED — but the invariant it encodes is PERMANENT. Do not delete these two rules when tidying:** **(1) The reveal predicate is IN-RANGE, never non-null.** `readSelectedIndex` accepts **any** non-negative integer, so `selected_index: 99` is non-null while selecting nothing; a non-null test would let a client send `99` for every item and harvest the whole key in **one request having attempted nothing** — the same bypass `{"answers": []}` used to be. The client mirrors this in **`answeredIndex()`**, which returns `null` out-of-range so `options[]` is never indexed with a raw echoed value. **(2) `correct_index` is presence-tested, never truthiness-tested.** It is **absent** (not `null`) on unattempted items, and **`0` is a valid, common answer index** — `if (r.correct_index)` would render a correct answer of option 0 as "not revealed", forever. **`revealedIndex()` uses `'correct_index' in r`.** **Any future quiz-item read, in any phase, must still omit `correct_index` from pre-attempt payloads.** Companions that remain OPEN: register **#32** (submission throttle, deferred) and register **#33** (`quiz_attempts` is a write-only, best-effort log). |
| 30 🔧 **DEFERRED (own step)** | **Quiz creation is two non-atomic inserts, not a real transaction.** `POST /api/quiz/generate` (P4.1) persists a quiz as **two separate** supabase-js calls — insert the `quizzes` row, then insert its `quiz_items` — because supabase-js has no client-side transaction across statements and P4.1 was route-only (no migration). **Mitigation in place:** if the `quiz_items` insert fails, the route issues a **compensating `delete`** of the just-inserted `quizzes` row, so a failure does not strand an **orphan empty quiz** (which, under `unique(document_id)` from register #28, the cache check would then return forever with no retry possible). **Residual gap:** if the process dies **between** the two inserts (after the quiz row commits, before the compensating delete runs), an orphan empty quiz is still possible. | Route-only step couldn't add DB objects; the compensating delete closes the common failure path but not a mid-flight crash. **Accepted for P4.1** — the window is tiny and generation failures are rare. | **Proper fix as its own step:** a **transactional `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC** (mirroring the `increment_usage` pattern — pinned `search_path`, `auth.uid()`-scoped, RLS-safe) that inserts the quiz **and** all its items in **one transaction**, so quiz creation is atomic and the orphan class is eliminated entirely. A student deleting a subject would **silently rewrite their own study history** — days they really studied vanish, the number drops, nothing recovers it. **The two lifecycles are opposites; no application-layer care fixes a table whose rows disappear when unrelated content does.** ⚠️ **This register's own founding presumption was WRONG and is corrected here:** it read *"a record for a history/progress feature that does not yet exist (**presumably** Phase 5's streak)"*. It names `quiz_attempts` **zero** times. The presumption, not the plan, was the error. **Its columns were NOT relocated into `study_events`:** `score`/`total` have no reader either, and smuggling quiz-specific columns into a generic activity log would make it a quiz table with a general name. If per-quiz score history is ever wanted it returns as its **own** table **with a named reader** — the discipline this register existed to enforce. **The original caveats (recorded for the archive):** **(a)** the table was **WRITE-ONLY and speculative** — nothing read it; **(b)** the insert **failed OPEN**, so it was a **best-effort log with missing rows**, never a complete one. **(a)** Nothing in the codebase **reads** this table. `POST /api/quiz/submit` inserts one row per graded submission and never queries it; feedback is unconditional from the first attempt, so no code path consults it to decide anything. Its original rationale — *"to know whether feedback has been given"* — is **vestigial**: that decision is not data-driven. The table is a **record for a history/progress feature that does not yet exist** (presumably Phase 5's streak). **A write-only table with no reader is exactly the class of object that silently drifts** — the same lesson as register **#11** (`profiles.plan`: defaulted, written, never read for entitlement, still sitting in the schema as a dead column years later). **(b)** The attempt insert **deliberately fails OPEN** (a failed bookkeeping write logs and still returns the already-computed grade — the student must not lose a correct submission to a broken INSERT). | 34 🔒 **DECIDED (design) 2026-07-09 — `usage_counters` is NOT a streak substrate. A student who hits their cap and receives **five 429s has incremented five times.** A streak read from this table would count **a day on which the app refused to do anything** as a day of study. **(c) ITS LIFECYCLE IS OWNED BY SOMEONE ELSE — fatal.** `usage_counters` is the **B7 cost ledger**. `PIVOT_PLAN.md` §7 row **7** schedules *"per-user rate-limit tuning"*. A perfectly reasonable action there — pruning rows older than N days, resetting counters, re-keying the table — would **silently delete a user-visible streak**. | Not a bug and not a deferral: a **decision recorded before the mistake is made**, because the wrong answer here is the cheap-looking one and the damage (a silently-wrong number the student reads about their own history) is invisible until a user complains. Same family as register **#33**'s cascade disqualification: **coupling an immutable claim about the past to a table whose lifecycle belongs to a different concern.** | **RULE: a streak's substrate must be OWNED BY THE STREAK.** `study_events` (PIVOT_PLAN.md §7 row 5 / line 65 / line 105; register **#14**) exists for this and must key **only to `auth.users`**, never to content and never to the limiter. **Do not reuse `usage_counters`. Bucketing the day at write time makes the bug permanent and unfixable without a backfill; compute the day in the **student's** timezone at read time. |

This is the positive form of the argument that killed `quiz_attempts` (#33): it cascaded `quiz_attempts → quizzes → documents`, so deleting one material erased attempts beneath it. Each one is a **structural** property: (1)+(2) are about **lifecycle ownership** — a table whose rows vanish when unrelated content does can never hold history, no matter how careful the application layer is; (3) is about **information destruction** — a bucketed day cannot be un-bucketed. See register **#36** for the grant that must be revoked if a streak ever earns anything. |
| 36 🔧 **DEFERRED — trigger-based, NOT a phase. Revisit IF a streak ever grants value.** | **`record_study_event(text)` is granted to `authenticated`, so it is callable directly from the browser** — exactly as `increment_usage(text)` is. The RPC prevents the two attacks that matter: **`user_id` cannot be spoofed** (`auth.uid()` resolves server-side) and **`occurred_at` cannot be backdated** (stamped `now()`), so a caller cannot fabricate a year-long streak in one request. What it does **not** prevent is a student calling it to assert *"I studied, now"* on a day they did not study. | **Acceptable TODAY, deliberately.** A streak **grants nothing** — it is a self-scoped vanity/retention number — so forging today's event is **self-cheating**, the same reasoning that reframed register **#29** from a security concern to a pedagogical one. Requiring a service-role write path now would mean a second Supabase client and a new server-only surface for **zero** current benefit. A direct-insert RLS policy was **rejected outright** (not merely declined): `with check (user_id = auth.uid())` constrains **which rows** you may write, **not what values** you may put in them, so it would permit **backdating 365 rows in a single request**. | **TRIGGER: the moment a streak EARNS anything.** The concrete case is **Phase 9 (AdMob)** — if a streak ever buys an ad-free day, a badge with value, a discount, or any entitlement, then forging today's event becomes a **revenue exploit** rather than self-cheating, and the browser can do it with the anon key without touching a route. **At that point:** `revoke execute on function record_study_event(text) from authenticated;` and call it **only** from a server route holding the **service-role** key (the pattern already used by `/api/paddle/webhook`). **Do not wait for the exploit to be observed** — the check is "does the streak grant anything?", and it is a one-line grep at Phase 9's design review. Same discipline as register **#29**'s in-range predicate: decide it while it is free. **EXTENDED 2026-07-10 (P5.3) — the READ side is client-asserted too, and it trips the SAME trigger.** `current_streak(p_time_zone text)` takes the student's **IANA zone from the browser** on every call. A hostile caller can therefore **re-bucket their EXISTING history** by claiming a different zone, moving day boundaries by **at most ~26h end to end**. Bounded, and the bound is tight: it can **never fabricate a day on which nothing happened** — two events more than **48h** apart land in non-adjacent days under **every zone on Earth** — so it can only rescue a streak whose events already **straddle a boundary**. This is a **genuinely new capability** beyond "assert *I studied, now*" (it exploits **past** events with **no new calls**), and it is still **acceptable today for the same reason**: a streak grants nothing, so it is **self-cheating**. A `profiles.timezone` column would **NOT** fix it — `001_initial_schema.sql:11` is a single **`for all`** policy and the browser already writes `profiles` at signup, so the column is **equally client-writable** (the same trap `profiles.plan` sets, and which `getEntitlement` defuses by **never reading it**). **AT THE SAME PHASE-9 TRIGGER**, when a streak earns an ad-free day: the zone must **stop being client-asserted** — pin it server-side per account behind a **write-protected** column (which means first replacing that `for all` policy) or accept the exploit **knowingly and in writing**. **One trigger, one entry, two revocations: the RPC grant AND the client-supplied zone.** |

| 43 ✅ **CLOSED 2026-07-21 (PR #63, merge `3378dd8`) — the gate's LAST unpinned dependency, and it redded `main` twice with no commit to the repo.** | **`supabase gen types` does not talk to Postgres itself — it shells out to `docker run public.ecr.aws/supabase/postgres-meta:v0.96.6`.** That reference is **compiled into the CLI binary** (verified: `grep -a` on the pinned 2.109.1 binary yields `postgres-meta:v0.96.6`), and it is a **MUTABLE TAG on a rate-limited anonymous registry** — the one dependency of `db-types` that sat **outside** `gen-db-types.sh`'s digest-pinning discipline, in a script whose own premise is that an unpinned toolchain *"turns any upstream release into a spontaneous red main, with no commit to this repo."* It fired on 2026-07-21: `main` at `d8c8fde` went red **twice** (runs `29835675126`, then again after a 17-minute cooldown) with `docker: toomanyrequests: Rate exceeded` / `error running container: exit 125`, while `tsc` stayed green and the merged change was provably innocent (the verify step passed, all 13 migrations applied, the failure was 26s later inside the CLI). **This was a PINNING hole, not merely a reliability one:** `db-types` is a `git diff --exit-code` gate, so a republished `v0.96.6` with a changed emitter is indistinguishable from schema drift. | Not deferred — surfaced and closed the same day, because a required check that reds on a third party's traffic trains people to re-run it (the exact anti-pattern `gen-db-types.sh` already argues against for its readiness probe: *"A gate that reds main on a coin flip is worse than no gate"*). The alternative of authenticating ECR or mirroring to GHCR was **rejected**: both keep a third-party registry in the hot path, and both would introduce a credential into a job whose stated property is that it *"never needs a Supabase token … cannot reach, read, or mutate production."* | **FIXED in `scripts/gen-db-types.sh`: `META_IMAGE` pinned by multi-arch INDEX digest `sha256:a84cc713…`, pre-pulled from Docker Hub, then `docker tag`-ed to the exact ECR reference before the CLI runs.** The digest is **byte-identical at Docker Hub and public.ecr.aws** (verified against both), so this is the SAME image over a registry that works — content addressing means docker verifies the bytes or fails the pull. **The retag is REQUIRED, not defensive:** a digest-only local copy under `docker.io/...` satisfies neither the registry, the repo path, nor the tag the CLI asks for, and `docker run`'s pull-if-missing would go straight back to ECR. **Deliberately in the SCRIPT, NOT `db-types.yml`** — per that workflow's own principle at `:57-60` (*"the generator is a script so that CI and a developer's machine run the exact same code path … a second place that only CI exercises … is where drift hides"*); relocating this into the workflow would silently un-fix local runs. Proven on the merged commit `3378dd8`: `toomanyrequests` count **0**, `Pulling from supabase/postgres-meta` count **0**, and `Unable to find image` fired **only** for `PG_IMAGE` — never for the ECR reference, i.e. the CLI never contacted ECR. **RESIDUAL, carried here rather than hidden behind the ✅ (the #38 precedent):** **(1)** Docker Hub is **also** anonymous-rate-limited per IP and GitHub runners share NAT pools — this job now makes **two** Hub pulls per run, so the same failure shape can return under a different registry name; today's evidence is that Hub works where ECR did not, which is not immunity. **(2)** The `v0.96.6` tag is **coupled to `SUPABASE_CLI_VERSION`**: bumping the CLI may change the compiled-in tag, leaving `META_TAG` naming an image the new CLI never asks for — the retag goes unused and the CLI **silently falls back to an ECR pull**. That degrades to flakiness, **never to a wrong result**, but it is invisible until the throttle hits, so **a CLI bump requires re-deriving the tag from the new binary** and updating both values. Related: **#44** (same class, still open). |

It is a third-party fetch in the **hot path of a required check** — structurally the same shape as **#43**, which was just closed for the image side of the same problem: `db-types` pins Postgres by digest and now pins `postgres-meta` by digest, but the **generator binary itself** arrives over an unpinned network fetch. The version string is pinned (`2.109.1`), so this is weaker than #43's mutable-tag hole — npm versions are immutable once published — but the FETCH is still unpinned by integrity hash, and an npm outage or throttle reds `main` with no commit to the repo, exactly as ECR did. | **Not scoped, not scheduled, no phase assigned** — recorded the moment it was noticed so the class is not silently considered "handled" because #43 closed. Run against production 2026-09-04, **15 checks, every `ok` true**: `0_preflight_empty count=0` · `1a_returns_void undefined` · `1b_no_failure_log none` · `1c_row_written 1` · `1d_row_shape user_id=000…000 email=null stage=synthetic-verification resolved_at=null` · `2_anon_boolean_true true` · `3_anon_reads_nothing 0 rows — "permission denied for table account_deletion_orphans"` · `4_resolved 1` · `5_anon_boolean_false false` · `6_deleted 1` · `7a_returned_error undefined + failure line — "Invalid API key"` · `7b_thrown_rejection undefined + failure line — "TypeError: fetch failed"` · `7c_timeout_bounded undefined + failure line, elapsed=5002ms — "TimeoutError: The operation was aborted due to timeout"` · `8a_table_empty count=0` · `8b_signal_clear false`. **Four things in that run are worth more than the pass.** (i) Step 3 ran **while the row existed** — against an empty table it is vacuously true and proves nothing; and what anon actually hit was `permission denied`, i.e. the **revoke** stopped it before RLS was even reached, so the two independent mechanisms are confirmed to be two. (ii) Step 7 exercised all three failure shapes, and 7c bounded at **5002ms** against a local server that accepts and never answers — the timeout is proven by a stopwatch, not by reading the constant. (iii) The run ends by **measuring** the table empty (`count(*) = 0` plus the anon boolean false), never by asserting a delete succeeded, because **an alarm trained to be ignored before it has ever been right is worse than no alarm, and it would be self-inflicted by the test of the thing.** (iv) Step 0 is a **refusal gate**: a non-empty table on entry aborts with exit 2 and writes, resolves and deletes nothing, so a real orphan can never be cleaned up by this harness. **Honest annotation, not corrected by re-running:** the harness captures `console.error` to assert the failure lines, so the first captured entry in its output is Node’s own `MODULE_TYPELESS_PACKAGE_JSON` warning rather than output from the function; the assertions match on the `[account-deletion-orphan-record-failed]` prefix and were unaffected. The harness is committed **byte-identical to the bytes that produced this output** — filtering that line would have meant stamping a blob that never ran. `tsconfig.json` gains `allowImportingTsExtensions` (legal only with `noEmit`, already set) so the harness stays inside the `typecheck` gate; the alternative was moving it out of `**/*.ts`, leaving the thing that verifies the recorder less checked than the recorder. **That flag is commit `070ef52`, split out and placed AHEAD of the recorder** — build-affecting configuration does not move as a side effect of feature work, and no commit on this branch may carry the `.ts` import without the flag that permits it. Store commit: `e6372ef`. **THE CALL SITES ARE WIRED 2026-09-04, AND THE GAP IS UNCHANGED BY IT.** `orchestrate.ts` (blob `352d9cd347bbb3e2d0d457c9afd78ff2e3466ddb`) now does three things in each orphan arm, in this order: the `[account-deletion-orphan]` line fires **first and unconditionally**, so the durable write can never regress what already existed; then an **awaited** `recordDeletionOrphan`; then the `stage: 'orphaned'` return that becomes the 409. **The await rests on a logged outcome in this project, not on a hypothesis:** a floating promise is dropped when a serverless invocation is frozen or torn down, and P5.2 already records that once as an agent emit lost on teardown. The five-second bound in `orphan-record.ts` is what makes awaiting affordable on a path that is already returning a 409 the user must read carefully. **THE TWO LINES ARE ENFORCED, NOT MERELY REVIEWED.** `scripts/verify-orphan-call-sites.py` (blob `956e59dc9efe69f116c9f5d3dd00975f793381de`) is fail-closed over ten invariants: the import exists; there are **exactly two** `stage: 'orphaned'` returns; each is preceded by the console line and then by an awaited `recordDeletionOrphan(admin, …)`, in that order; the logged `stage=` and the recorded `stage:` agree at both arms; and the recorder still returns `Promise<void>`, still carries `AbortSignal.timeout(...)`, is still wrapped in `catch`, and still contains no `throw`. It is wired into `.github/workflows/typecheck.yml` (blob `35967078784891735627ef65f3aa9912fe4c4d67`) rather than into a new workflow, because **#65** is that an absent run is as silent as a failed one — a new workflow is a new thing that can be absent, while a step inside an already-required check cannot go missing without the check going missing — and the workflow keeps its name because renaming it would break the required status check branch protection is configured against. **THE VERIFIER WAS PROVEN TO REJECT, NOT ONLY TO PASS.** A fail-closed check never observed to fail is a check nobody has tested — the same defect shape as an anon-read step run against an empty table. Eight mutations were applied to a scratch **copy** (the repository untouched) and **every one was rejected while the unmutated control passed**: dropping the `await`; deleting a recorder call; recording a stage that disagrees with the logged one; adding a third orphan arm; dropping the import; removing the timeout; adding a `throw` to the recorder; changing the return type to `boolean`. On the real tree: **10 invariants hold, exit 0**. **BOTH OF THE FOLLOWING STAND, AND NEITHER MAY BE USED TO SOFTEN THE OTHER.** The static check **does not shrink the gap** — the gap is that the arms never execute under verification, and a static check executes nothing either. What it removes is **silent drift and omission**, which the gap wording never covered and which is the likelier of the two to bite: the realistic failure is not that today’s two lines are wrong, it is a future third arm added by someone who does not remember any of this. **THE GAP THAT DOES NOT CLOSE — the standing statement for this branch, verbatim and never to be softened. The two call sites it names were wired on 2026-09-04 and the sentence below is unchanged, exactly as promised when it was first recorded:** “recordDeletionOrphan is proven against the live table. That orchestrate.ts:130 and orchestrate.ts:155 actually call it is verified by inspection only — reaching either arm requires fault injection, refused at the start of the deletion arc and again at its end. This gap does not shrink with further testing and must not be described as closed.” **THE SIGNAL LANDS 2026-09-04. ALL THREE PARTS NOW EXIST AND THE ROW STILL DOES NOT CLOSE.** `.github/workflows/deletion-orphan-watch.yml` (blob `34443a58ee4c3d3e977c167bfdd6aca3690f7dee`) runs at `17 */6 * * *` and on demand, calling `scripts/orphan-watch.ts` (blob `3007c2db4fd2714e02f89a27509de23c4d149ad3`). **THREE OUTCOMES, NOT TWO:** `present` opens or keeps the orphan issue, `clear` closes it, and **anything else opens a DISTINCT "cannot read production" issue**. The obvious two-outcome watcher (`if bit === true`) reports NOTHING when the project is paused, when PostgREST errors, when the key is stale, or when the network fails — which is exactly what it reports when there are no orphans. Silence would mean two opposite things: **this row’s founding sentence, rebuilt inside the last component of its own fix.** Not hypothetical — `6513cb2` records this Supabase project auto-pausing **undetected**, so the single condition most likely to silence the watcher has already happened here once. State lives in **GitHub Issues, not in run history**, because an operator reading issues sees an artifact while an operator reading run history sees an absence — and an absence is also what a broken watcher produces. **PROVEN AGAINST PRODUCTION ON ALL THREE BRANCHES** by `scripts/verify-orphan-watch.ts` (blob `3041355510e17f8340d6f08fca5ced76a68df5d8`), which imports the shipped classifier **and** writes its sentinel through the real `recordDeletionOrphan` rather than a hand-written `INSERT`: **14 checks, every `ok` true** — `0_preflight_empty count=0` · `1_clear state=clear action=close-orphan` · `2_present state=present action=open-orphan` · `3a_sentinel_found 1` · `3b_resolved_clears state=clear` · `3c_deleted 1` · `4_clear_again state=clear` · `5_unreachable state=unreadable — "request failed: fetch failed"` · `6_bad_key state=unreadable — "http 401: Invalid API key"` · `7_ok_but_unrecognised state=unreadable — "http 200 with an unrecognised body"` · `8_wedged_timeout state=unreadable, elapsed=15010ms` · `9_unconfigured state=unreadable — "missing url or key"` · `10a_table_empty count=0` · `10b_signal_clear state=clear`. Step 7 is the branch a two-outcome watcher gets wrong: **HTTP 200 carrying HTML must read as unreadable, never as clear.** **THE CREDENTIAL IS INLINE, AND IT IS NOT A SECRET.** It is the anon key, which already ships in the public site’s browser bundle, and it buys one boolean — the table itself is unreadable to anon (RLS with no policies plus a revoked grant, both machine-checked). The service-role key was rejected: it bypasses RLS on all twelve tables and spending that to learn one bit is a grotesque trade. An Actions secret was rejected too: it moves a non-secret into exactly the unreviewable, undiffable dashboard configuration **this row already condemns for Railway**. The repo’s own tripwire blocks a JWT in the tree, correctly and by design — an anon key and a service-role key are indistinguishable by shape — so commit `dca7b6c` pins **one** literal by (path, sha256), proven by six mutations against a scratch copy in which altering the key by one character, moving it to another file, substituting a different JWT, and adding a second JWT beside the pinned one were **all still rejected**. **That pin has a stated deletion condition, not an aspiration:** it goes when the watcher migrates to a Supabase publishable key (`sb_publishable_...`), which is not a JWT and matches nothing. **FOUR TIMES IN THIS ARC THE FIX HAS BEEN AT RISK OF REBUILDING THE DEFECT IT REPAIRS, AND EVERY ONE WAS CAUGHT BY RUNNING OR READING THE THING RATHER THAN BY TRUSTING IT:** (i) a recorder that could throw or hang past the irreversible boundary, converting a recorded incident into a silent one; (ii) proving the STORE and calling it proof of the WRITER, which would have shipped the deliverable unexecuted while this row read green; (iii) the two-outcome watcher above; and (iv) — caught by re-reading the YAML — issue lookup by `gh issue list --search "<title> in:title"`, GitHub’s **fuzzy** text search, where both titles begin "Account-deletion orphan", so a `clear` run could have closed the **"cannot read production"** issue while reporting that it closed the orphan one: **silently resolving the alarm that says the signal is unknown.** Now two distinct labels (`orphan-watch:unresolved`, `orphan-watch:unreadable`) with every lookup keyed by label, because a label is an exact key and a search phrase is not. Two further defects were caught by RUNNING the proof: the CLI executed on import (`endsWith('orphan-watch.ts')` is also true for `verify-orphan-watch.ts`), which in CI would have written a spurious `state=unreadable` into `GITHUB_OUTPUT`; and `reason`, built from an HTTP response body, could carry a newline into that same line-oriented file and **forge additional workflow outputs** — now collapsed to one line at construction. **WHAT IS NOT PROVEN, AND MUST NOT LATER BE DESCRIBED AS VERIFIED:** the `gh issue create` and `gh issue close` calls. The proof covers the read and the classification — where the "silence means two things" defect lives — and stops at the API call. That is YAML plus one `gh` call per branch, verified by inspection only. **The watcher has also never run on `main`**, and **#65** is precisely that a green PR does not prove a run happened. **ACCEPTED RISK, NAMED WITH ITS TRIGGER, AND EXPLICITLY NOT CLOSED: GitHub disables scheduled workflows in a public repository after 60 DAYS WITHOUT COMMIT ACTIVITY.** If that fires, this watcher stops and its silence looks exactly like health — **the failure mode this register was opened for, occurring in the component built to close it**. Every fix inside the file is a monitor for the monitor, so it is recorded here rather than engineered away, in the same treatment as the storage sweep and the orphan state itself. **THIS RESIDUAL IS NOT CLOSED**, and register #54 does not close while it stands. **THE WATCHER IS EXECUTED ON `main` 2026-09-04, AFTER THE SQUASH-MERGE `5674403` — FOUR `workflow_dispatch` RUNS, ALL GREEN, DRIVEN BY HAND BEFORE THE FIRST CRON FIRE SO THAT THE FIRST EVER EXECUTION OF THE UNPROVEN `gh` PATH WAS WATCHED RATHER THAN UNATTENDED** (**#65**: a failed scheduled run and an absent one are indistinguishable). `33920498611` — empty table → `state=clear action=close-orphan`; `gh label create --force` created **`orphan-watch:unresolved`** and **`orphan-watch:unreadable`**, so the **colon-bearing label names — legal but unexercised, and named in advance as a thing that could not be vouched for — are now proven** through label creation and both `gh issue list --label` lookups. `33920636094` — a SECOND `clear` run, **dispatched in error**: the sentinel write that should have preceded it had failed (`ERR_UNSUPPORTED_NODE_MODULES_TYPE_STRIPPING` — Node refuses type-stripping under `node_modules`, where the temporary driver had been placed) and the commands were newline-chained instead of `&&`, so the dispatch fired anyway against an empty table. **Recorded rather than omitted**, and it bought one real datapoint: `gh label create --force` is idempotent and silent against labels that already exist. `33920731843` — sentinel written by the **real `recordDeletionOrphan`** (count 0→1, anon boolean false→true) → `state=present action=open-orphan` → **`gh issue create` EXECUTED**: issue **#98** opened, correctly labelled, with the operator runbook rendered intact including *"SET email TO NULL IN THE SAME STATEMENT"*. `33920785169` — sentinel resolved and deleted → `state=clear` → **`gh issue close` EXECUTED**: #98 closed at 21:22:39Z with a comment naming the run. **THE CLAIM, AND NOT ONE WORD MORE:** `gh issue create` and `gh issue close` are now **executed on TWO of the THREE branches**. The `unreadable` branch’s **API path is proven by SHARED CODE** — it calls the same `open_if_absent` that opened #98 — while **its TRIGGER remains INSPECTION-ONLY**: no run has reached `state=unreadable` against production, because reaching it means breaking the read on purpose from `main`. Equally unexercised: the `set -euo pipefail` hazard, where any `gh` surprise kills the step and the run reads as broken rather than as a finding — every step exited 0, so that path is **not disproven, merely untriggered**. **PRODUCTION WAS LEFT MEASURABLY EMPTY**, not presumed empty: `count(*) = 0` over the whole table **and** the anon boolean `false`, read after the last run, so the first scheduled fire (`17 */6 * * *`) cannot find a sentinel and cannot train anyone to ignore the alarm. **REGISTER #54 DOES NOT CLOSE, AND NOTHING IN THIS EXERCISE TOUCHES THE 60-DAY RESIDUAL** — GitHub still disables scheduled workflows in a public repository after 60 days without commit activity, and that failure still looks exactly like health. **THE FIRST SCHEDULED WINDOW PRODUCED NO RUN, REPORTED IN THE PRE-REGISTERED TERMS AND NO STRONGER: "no schedule-triggered run within 55 minutes of the first eligible fire (00:17Z, 2026-09-05)".** Four runs existed at that point, all `workflow_dispatch`, zero `schedule`. **THIS IS NOT A CLAIM THAT THE CRON IS BROKEN** — GitHub’s scheduled delivery is best-effort and routinely delayed, and the mechanisms that would make it dead are excluded by measurement: the workflow reports `state: active`, was created at 21:12:29Z on the default branch (`5674403`), and the 60-day inactivity disablement cannot apply to a repository committed to five times that day. **One missed window is evidence, not proof; two consecutive misses is a different claim from one.** **THE INTERPRETATION WAS FIXED BEFORE THE DATA, AND THE PROVENANCE IS A COMMIT, NOT A MERGE:** `b1ce0bf` on the retained branch `docs/54-prereg-schedule`, authored and pushed **2026-09-04T23:34:20Z** — 43 minutes before the window and before any observation. Its PR was **closed unmerged**: the merge authorization arrived at 01:12Z and a squash would have dated the claim AFTER the fire it says it precedes. **The finding from that, which generalises: a time-boxed record must not depend on a round-trip.** It survived because it was committed and pushed, and failed only as a pull request. **LIVENESS AND `force_state` LAND AS ONE EDIT TO THE ALARM PATH** (workflow blob `828af9ab209115c6e1f64ec581600e3d84437320`), **and the gate that would have delayed them until the schedule was confirmed was on the wrong thing** — neither depends on the cron firing, and the trigger is precisely what liveness exists to observe. **WHAT LIVENESS BUYS, AND NOTHING MORE: IT CANNOT MAKE ANYONE LOOK.** It does not close this row and **it does not close the 60-day residual, which stays open and stays named**. What changes is the shape of the failure: **an absence with no evidence either way becomes a dated artifact that visibly goes stale.** The need is measured, not argued — the 00:17Z miss was discovered only because a poller was stood up by hand; nothing in the system reported it. Liveness runs **after** the `case`, deliberately: under `set -e`, bookkeeping placed first could abort the step and **suppress the orphan alarm**, and a secondary signal must never be able to silence a primary one. `force_state` is dispatch-only by construction (`inputs` does not exist for a `schedule` event), a fixed `choice` compared against a literal — no caller string reaches a shell or a request — selecting between two hardcoded URLs. **PROVEN BY DISPATCHING THE BRANCH’S OWN VERSION BEFORE IT REACHED `main`, THREE RUNS, ALL GREEN:** `33936140456` (`force_state=off`) → `state=clear`, liveness issue **#103 created**; `33936181550` (`force_state=unreadable`) → `state=unreadable action=open-unreadable`, issue **#104 opened**, liveness **edited** on #103, and **the orphan issue correctly left alone** — an unreadable signal must never be reported as clear; `33936216675` (`force_state=off`) → `state=clear`, **#104 closed** with a comment, liveness edited again. **ALL THREE BRANCHES OF THE WATCHER HAVE NOW EXECUTED**, and `unreadable` reached its `gh issue create` **through its own trigger** rather than by shared code. Production was never touched by the forced runs — the read went to `127.0.0.1:1` — and was measured empty afterwards: `count(*) = 0` with the anon boolean `false`. **REGISTER #54 DOES NOT CLOSE. The 60-day residual stands, and no schedule-triggered run has been observed yet.** **THE FIRST WINDOW IS OBSERVED AND THE CLAIM OF RECORD IS THE PRE-REGISTERED ONE: "no schedule-triggered run within the observed window", 22:45:40Z to 02:46:25Z, covering the 00:17Z fire with two and a half hours of slack.** Seven runs of the watcher exist; all seven are `workflow_dispatch`; **zero are `schedule`**. **A CORRECTION TO OUR OWN INSTRUMENT, RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED:** the polling script printed *"This is the finding, not an inconclusive result: the cron did not fire."* That line was written before the interpretation was pre-registered and is **looser than what was committed to** — the pre-registration says "no schedule-triggered run within N hours of the first eligible fire" and says explicitly that it is **not** a claim that the cron is broken. **The register’s wording is the claim of record; the script’s is not, and the looser line survives nowhere.** **THE WIDER EXPLANATION IS EXCLUDED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY ARGUMENT.** `production-monitor.yml` is also schedule-triggered and fired at **00:18:35Z — one minute after the missed window** — so scheduled delivery reaches this repository. The same control measures the delivery itself: **100 scheduled runs between 2026-08-24T11:36Z and 2026-09-05T00:18Z, 276.7 hours — one run per 2.77 hours against a requested one per 0.5, roughly four invocations in five dropped**; the twelve most recent gaps were 120, 148, 168, 213, 266, 282, 261, 124, 147, 185, 280 and 314 minutes against a requested 30. Against that base rate **one miss out of one opportunity is unremarkable**, and it remains evidence rather than proof. **THE CRON IS THEREFORE CHANGED TO `*/30 * * * *`** (workflow blob `ac9336190ded0ba0a14fd5b1e4022e3c86fde172`), copied from `production-monitor.yml` because that exact value has 100 measured runs behind it here — **not because it is hoped to be better**. `17 */6` was **mis-specified, not broken**: four fixed opportunities a day in an environment dropping ~82% of them yields under one run per day, for a watcher meant to notice an orphan within hours. The earlier refusal to touch the cron — *"editing a schedule you have not diagnosed is guessing dressed as a fix"* — was correct **while it was undiagnosed**. **THE STANDARD DID NOT CHANGE; THE EVIDENCE DID, and the diagnosis came from a workflow this change never touched.** **THE PRE-REGISTERED SECOND OBSERVATION IS SUPERSEDED BY THIS FIX RATHER THAN ANSWERED BY IT, AND IS RECORDED AS SUPERSEDED RATHER THAN LEFT DANGLING.** The pre-registration’s next act on a null result was to observe the 06:17Z window; moving to `*/30` **removes that window** — there is no 06:17 fire left to observe, only the next of many half-hourly attempts. **What replaces it is stronger and outlives the session that built it:** at the measured base rate `*/30` should produce a run every two to five hours, so **the liveness issue’s own timestamp is the test** — if **#103** advances within a few hours of this merge, scheduled delivery reaches **this workflow specifically**, which the control can never establish. A poller dies with a session; the artifact does not. **No first successful `*/30` run closes anything.** **AND A DEFECT IN WHAT MERGED FOUR HOURS EARLIER (`7717b3b`), CAUGHT BY MEASURING THE CONTROL RATHER THAN BY REVIEWING THE DESIGN:** the liveness issue shipped saying *"Last run" should never be more than about six hours old* — a threshold taken from what the cron REQUESTS. At the measured delivery rate a 6-hourly cron can go quiet for a day while working perfectly, so **the artifact built to stop an alarm being trained-to-ignore would have cried wolf on its first quiet day**: the exact failure it exists to prevent, self-inflicted. It now states what the environment delivers — a few hours expected, several hours not a fault, more than about **12 hours** wrong, **days** means the watcher is not running — with the measurement quoted beside it so the number is checkable rather than asserted. Related: **#51**, **#52**, **#45**, **#39**, **#17**, **#71**. |

and the deletion pathway now carries its own `<h2>` — *“Deleting Your Account and Data”*. **The two were fixed together deliberately: they are conditions of ONE requirement on ONE page, and shipping half would have earned this row a THIRD correction.** **WHAT STANDS, RE-VERIFIED RATHER THAN CARRIED FORWARD.** *In-app deletion:* `DELETE` at `src/app/api/account/route.ts:32`, and `DeleteAccountCard` is **actually wired, not merely present** — imported and rendered at `src/app/[locale]/dashboard/settings/page.tsx:84`. **Apple 5.1.1(v) stands** on that path. **THE MISSING LEGAL ENTITY IS NOT A BLOCKER FOR THIS REQUIREMENT, AND NOTHING WAS INVENTED TO ROUTE AROUND IT.** Play’s User Data policy reads: *“The entity (for example, developer, company) named in the app’s Google Play store listing must appear in the privacy policy **or** the app must be named in the privacy policy.”* **It is an OR.** Naming the app satisfies it, so an owner with no registered company clears it by naming **KnowFlow** — which is what shipped. **No company name, no personal name and no postal address was added**, because none exists to state truthfully and Play does not ask for one here. The same policy also asks for *“developer information and a privacy point of contact or a mechanism to submit inquiries”*: the contact half is `support@tryknowflow.com`, on the page twice; the identity half is answered as far as it truthfully can be — *operated by an independent developer* — and no further. **TWO FORWARD CONSTRAINTS ON PHASE 10, RECORDED NOW BECAUSE THE PAGE IS LIVE AND THE LISTING DOES NOT EXIST YET.** **(1) THE LISTING MUST MATCH THE PAGE, NOT THE OTHER WAY ROUND.** The requirement is that the policy reference the name *“as it appears on your store listing”* — the policy now says **KnowFlow**, and `SITE_NAME` (`src/lib/site.ts`) says KnowFlow, so **the Play listing must be KnowFlow or must begin with it.** A listing named anything else would contradict a page that shipped first. **(2) PLAY PUBLICLY DISPLAYS A DEVELOPER NAME, AND WHICH ONE IS DECIDED WHEN THE ACCOUNT IS CREATED** — individual or organisation. Until that account exists there is **no verified developer name to publish**, and publishing a guess would create exactly the contradiction (1) exists to prevent. **When the account is made, read the developer name Play shows and reconcile it with this policy in the same sitting.** **ONE THING THAT IS EXPLICITLY NOT A PLAY PROBLEM, RULED FROM THE POLICY TEXT RATHER THAN FROM INSTINCT.** The three legal pages serve **English inside `dir="rtl"`** on `/ar` — measured on production 2026-09-12: `/ar/privacy` renders **1,246 Latin characters to 21 Arabic** — on a product whose routing default is **Arabic** (`src/middleware.ts:5` `defaultLocale = 'ar'`, and `getLocale` sends every non-`en` browser to `/ar`). **Play’s deletion-resource requirements contain no sentence about language, localisation, translation or locale**, read directly from the policy page. **So this is register #17’s scope and a real market defect for the Moroccan and Gulf students this product is aimed at — and it is NOT a publishing blocker.** Calling it one would be the mirror image of this row’s twice-made error: asserting a store requirement the store does not state. **NOT REACHED BY THIS RULE:** `terms/page.tsx` and `refund/page.tsx`. Play’s naming requirement attaches to the **privacy policy** and the **deletion resource**, and neither page is either. **It is moot in any case** — both already name the app (`terms` ×3, `refund` ×1, counted 2026-09-12). *Apple’s guidelines were NOT fetched for this change, so an equivalent Apple naming rule is **unchecked rather than cleared**; it is moot for the same reason.* **NOT DONE HERE:** the privacy page is **hardcoded English throughout** — it takes `locale` only to set `dir`.
