ALL THREE ARE FIXED 2026-09-12.** This row had only ever tested Apple against **5.1.1(v)**, account deletion, and passed it. Reading the neighbouring clause found that the privacy policy must *“confirm that any third party with whom an app shares user data … will provide the same or equal protection”*, must *“explain its data retention/deletion policies”*, and must be linked *“within the app in an easily accessible manner”*. **Measured before the fix:** the policy named **zero** third parties (grep: Supabase, Anthropic, Voyage, Paddle, Resend, Vercel, Railway, `third part*`, `service provider`, `sub-processor` — all 0) while document text went to two AI vendors; the word `retention` appeared **0** times; and `git grep` found **exactly one** link to `/privacy` in the whole product, `page.tsx:158`, the marketing footer — so a signed-in student had no path to it, and at Phase 8 the Capacitor shell would have had none either. **FIXED:** a *Who We Share It With* section naming all eight with the equivalence confirmation; a *How Long We Keep It* section; and a link in `dashboard/settings/page.tsx`. **The naming requirement remains Play-only** — Apple has no equivalent, which is why #148 was a Play fix and this is an Apple one. |

| 65 🔴 **OPEN — opened 2026-09-02. AN ABSENT RUN AND A FAILED RUN ON `main` ARE BOTH SILENT, AND BRANCH PROTECTION CANNOT SEE EITHER: IT GATES THE MERGE, NOT THE RESULT — AND THE COMMIT IT GREEN-LIT IS NOT THE COMMIT THAT LANDS.** Measured from the runs API on 2026-09-02, not inferred. `126537e` recorded the symptom and **deliberately declined to open a row because the cause was not diagnosed**; the mechanism below is that diagnosis, which is why the row opens now and not then. | **THE 2026-08-26 INCIDENT, TIMED FROM THE RUNS API.** PR **#85** merged at **15:41:59Z** with **five checks green on head `3efb4a8`**, `ingestion-image` among them. It squash-merged (`commits: 1`), so **`3efb4a8` IS NOT IN `main`'s ANCESTRY** — `git merge-base --is-ancestor 3efb4a8 origin/main` is false. The commit that actually landed, **`dc37f85`**, was then **never successfully built by any of the three workflows.** **(a) `db-types`** run `32985901592`, created **15:42:09Z**: job `db-types` started 15:44:56Z with **ZERO STEPS** and was **cancelled** at 15:59:58Z; run conclusion **`failure`**. **(b) `typecheck`** run `32986043413`, created **15:45:03Z**: conclusion **`startup_failure`**, **ZERO JOBS**. **(c) `ingestion-image`: NO RUN OF ANY KIND — not failed, not cancelled, NEVER CREATED.** `ingestion-image.yml` declares `on: push: branches:[main]` and carries **no `paths:` filter**, refused in its own comment precisely so the job cannot be skipped, so that absence contradicts the workflow's own design. **THIS WAS NOT A TOTAL OUTAGE, AND THAT IS THE POINT:** an `ingestion-image` **`pull_request`** run on `c755a4a` (PR **#86**'s head) **succeeded at 16:10:30Z the same day**. The control plane degraded **one push event**, recovered inside half an hour, and **nothing retried `dc37f85` — because nothing was watching it.** **NOR WAS IT A REPOSITORY DEFECT:** `git diff --stat dc37f85 436fe19` is **one added file** (`src/lib/account-deletion/paddle.ts`, +255) with **zero workflow changes**, yet the next push — `436fe19` at **21:49:42Z** — ran **all three green**. Nothing in the tree was fixed because nothing in the tree was broken. **`dc37f85` HELD `main`'s TIP UNVERIFIED FOR 6h07m33s** (15:42:09Z → 21:49:42Z), and the incident stood **unremarked for three days**, found 2026-08-29 while re-deriving this file's header tally for PR #88. | **THE VACUOUS-GREEN CLASS, ARRIVING A THIRD TIME IN A THIRD WORKFLOW — AND STRUCTURAL, NOT INCIDENTAL.** Branch protection on `main` (API, read 2026-09-02) requires exactly **`tsc`** and **`db-types`**, with `enforce_admins: true` and **`strict: false`**. Those contexts are evaluated **on the PR head**, and **the merge is the only thing they gate**; once merged, the push-triggered runs on `main` gate nothing and report to nobody. A red on `main`'s own tip is therefore not merely unblocked — it is **UNOBSERVED BY CONSTRUCTION** — while the green that authorised the merge attaches to a SHA the squash then removes from history. **Register #52's promotion rule counts greens, and A COUNT OF GREENS CANNOT SEE A COMMIT THAT WAS NEVER BUILT:** the absent `ingestion-image` run is invisible to the very rule that would promote it to required. **The cancellation signature is register #60's, exactly** — a job reporting `cancelled` with **zero steps**, where that row's own words are *"reported nothing is indistinguishable from was never scheduled"*; there it was a scheduled probe, here it is a **required** check. **Siblings, not duplicates:** **#40** was a required check green on a **wrong result**; **#52** a required check green on a **file it never reads**; **#60** a scheduled probe that **never probed**; **this row is a required check that produced NO RESULT AT ALL and was not missed by anything.** | **NO FIX IS DESIGNED HERE, DELIBERATELY.** This row carries a mechanism and stops. Naming a remedy in the same breath as a diagnosis is how a symptom becomes a half-built alert with no owner — the failure this register exists to prevent — and **any remedy belongs in its own PR, raised against this row** rather than folded into it. **ESTABLISHED:** the timings, the zero-step and zero-job signatures, the never-created run, the `strict: false` gating asymmetry, and the recovery run that rules out an outage. **NOT ESTABLISHED, AND NOT TO BE ASSUMED:** why one push event lost one of three workflow dispatches, whether the three failures share a single cause, and whether GitHub retains anything that would answer either. **DELIBERATELY NOT RESTATED: the `ingestion-image` tally in this file's header**, which `126537e` left uncarried for exactly this reason; nothing in this row licenses carrying it forward. **This row reopens nothing.** #40 and #52 keep their status, #60 keeps its scope, and #62 is untouched — this is their fourth sibling and it stands on its own evidence. |
| 66 🔴 | **THOROUGH VERIFICATION OF THE WRONG REQUIREMENT IS A DISTINCT FAILURE MODE FROM INSUFFICIENT VERIFICATION, AND THIS REPOSITORY NOW HAS A MEASURED INSTANCE.** `1c525f1` latched the account-deletion control off after a 409, because the code comment above that branch asserted *"The control is left disabled behind this message on purpose"* and the code did the opposite. It was the most heavily verified change of the 2026-09-03/04 arc: a fetch-intercepted 409 against a real signed-in session, the server proven unreachable first, and **five separate attempts to defeat the latch — real mouse click, programmatic `.click()`, synthetic `MouseEvent`, Enter, Space — all refused.** It was still wrong. Experiment (iii) then measured the shipped `cancelUserSubscriptions` against a genuinely cancelled Paddle sandbox subscription and got `{ ok: true, outcome: canceled, canceled: 1 }` with `canceledAt` unmoved, so **retrying costs the user nothing on any path and completes the deletion on at least one** — the instruction was the defect and the enabled button had been correct behaviour throughout. Reversed forward by `000d2e2`, which keeps `1c525f1` in the branch history on the ruling that a verified change enforcing a refuted requirement is worth more in the record than a clean history. **The per-mount bound is recorded as what `1c525f1` was, not as a live defect:** its latch survived only for the life of the mount, so a page reload re-armed the control the message forbade — moot the moment the policy inverted, and never fixed because it should not have been. | Not deferred work — a standing hazard with no code remedy. Review cannot detect it: every reviewer, including the author, agreed the latch was correct. | **Standing.** The only detector demonstrated is execution against the requirement itself, not against the implementation. Cite this row when a change is defended on the thoroughness of its verification. |
| 67 🔴 | **`TaskStop` DOES NOT KILL `next dev`, AND PORTS MUST BE VERIFIED FREE RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** Observed **five** times on 2026-09-03/04: the tool reports `Successfully stopped task` and kills the `npm`/`npx` wrapper while the `node …/next/dist/server/lib/start-server.js` child keeps listening. **The hazard is not tidiness.** A server started at 16:41 — hours before `.env.development.local` existed — was found still holding **port 3000**, the port anyone opens by default, configured from `.env.local` alone and therefore carrying **live Paddle credentials**, during an arc whose entire purpose was to avoid a real charge. It survived two of Claude's own explicit claims to have shut it down, both of which are false in the record. Detection is `Get-NetTCPConnection -State Listen -LocalPort 3000`; the remedy is `Stop-Process -Force` on the owning PID and a re-check that returns nothing. | Not deferred work — an operational hazard in the agent harness, not in this repository. | **Standing.** Verify ports free before starting a server; verify them free again after stopping one; and start with an explicit `-p` so a silent fallback to another port cannot happen unremarked. |
| 68 🔴 | **THREE TIMES IN ONE ARC A "COMPLETE" FIX COVERED ONE SIDE OF A BOUNDARY, AND EXECUTION FOUND ALL THREE WHILE REVIEW FOUND NONE.** (a) `.gitignore` listed `.env.local` and `.env` as **literal names**, leaving `.env.development.local` — the standard Next override file, which *outranks* `.env.local` — committable in a **public** repository; fixed in `3598a8f` (PR #94) with `.env*` plus `!.env.example`. (b) The Paddle environment switch was made configuration-driven on the **server** (`b26ad57`, PR #93) and not in the **browser**, where `initializePaddle({ environment: 'production' })` was a literal; a sandbox token therefore asked production Paddle to open a checkout for a sandbox transaction, and the failure was invisible from every side — server `200`, generic white overlay, silent console, and a CSP report to *Paddle's* Sentry. Fixed in `4d5f46a` (PR #95). (c) `/api/paddle/checkout` catches Paddle's error — which arrives with a `code`, a `detail` naming the exact remedy, and a `documentationUrl` — and returns a generic `Internal Server Error`, discarding all of it; **still open**, same family as register #84. | The pattern is the item. Each instance was individually plausible; the shape only became visible after the third. | **Standing.** When a fix crosses a boundary — server/client, one filename/a glob, error caught/error reported — check the far side explicitly before calling it complete. (c) is the outstanding instance and is deliberately unfixed: repairing it mid-arc would have contaminated an experiment measuring unmodified behaviour. |

| 70 🔴 | **THERE IS NO WAY TO CANCEL A SUBSCRIPTION EXCEPT BY DESTROYING THE ACCOUNT: a paying customer who wants to stop being billed must delete every byte of their data.** Measured 2026-09-04: `subscriptions.cancel()` appears **exactly once** in the codebase, at `src/lib/account-deletion/paddle.ts:142`, inside the deletion orchestrator; and greps for a customer portal, manage-billing, downgrade or plain-cancel path return **zero matches**. The narrower worry that prompted the audit — that `effectiveFrom: 'immediately'` might reach a non-deletion path and take away time a customer had already paid for — **does not exist, but only because the gentler path does not exist either.** For deletion, `immediately` is correct: the account is being destroyed and leaving billing live to period end would be worse. Commercial weight: the likely real-world behaviour of a customer who will not destroy their work is to keep being billed and then charge back, and both app stores expect subscription management to be reachable. Any future cancel path must also handle the multi-row case that `subscriptions.user_id`'s missing unique constraint permits (register #9). | Not a defect repair — a product decision about what a cancel path should do to entitlement and to data. | **Issue [#96](https://github.com/tornidomaroc-web/knowflow/issues/96). THE PATH IS BUILT ON `feat/70-cancel-subscription` (2026-09-05) AND THIS ROW DOES NOT CLOSE YET — SANDBOX-PROVEN THROUGH OUR OWN CODE 2026-09-07, PRODUCTION STILL UNPROVEN. PR #106 MERGED AND DEPLOYED 2026-09-07 (`a85711b`).** `POST /api/account/subscription/cancel` (blob `974ed52cf28a09d05e00b2f1aa415c2186f964c8`) calls `src/lib/subscription/cancel.ts` (blob `136314706c048183da09a262ffb0069e5ff77092`) with **`effectiveFrom: 'next_billing_period'`** — period end, never immediately, because the customer has already paid for the period and a refund is a separate `adjustments` call this codebase has never made, so `immediately` would mean **keeping their money and withdrawing the service**. The deletion path keeps `immediately` and stays right for the opposite reason. **THE FINDING THAT SHAPED IT:** the deletion module’s `verifiablyCanceled` asserts `status === 'canceled' && !scheduledChange`, the **exact opposite** of a period-end cancellation’s end state — reusing it would have **reported failure on every success** and pushed the customer toward the delete button. A separate `verifiablyScheduledToCancel` exists and the reason it is not shared is written in the code, not only in a PR body. **NO DDL AND NO DATABASE WRITE, AND THE ABSENCE OF THE WRITE IS THE DESIGN:** Paddle leaves the status `active`, the row already carries a future `current_period_end`, so `getEntitlement` keeps the customer Pro for the time they paid for and lapses them **by the clock**. Writing `status = 'canceled'` would revoke access instantly — the trap, named in the code so nobody adds it. The **customer portal** was rejected: it needs `paddle_customer_id`, written **only** by the webhook; its immediate-vs-period-end semantics live in a Paddle dashboard setting nobody here can read; and it is an off-domain redirect that abandons the Arabic half of the product. UI sits **above** the delete card and is deliberately lighter — one click to open, one to confirm, **no typed email** — because deletion is unrecoverable and this is undone by resubscribing; the no-refund fact is stated **before** the decision in both languages. **WHAT IS PROVEN AND WHAT IS NOT.** Proven: `tsc` clean, and the shipped predicates executed against synthetic shapes — 7 checks, including that the period-end end state is accepted and the **immediate** end state is rejected. ~~**NOT PROVEN: THE PADDLE ROUND TRIP.** The local `PADDLE_API_KEY` is a **live** key (no `_sdbx`) and `PADDLE_ENV` is unset, so `src/lib/paddle.ts` resolves to production; there is no sandbox credential on this machine and a cancel was **not** run against a live key.~~ **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-07 — written when it was true, kept because the sequence is the record.** A sandbox credential now exists and the round trip HAS been run, repeatedly, through our own code. **WHAT WAS WITNESSED, AGAINST `sub_01m1yqapt1zxhjvamk4hzjmsfr` — A REAL PADDLE SUBSCRIPTION MINTED BY INVOICE, NOT BY CHECKOUT.** `scheduleSubscriptionCancellation` returned `{ ok: true, outcome: 'scheduled', scheduled: 1, effectiveAt: '2026-10-07T20:00:38.204Z' }` — **the value Paddle holds**, not a value we computed. `scheduledChange.effectiveAt` equalled `currentBillingPeriod.endsAt` **EXACTLY**, which is this row's own design assertion — period end, never immediately — executed rather than argued. `readScheduledCancellation` read that same timestamp back with the change attached and returned `null` after the reset, never throwing. `verifiablyScheduledToCancel`, imported and genuinely executing, returned **true**; the deletion module's `verifiablyCanceled` returned **false on BOTH terms** against that same live success state — the trap this module exists to avoid, demonstrated on a real object. And the **catch-then-re-read arm executed** on the double-click, where Paddle throws `subscription_locked_pending_changes`: a customer who clicks twice is told the cancellation is in place, and it is. **EVERY BRANCH OF THE MODULE HAS NOW RUN.** One defect was found by doing this — a partial cancellation reported as a total failure — ruled on, fixed in `8262dd7`, and re-proven on both the all-fail and partial cases. **THE WEBHOOK DISTINCTION, TO BE KEPT INTACT:** this is correct without the webhook — entitlement lapses by the clock — but it is **unverifiable in production until a subscription exists, and the webhook is the ONLY writer of `subscriptions` in the codebase**. Nothing here may be described as production-verified. **AND THAT SENTENCE STANDS UNCHANGED, DELIBERATELY.** The key was a **sandbox** key (`_sdbx`), the Supabase client was a **stub** so no database was read or written at any point, the webhook has **never fired at our endpoint** in this arc, and our `subscriptions` table holds **no row** for `sub_01m1yqapt1zxhjvamk4hzjmsfr`. Per the owner's ruling of 2026-09-07, **production `subscriptions` holds ZERO rows**, so the full checkout → webhook → row path has never completed for a real customer — *stated on the owner's authority and NOT independently verified in that session, because `.env.local` is unreadable to the agent and no production credential was available; it is recorded as their finding, not as a measured one.* Note the conclusion does not rest on that count alone: a stubbed database and a webhook that never fired are on their own enough that nothing here has a production witness. **THE BOUNDARY, NAMED SO NOBODY HAS TO INFER IT.** *Proven:* the cancel module end-to-end against real Paddle, through our code, in **sandbox**. *Unproven:* anything in **production** — that a real customer can reach checkout, that the webhook writes the row, that `getEntitlement` then reads it, and that the cancel button works for a person who is actually paying. *What would move it:* one real subscription existing in production with a `subscriptions` row written **by the webhook**, then this same sequence run against it. NOTHING ELSE IN THIS ROW DOES, AND THE ROW STAYS OPEN.** Superseded clause, quoted so the sequence is the record and the correction is not mistaken for a contradiction: *“the webhook has **never fired at our endpoint** in this arc”* — written 2026-09-07 and true when written. On **2026-09-11** the owner ran a single-event simulation from the **LIVE** Paddle dashboard (`ntfsim_01m28s0mnzyyvsmh1fbc3c1efn`, “KnowFlow secret probe”, `subscription.created`, default payload, run once) against the active destination `ntfset_01kprw8mnymg1tyrk5kqrt90ed`, after temporarily changing that destination’s usage type from **Platform** to **Both** and restoring it to **Platform** immediately afterwards. **Observed: HTTP `200`, body `{"received": true}`.** Every dashboard fact here is `owner-attested`, read from the screen; no agent held a Paddle credential at any point. <br><br>**WHAT THAT `200` PROVES — DERIVED FROM THE ROUTE’S OWN SOURCE, NOT FROM THE DASHBOARD.** `src/app/api/paddle/webhook/route.ts` returns `{received: true}` at exactly one place (**line 182**), reachable only by running the entire `try` without throwing, and **every** branch of `classifyWebhookFailure` returns 400/401/500/503 — never 200 (`src/lib/paddle-webhook-errors.ts:164-224`). A 200 therefore licenses four facts and no more. **(1) THE DEPLOYED `PADDLE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` IS DESTINATION `…90ed`’S SIGNING SECRET** (masked tail `OyQD`, **#88**): `paddleClient.webhooks.unmarshal` (line 54) throws on an HMAC mismatch, and the probe’s one premise is that Paddle signs a destination’s deliveries — simulated or genuine — with that destination’s own secret. **That premise is named rather than buried, because the whole inference rests on it**; the alternative requires Paddle to have signed with a key that coincidentally equals our deployed value, which is not a hypothesis worth carrying. **(2) `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` AND `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` ARE BOTH SET AND NON-EMPTY IN THAT RUNTIME** — `getSupabaseAdmin()` is called **inside** the `try` (line 51) and `@supabase/supabase-js@2.103.0` throws `supabaseUrl is required.` / `supabaseKey is required.` on a falsy value, which would have been caught and answered non-2xx. **(3) THE DEMO PAYLOAD CARRIED A NON-NULL `current_billing_period.ends_at`** — the payload’s status was `active`, `ENTITLING_STATUSES` contains `active` (line 24), and the S1 HARD OBLIGATION at line 92 answers **400** when an entitling status arrives with no period end. **So that guard was evaluated in production and not taken** — the first time it has run outside a test. **(4) THE SERVICE-ROLE CLIENT REACHED THE PRODUCTION DATABASE AND THE STATEMENT RETURNED NO ERROR** — the `else` branch answers **500** on any `updateError` (lines 163-176), so PostgREST was reachable from the function, the `subscriptions` relation exists, and that key is authorised to update it. <br><br>**WHAT IT DOES NOT PROVE, AND THIS ROW TURNS ON IT.** The payload was Paddle’s demo object — subscription `sub_01hv8x29kz0t586xy6zn1a62ny`, subscription-level **`custom_data: null`**. `const userId = sub.customData?.user_id` (line 84) was therefore `undefined`, the handler took the **`else`** branch, and the `UPDATE` keyed on `paddle_subscription_id` matched **zero rows**. **Nothing was written — and that is an INFERENCE from the source plus the payload, not a measured post-state.** No agent read `subscriptions` before or after the run; per the owner’s 2026-09-07 ruling the table holds zero rows, and that ruling is itself `owner-attested`. **The run was not read-only in intent:** a write statement was issued against the production database with the service-role key and it matched nothing. What the simulation exercised is the **attribution-less** path — the one path this row does not need — and the silence of that path is now its own register row, **#87**. <br><br>**THE BOUNDARY, RE-DRAWN.** *Proven in production as of 2026-09-11:* the route is deployed and reachable; the deployed webhook secret matches the live destination; the Supabase admin environment is present and the `subscriptions` write path is live; the entitling-status guard and the no-`custom_data` branch both execute. *Still unproven in production, each needing its own witness, and none of them moved by a simulation:* **(i)** that a genuine checkout-born subscription arrives carrying `custom_data.user_id` — Paddle documents that a transaction’s custom data is copied to the subscription it creates (`developer.paddle.com`, read 2026-09-11, `vendor-documented`, **needs a re-read date**), and this repository has never witnessed it (**#87**); **(ii)** the checkout overlay itself and the price a student is actually quoted (**#81**); **(iii)** money moving; **(iv)** a renewal advancing `current_period_end`; **(v)** the entitlement flip on a real row, and the cancel button this row exists for working for a person who is actually paying. **The superseded *“what would move it”* above stands verbatim and is NOT relaxed by this update** — a simulation cannot mint a `subscriptions` row, so it can never be that witness. **Also still unread:** the destination’s URL was not quoted in the report, so “production” rests on the owner’s reading of the destination row rather than on a quoted field (**#88**). The comment at **`:197`** is explicit: *"do not update the user because we can't be sure of their claimed identity."* Only the confirmation mail is re-sent (`:250`, which is what **#75** settled). **The stored `encrypted_password` remains the one from the FIRST signup.** The teeth are that `noticeLinkExpired` — *"That confirmation link is no longer valid. Sign up again to get a new one."*, shipped in PR #126 and confirmed TRUE by #75 — **instructs the user to do exactly this**. Someone who has forgotten the original password, or who simply chooses a fresh one at a form that asks for a password, will type a new one, receive a mail that works, confirm successfully, and then find that the password they just chose does not sign them in. Nothing on screen contradicts them, and the failure is indistinguishable from mistyping. | **Not a defect in GoTrue and not something the app can suppress.** Refusing to re-send would be worse (it is the only way back for an expired link), and the password cannot be updated on an unproven address without handing account takeover to anyone who knows the address — which is the same reasoning behind `RemoveUnconfirmedIdentities` in **#74**/**#76**. The unconfirmed repeat falls through to **`:344`** and returns the **REAL** user, whose `created_at` is the ORIGINAL signup time — so the very same age-gap discriminator this PR built for **#74** would identify it exactly. **Do not build that.** The person filling in a signup form is not necessarily the owner of the address they typed, and branching the UI on that timestamp would disclose *"this address is already registered"* to a stranger: precisely the leak `sanitizeUser` exists to prevent, which upstream has simply left open on this path. **The copy must therefore be unconditional, or driven by the notice the user arrived with, never by reading the response.** **SHIPPED unconditional**, under the password field on `/signup`, both locales: *"Signing up again with an email you already used sends a new confirmation link, but keeps your first password."* with `/forgot-password` offered on the same line. **Detection was deliberately NOT built**, and the reason is recorded in a comment at the call site so the next person does not helpfully add it. Shown to everyone, discloses nothing about anyone. **This row is closed by the §5 rule it is the first test of: when a remedy ships, update the row that named it, because a register row outliving its defect becomes a false description of production and nothing in this repository detects that.** |

Losing a notice is a failure this feature already accepts; losing a sign-in that has ALREADY succeeded is not, and an unguarded throw on this path answers that person with a 500. **The justification is now executable rather than argued:** `scripts/verify-password-replaced-notice.mjs` asserts that a `null` element inside `identities` **throws** (15/15). If upstream ever changes so that it does not, the script says so from a local run instead of the guard silently becoming redundant, or worse, the hazard silently moving. **(a) is deliberately still unguarded** and must stay that way: lowercasing the provider comparison would convert a genuine upstream change into a shrug, and the timestamps logged on the `exchange ok` line by #135 are what would actually reveal one. **Read with #79**, whose real-payload observation showed that the email guard is load-bearing rather than decorative. |

GoTrue reuses the existing user row and never touches its `created_at`, so the provider identity is much newer; on a fresh signup the two are minted together. **`full_name` needed no work**: GoTrue clobbers `auth.users.raw_user_meta_data`, which `grep -rn user_metadata src` shows this app never reads, while `profiles.full_name` is written once by the signup trigger and survives. | **Shipped and measured.** #131 built `detectPasswordReplaced`, the callback wiring and the one-shot dashboard banner; #135 added the two timestamps to the `exchange ok` log after the first instrument turned out to be unreadable (a detector that fails closed logs `false` for a harmed user too, so an unbroken run of `false` was equally the signature of "armed and waiting" and "silently broken"); **removing only the email identity from the same payload returns `true`.** Without that guard this real, confirmed, correctly-linked user would have been told their password had stopped working. **(2) Both timing shapes are now measured ON THE WIRE rather than inferred from source or fabricated.** The email identity was minted **22 ms** after the user row — against the 200 ms that had merely been guessed for the same-transaction case — and the Google identity **106.6 minutes** later, both against a 60,000 ms threshold. **(3) The response shape matches what the code assumes:** `identities` is an array of objects carrying `provider` and `created_at`, and both timestamps arrive as parseable ISO strings with microseconds. | **A LIVE `true` HAS NEVER BEEN OBSERVED, AND THIS ROW MUST NOT BE READ AS CLAIMING OTHERWISE.** What is closed is that the detector is armed, correctly shaped against real data, and correct in the negative direction on a real user for the right reason. What is **not** closed is the positive direction: no harmed user has ever passed through this code. **The recipe that would produce one, RECORDED AND DELIBERATELY NOT RUN:** sign up with email and password using an address that has a Google account, **do not open the confirmation mail**, then sign in with Google from that same address. GoTrue nulls the password, drops the email identity, and the next callback logs `identities: ['google']` with `user_created_at` older than `identity_created_at` by more than 60 seconds. It needs no password typed at Google and no new account — **but it requires a deliberately unconfirmed signup against production, which is a production WRITE, and is not to be done casually.** Weigh it against what it buys: the negative direction is measured, the shape is measured, and the positive direction is a single comparison of two numbers that have already been shown to arrive correctly. **See also #78** for the two wrong shapes that do NOT fail closed and rest on GoTrue's guarantees rather than ours. |

what remains is the undecided price, its three gates (of which only the gross price is answered), and the Pro-figure problem named above. **This row does NOT close** — a row about an undecided price closes when the price is decided, not when the copy stops lying. <br><br>**A THIRD THING NOW WAITS ON THE SAME DECISION, ADDED 2026-09-11: WHETHER THE REFUND PAGE PUBLISHES A VOLUNTARY WINDOW.** The page was rewritten that day to stop promising **7 days** and to stop denying refunds after them (**#18**, and the absolute denial was unenforceable either way). **It now publishes NO window of its own, and that is a DEFERRAL to this row, not a permanent answer.** **The reason it cannot be decided ahead of the price:** Paddle’s published policy says *“if local consumer protection laws **or a Supplier of a Product** provides you with additional or non-waivable rights, the highest level of rights will always apply”* — so **a window we publish is a binding grant we cannot retract**, harder to walk back than the price itself, and it would attach to a figure this row has not settled and to a free tier **#80** has not settled either. **Publishing one now would repeat this row’s own corrected error at higher cost.** **WHAT THE OWNER WILL BE CHOOSING BETWEEN, so the deferral is a scheduled decision and not a dodge — the number is his and is deliberately not proposed here:** **(a) NONE** — costs nothing, fully retractable, and matches what monthly comparators actually publish (Coursera offers 14 days but **excludes monthly subscription charges**; Chegg’s 7 days requires a reason; Quizlet generally does not refund a missed cancellation). **(b) A SHORT WINDOW** — reads as a safety net, and cheap in practice because refund requests cluster in the first days; the cost is that it is a grant, plus the handling. **(c) ~TWO WEEKS** — the strongest trust signal, and it adds little exposure Paddle’s own discretionary period does not already create; but **on a MONTHLY plan two weeks is close to half the service free on request, where on an annual plan it is 4% of the term** — that asymmetry is why the comparators carve monthly out. **The coupling to record: a TRIAL would remove most of the reason for a window**, and the price currently has none (Trial `-`, read 2026-09-11), so (a)/(b)/(c) and the trial are ONE decision taken together with the price, not four taken separately. |

It is also self-liquidating: with the 600 cap no new answer can approach 1,200 tokens. **THE LOGGING:** one structured `kf-usage` JSON line per answered question — input/output/cache tokens, `stop_reason`, truncation flag, context chunks, history kept/dropped, and the USD cost computed from rates read 2026-09-13. **No question or answer text is logged**; `user_id` IS, because the decision this data exists to inform is the Pro daily cap and a distribution cannot answer *“what does the heaviest user cost”* without it — and Vercel is already a disclosed sub-processor on the privacy page. **THE INERT CACHE IS REMOVED, NOT REPAIRED.** `cache_control: {type:'ephemeral'}` sat on a ~110-token system prompt against Haiku 4.5's **4,096-token minimum**: it returned `cache_creation_input_tokens: 0` and read as though caching were handled. It cannot be made real here — the retrieved context changes every query by construction, and the only growing-stable prefix is the history, which **Lever 2 now caps at 1,200 tokens, so it can never reach 4,096**. The two are in direct tension and the budget wins: 37% of the worst-case question against roughly a tenth of a cent. <br><br>**PROJECTED, NOT MEASURED:** worst case **$0.02493 → $0.00691 (−72%)** and the 4.3× spread collapses to **1.28×**. **Levers 1 and 2 alone account for that**; the ceiling is what a price must survive, and collapsing the spread matters more than moving the mean. <br><br>**TWO LEVERS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT HERE.** **Lever 3 (`MATCH_COUNT` 8→5) stays out and I agree it should:** it is the only one of the four with a real quality cost — fewer passages means more *“not in your materials”* — and the measurement that would price that cost is the thing this PR installs. Cutting retrieval before the log can show the no-answer rate is cutting blind. **Lever 4 (voyage-3-large → voyage-4) was REMOVED FROM THIS PR ON MY OBJECTION, and the reason is arithmetic:** the query embedding is **$0.000007 of a $0.00745 question — 0.1%**. Lever 4 does not cut cost per question at all; its value is upload cost (3×) and a 200M-token free allowance. Meanwhile it is the ONLY lever needing a schema change, a dual-write path, a production backfill and a cutover. Bundling it would hold the logging — which everything else waits on — hostage to the riskiest work in the plan. **And it is more dangerous than it looks: voyage-4's default `output_dimension` is 1024, the SAME as voyage-3-large** (docs.voyageai.com, read 2026-09-13), so `chunks.embedding vector(1024)` will **silently accept a mixed corpus**. A dimension change would have thrown; this one cannot. Mixed vectors do not error — retrieval just returns wrong passages. Nothing else changes; live traffic keeps using `embedding`. **(2)** Ingestion DUAL-WRITES both columns for the whole transition — without this, uploads during the backfill land with only the old vector and become permanently invisible after cutover. **(3)** `backfill.py` populates `embedding_v4` for every existing chunk. Live queries are untouched throughout because nothing reads the new column yet. **(4)** GATE: `select count(*) from chunks where embedding_v4 is null` must be **0**, verified on the live DB, before anything flips. **(5)** Cutover is a single env var that moves BOTH the query model and the function called. **(6)** Old column dropped in a later PR, never the same one. **WHY NOT A WINDOW:** a window would still leave the corpus mixed DURING it; the dual column removes the mixed state entirely rather than hiding it behind a downtime notice. **WHAT I CANNOT DO FROM HERE:** run step 3 — local dev writes to the PRODUCTION database, so the backfill is an operational act for the owner with step 4 as its gate. Re-embedding itself is free (voyage-4 ships 200M free tokens; the whole corpus is far below that). Related: **#15** (the bge-m3 seam this reuses), **#94**. |

Next serves `/icon` and `/apple-icon` and emits the `<link>` tags every browser reads, but a client that probes the bare path and ignores the link still gets nothing. **The drop-in is now known and cheap:** the matcher ALREADY exempts `favicon.ico`, so committing a real `.ico` at `src/app/favicon.ico` would serve immediately with no middleware change. The route table still reads `● /[locale]/opengraph-image` with both locales prerendered, **not `ƒ`**, so the font read stays inside the build as that file’s own warning requires.<br><br>**THE PRODUCTION WITNESS, TAKEN ON BOTH LOCALES BY DECODING THE BYTES.** `GET /ar/opengraph-image` and `/en/opengraph-image` each return **200**, `image/png`, **21,669 bytes**, `Age: 0`, `X-Vercel-Cache: PRERENDER`, and sha256 `d4e54a57370725bc281831ba0c4cb65501ab8e8439ffd4615b412276acc35c3d` — **equal to the build artifact, byte for byte, on both**. Decoded: IHDR **1200x630**, RGBA 8-bit, non-interlaced; corner `#14110d`; mark centre `#f5f0e8`, which is the cream actually arriving rather than the cream intended; mark ink bbox `x[549..651] y[107..209]` = **103x103px**, matching the derived 103.2px; **2,742 ink pixels**; and **TWO connected components at 8-connectivity**, sized 2,094 and 648. **The component count is the load-bearing number**, as it was for the tile: it is what proves the 10-unit gap survived rasterisation rather than the mark arriving as one blob.<br><br>**THE POINTER WAS WITNESSED TOO, BECAUSE CORRECT BYTES AT A URL NOBODY IS SENT TO ARE WORTH NOTHING.** Both `/ar` and `/en` serve `og:image` and `twitter:image` pointing at their own `opengraph-image` route, with `og:image:width` **1200**, `og:image:height` **630**, and `twitter:card` still `summary_large_image` — the tag that made this file necessary in the first place.<br><br>**A CLAIM MADE IN THE PREVIOUS SESSION IS CORRECTED HERE RATHER THAN LEFT STANDING.** The `og:image` query hash was described as CONTENT-ADDRESSED. It is not. It moved with this change — `?d5f1e675ae952e5f` before, `?f5a4c9693edca08c` now — but a local build of the *identical* source emitted `?d9c1dfe555ac83f0` while producing **byte-identical** images, and both locales share one hash in both builds. So the hash is per-module and build-specific, not a function of the image bytes. **What survives the correction is the part the cache argument needs:** the URL changes when the card changes, so a consumer re-reading the HTML is sent to a URL it has never cached. What is NOT supported is the stronger claim that it changes *only* then.<br><br>**KNOWN AND ACCEPTED LIMIT, RECORDED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER.** Below roughly 100px wide the diamond starts to fail where the band would have survived. Ruled acceptable. At the 200px messaging thumbnail that actually binds this surface the mark holds: 16x17px of ink, the gap clearing 100% to the ground, two components at every threshold tested.<br><br>**THE TWO RESIDUALS THIS ROW DOES NOT CLOSE.** (1) **No WhatsApp, X or LinkedIn render has been seen**, and none can be from here — there is no API, no account, and the rendering is theirs. The claim is bounded to what we serve. (2) **Their page-level preview cache, keyed on the shared page URL, may still hold the old card.** A manual re-scrape through Meta’s Sharing Debugger or LinkedIn’s Post Inspector is the available lever; that is reported from knowledge and was **not measured this session**. |

Produces the App ID; needed by the native shell (Phase 8) and by step 2. **(2) Identifiers → Services ID:** a second identifier (for example `com.knowflow.web`), Sign in with Apple enabled, **Configure** → primary App ID = step 1, **Domains:** `<supabase-project-ref>.supabase.co`, **Return URLs:** `https://<supabase-project-ref>.supabase.co/auth/v1/callback`. Produces the Services ID, which is the OAuth **client ID**. **(3) Keys → new key** with Sign in with Apple enabled, primary App ID = step 1: download the `.p8` once (Apple never shows it again) and note the **Key ID**; the **Team ID** is at the top of the membership page. **(4) The client secret:** a JWT signed with the `.p8` (Supabase's guide has a generator; `iss` = Team ID, `sub` = Services ID, `kid` = Key ID, `aud` = `https://appleid.apple.com`). **Apple caps it at six months, so it expires and must be regenerated twice a year; write the date down where the Paddle notes live (#88).** **(5) Supabase → Authentication → Providers → Apple:** enable; **Client IDs** = the Services ID (and, when Phase 8 ships, the App ID after it, comma-separated, Services ID first, per the guide); **Secret Key** = the JWT from step 4. Save. **(6) Supabase → Authentication → URL configuration:** the redirect allow-list already carries `/api/auth/callback` for both origins (#125), so nothing to add unless a new origin appears. **(6b) Show the button:** in Vercel → Project → Settings → Environment Variables, add `NEXT_PUBLIC_APPLE_SIGNIN` = `enabled` for Production (and Preview), then redeploy; the button appears on the next build (`src/lib/auth/providers.ts`, #121). **(7) Witness:** open `/en/login` on production, tap Sign in with Apple, complete Apple's sheet, land on `/dashboard`; then the same on `/ar/signup`. Only the owner can take it: it creates or signs into a real account. **NOTED FOR PHASE 8, NOT FOR NOW:** inside the iOS shell Apple expects the native sheet (`signInWithIdToken` with the identity token), not the web redirect; the web start shipped here is what the web app and the Android shell use. **Related:** PIVOT_PLAN.md §5 and §7, **#76** (a provider that returns no verified email; Apple's private relay returns a relay address, which IS verified), **#125**. |

the owner sets it in Vercel after #119's console steps and redeploys, and the button appears with no code change. **(2)** The five dashboard canvases are start-aligned (`max-w-6xl`, no `mx-auto`), so the content sits against the sidebar in both directions. **(3)** Subject and date are each a `<bdi>`, rendered and proven. **(4)** `materialTopic`: the summary's first sentence when one exists (read on the server, one line, `summaryLead`), else a file name only when it reads like a title (spaces or Arabic letters, not a letters-and-digits code), else the subject alone; every inserted name is wrapped in FSI…PDI (U+2068/U+2069) and the buttons carry `dir="auto"`. **(5)** `src/lib/format-date.ts`: `ar-MA` (Arabic months, Western digits) or `en-GB`, day-month-year, used everywhere; the proof walks `src` for any `toLocale*String` left. The platform label is a dictionary key ("الويب" / "Web"). **(6)** `MaterialCard` renders the four controls in one grid (2 columns, 4 on `sm`) with full-width idle buttons; an opened panel spans the row (`:has`). **Related:** #85, #119, #113. |

## 5. Standing rules (working principles)

- **When in doubt, deny.** Fail closed on limits/entitlement/uploads.
- **Fix what we break, in place.** After fixing a defect, hunt its siblings
  (e.g. the DropZone accept↔allowlist↔hint alignment; the 429 body swallow).
- **Close both halves of a vulnerability** — extension *and* MIME, filename
  sanitize *and* tempfile suffix, etc.
- **All entitlement reads go through `getEntitlement`** — never a raw
  `subscriptions`/`profiles.plan` read. One source of truth.
- **Never promise what the app doesn't do** — no page-level citations (file/section
  only), no "ad-free" until ads exist, no "Unlimited", real interpolated limits.
- **No usage number reaches a public surface until it has been checked against the
  cost model — and a number the model cannot yet justify is not published at all.**
  A cap printed to a student, or a price printed beside it, is a PROMISE we have to
  fund. Ruled 2026-09-10, on three instances found in one session. `$49`/month is live
  on `tryknowflow.com` in both locales while `PIVOT_PLAN.md:45` has listed that exact
  figure under *"must change"* since the pivot (register **#81**); the same block
  promises Pro *"10 Subjects"* while the code grants **50**; and PR **#142** was one
  review away from printing Pro's **2,000 questions/day** to a Pro student — a ceiling
  register **#80** measures at **$615/month** of inference against **$9–11** net revenue.
  **The gate is the model, not the reviewer's memory**, and per #80 the model currently
  cannot price anything: it has no Paddle fees, no VAT, no per-market table and no
  usage distribution. **Free figures publish** — they are settled, and they are what an
  upgrade decision needs. **Unsettled ceilings do not**, because publishing one
  publishes a number we already intend to break.
- **Honest placeholders** — a not-yet-wired feature reads as *not measured*
  (ghost `—`), not as a working zero.
- **Branch protection on `main` with `enforce_admins`** — every phase is one
  branch → PR → review → merge. Nothing merges automatically.
- **One step at a time** — commit per step, stop for review, don't jump ahead on a
  deferred item or skip a main phase (this file exists to enforce that).
- **Presentation-only means locale-diff-clean & logic-verbatim** — restyle without
  touching engine wiring; prove preservation (diff/grep), don't assert it.
- **Verify payment/security facts in code, not from memory** before asserting them.
- **A migration file is a plan, not a fact.** After any migration, **verify the
  live DB matches the repo** (`information_schema`) — never treat a written
  migration file as an applied one (see register #23: the Phase-0 rate limits sat
  inert because `usage_counters`/`increment_usage` were never actually applied).
- **Apply a migration to production BEFORE merging it.** The merge is the record;
  the apply is the fact, and they are not the same act. Applying first means a
  failure amends a branch instead of owing a corrective migration, and it removes
  the window in which a dated repair file sits in `main` describing something that
  never happened. **Ruled 2026-08-29 on register #23's precedent** — the sibling
  of the rule above, and the pasted live read is what closes a register row, never
  the merge.
- **When a remedy ships, update the row that named it.** A register row describes a
  defect; the moment the defect is repaired the row becomes a false description of
  production, and nothing in this repository detects that. Register **#61** went on
  calling a corrected privacy sentence *"a live, public, false claim"* for **nine
  days** after `2968ee5` fixed it, and that stale text was then used to justify a
  sequencing decision. **This file is a record, and register #62's thesis applies to
  it: a record is not a fact.** Ruled 2026-09-03 on #61's own instance. **EXTENDED 2026-09-10, on a THIRD instance with a shape the rule as written did not
  reach.** #61 points at *the row that named it*; the #74 rule below points at *the row
  your subject names*. **Neither reaches a row that merely CITES another row's defect in
  passing** — and that is exactly where the third one hid. Register **#80** closed with
  *"NOT FIXED, AND THIS CHANGE MAKES IT LOUDER: the 429 body is English in every locale
  (register #1)"*. PR **#142** fixed precisely that, and #80's sentence became false at
  the moment of the merge — in a row nobody was editing, about a defect nobody was
  working on, under a subject naming a different register. **So the rule is: when a
  remedy ships, update the row that named it AND grep the register for every other row
  that cites the same defect.** One command: `grep -n "register #NN" docs/PROGRESS.md`.
  A claim does not become true because it is quoted somewhere unrelated.
- **Pass `--body` explicitly when squash-merging, too — the rule below covers the
  subject and the subject alone.** `squash_merge_commit_message` is
  `COMMIT_MESSAGES` (verified 2026-09-04 via the API), so the permanent body is
  taken from the **commit**, and the pull-request body — where reviewers write
  caveats, because that is where reviewers are reading — never reaches `main`.
  The failure is the same shape as the subject rule's: a line that cannot be
  corrected afterwards on a protected branch is chosen by something other than
  the author's intent. Caught on PR **#93**, whose commit message carried a
  verification matrix and no caveat while its PR body carried the one open
  claim; it was passed explicitly and survives in `b26ad57`. Caught again on
  **#95**, whose PR body alone held the register-#84 note.
- **Before merging, read the register row your `(#NN)` subject names, and check
  it is actually the row you worked on.** One command, the same shape as the
  content check below: `grep '^| NN ' docs/PROGRESS.md`, then read it. Ruled
  2026-09-10 on register **#74**'s own instance. #74 says *"there is no password
  reset in this application at all"*. It was remedied by PR **#128** on
  2026-09-08 — and then **three further PRs (#131, #135, #136) shipped under a
  `feat(#74)` subject while being about something else entirely**, the
  password-destruction notice, which had no row at all until **#79**. The tag
  propagated across three merges, three PR bodies and two register edits without
  once being read against the text it pointed at, and the row sat 🔴 OPEN on
  `main` for **36 hours** describing an absence that had already been filled,
  still carrying sequencing advice that had already been followed. **Nothing in
  this repository detects that**, which is the same sentence register #61 earned
  and the reason the "when a remedy ships, update the row that named it" rule
  exists — so this is the SECOND instance of a failure that rule was written to
  prevent, and the rule did not catch it because the rule assumes you know which
  row you are on. **A subject is a claim about which defect this commit
  addresses. It is permanent on a protected branch, and it is the string every
  future reader greps for.**
- **"Fails closed" is a claim about the PRODUCER's guarantees, not a property of
  your parser — name what it rests on, or do not make the claim.** A function
  that returns a safe default on every input *it* rejects is only safe overall if
  the thing upstream cannot hand it the inputs it *accepts and misreads*. Those
  are different statements, and the second one is the one that matters. Ruled
  2026-09-09 on register **#78**: `detectPasswordReplaced` was described as
  failing closed on any wrong shape, and **executing it showed two shapes that do
  not** — a false positive that would tell a user with a working password that it
  had stopped working, and an uncaught `TypeError` that would answer a successful
  sign-in with a 500. Both are unreachable, but **only because of two incidental
  properties of GoTrue** (a lowercase provider literal its own lookups depend on,
  and a value-typed slice that cannot contain nulls) — neither of which is a
  promise upstream made to us, and both of which live in a release we do not
  control. **The rule is not "add a `try`/`catch"'.** It is that a fail-closed
  claim must arrive with the producer guarantee it stands on written next to it,
  because an unqualified one reads as a property of code we own and survives long
  after the guarantee it silently depended on has moved.
- **Before squash-merging, prove the content is not already on `main` — with
  `git diff --stat origin/main <commit>`, never with `git branch --contains`.**
  An empty diff means the work is already there, whatever `--contains` says.
  **What was believed, 2026-09-09:** that `ab6ab5c` was unmerged and would ride
  into an unrelated PR. **The command that produced the belief:**
  `git branch -r --contains ab6ab5c`, which returned the two feature branches and
  never `origin/main`. **What that command cannot see:** a squash mints a NEW
  SHA, so the original commit is contained *nowhere*, permanently. PR **#130**
  had already squashed exactly that content into **`8401fad`** — the very commit
  the corrective rebase was cut onto. `--contains` answers *"is this SHA an
  ancestor"*, which is not the question; the question is *"is this work on
  `main`"*. **This is register #71's class: an absence of evidence asserted as a
  fact.** **What it cost:** PR **#132** merged as **`936db26`** and changed
  nothing — `git diff --stat 45d4707 936db26` is **empty**. An unremovable empty
  commit sits on a protected branch duplicating #130's subject. **What it would
  have cost had `ab6ab5c` diverged from what #130 squashed:** stale content
  re-applied on top of `main` under a subject asserting it was new work, with
  **every check green**, because each PR is only ever green against its own base.
  The API did not contradict any of this: it reported `mergeable: true` and
  `changed_files: 4` because that diff is computed against the merge base
  `93aa90c`, the **parent of the squash**, not against current `main`.
- **Two PRs that are green individually are not a tested combination — merge them
  locally and run the gates BEFORE the combination exists on `main`.**
  `required_status_checks.strict` is **`false`** (read 2026-09-09 via the API), so
  a PR is never rebuilt against work that merged after its own checks ran, and
  nothing on the protected branch ever tests the union. The sibling of the rule
  above and of register #23's: **the merge is the record, the run is the fact.**
  Instance, 2026-09-09: **#131** and **#132** both edited `en.ts` and `ar.ts`, and
  neither one's `tsc` run had seen the other's tree. With #131 already on `main`,
  #132 was merged onto it **locally** first and the full set run on the result —
  `tsc` clean, dash scan clean, `verify-recovery-landing` 7/7,
  `verify-password-replaced-notice` 14/14. **It passed, which is exactly why this
  is a standing rule and not a register row:** the check that finds nothing is the
  one worth keeping, because the run is what makes the combination a fact rather
  than an inference from two unrelated green ticks.
- **Pass `--subject` explicitly when squash-merging.**
  `squash_merge_commit_title` is `COMMIT_OR_PR_TITLE` (verified 2026-08-29 via the
  API), so the permanent subject is chosen by the branch's **commit count** — the
  commit title when there is one, the PR title when there are more. An incidental
  property of a branch should not decide a line that cannot be corrected afterwards
  on a protected branch. Owner records `dc37f85` as having shipped a false subject
  through this path.
- **When a probe could write to the signal you are relying on, read that signal
  BEFORE and AFTER — only the pair can tell contamination from a genuine null.**
  Ruled 2026-09-11, and **REWRITTEN THE SAME DAY ONTO THE INSTANCE THAT ACTUALLY
  HAPPENED.** **Superseded rule, quoted so the failure is legible:** *“A probe against
  a live system can consume the evidence it was run to test — record the signal’s
  BEFORE state, in writing, or the probe destroys the thing it was meant to confirm …
  Nobody read that line before firing the probe, nobody read it after.”* **That rule was
  written from an event that did not occur.** The destination’s log WAS read — twice
  before the simulation and once after — and it was unchanged (register **#88**).
  **A rule in this section asserting a thing happened, when it did not, is the exact
  record-versus-fact failure §5 exists to catch**, and keeping it with a disclaimer
  attached would have been worse than deleting it: a rule that carries its own
  falsification is dead weight, and a reader who finds one stops trusting the citations
  on the rules beside it. **So it is neither kept-with-a-caveat nor dropped.** The run
  earned a real rule; it is simply the inverse of the one first written. **WHAT ACTUALLY
  HAPPENED, AND WHY IT IS WORTH A RULE:** the owner read `"No notifications yet"` twice,
  fired the probe, then read it again — still empty, zero rows, Events 3, Active.
  **The after-reading alone proves only that a SIMULATED delivery does not enter that
  log.** It is the BEFORE pair that turns the after-reading into *no genuine notification
  has ever been delivered to this destination* — a positive measurement across the
  **142 days** since its creation, where register **#70** had carried an inference. Had
  the probe written to that log, the before-reading would have been the only thing
  separating “the probe contaminated it” from “it was never empty”, and after the fact
  nothing could reconstruct it. **This is register #71’s class handled correctly rather
  than repeated:** #71 is an absence of evidence asserted as a fact; this is an absence
  measured at two known instants and therefore admissible. **The cost of the discipline
  is one line read twice. The cost of skipping it is a conclusion that cannot be
  reconstructed at any price.**

---

## 6. Maintenance

Update at the **end of each step**: flip the step's status, add its commit, move
any newly-deferred item into §4 with its target phase, and bump *Last updated*.
When a phase merges, set its row to ✅ **DONE** with the merge commit/PR.

---

## 7. Changelog

**Append-only. Newest first. One block per update.** Entries are **immutable once they land** - a
correction is a NEW entry that supersedes the old one, never an edit to an existing one. Each update
adds exactly one `### YYYY-MM-DD - <slug>` block directly below this preamble and changes nothing
else in this section, so `git diff` renders it as a single added hunk with zero deletions. Dates
repeat, so every slug disambiguates its own entry.

**Migrated 2026-07-22** out of the `Last updated` header field, which had accreted this log into a
single 37,590-byte line. All **17** entry bodies moved **byte-identical** - nothing reflowed,
re-wrapped, added, or stripped, including each body's original (and in nine cases unbalanced)
parentheses. **Preservation proof:** the sorted, NUL-joined sha256 of the 17 bodies is
`53e227aefee8d1e8a769d092c824345baaca6679419473f67d24bf930d0eb78b`, identical before and after; the
bodies total 37,095 bytes. **This retires the Option C frozen-tail invariant by design** - that hash
existed only to police a boundary inside an unreviewable single line, and the append-only rule above
supersedes it. No bespoke hash is needed for future updates: the diff is the proof.

### 2026-09-25 - six defects seen on production the same day, fixed and held: #121 opens and closes; #119 takes the flag that shows the Apple button once the provider is enabled

**Appends one block; adds row #121; corrects #119 in place (one sentence, quoted). Outside this file: `src/lib/auth/providers.ts` (new), `src/lib/format-date.ts` (new), `src/lib/ask-suggestions.ts` (topic from content, isolates), the agent page (reads the summary lead), `ChatBox.tsx`, `KBSelector.tsx`, `StudentHome.tsx` (canvas, Continue card, locale), `SubjectsList.tsx`, `SettingsPanel.tsx`, the subject page and the new-subject page (canvas), `RecentActivity.tsx` (translated platform, one date), `ConversationSidebar.tsx`, the settings page, `MaterialCard.tsx` (the action grid), the four material controls (full-width idle buttons), one dictionary key per locale, a new proof `scripts/verify-production-defects.mjs`, and one step in `.github/workflows/typecheck.yml`. Rows 42, 69 and 81 are not touched, and Section 7 takes no deletions.**

**WHAT THE OWNER SAW, AND WHY EACH ONE GOT PAST THE PREVIEWS.** The previews render literal data in the agent's own screen sizes. Real data has file names that are export codes, real conversations have Latin subject names inside Arabic sentences, and a real monitor is wider than the 1568px window the witnesses were taken in. The Apple button was shipped knowingly unwired (#119 said so) and a student cannot read a register row; that one was a judgment error, corrected here: a control that cannot work is not shown.

**THE SIX FIXES.** (1) `NEXT_PUBLIC_APPLE_SIGNIN=enabled` shows the button; anything else hides it. (2) The canvases start at the sidebar. (3) `<bdi>` around the subject and the date. (4) The topic of a suggested question is the summary's first sentence, else a title-like file name, else the subject; every inserted name is isolated with FSI…PDI and the buttons are `dir="auto"`. (5) `formatDate` everywhere: `ar-MA` with Western digits or `en-GB`, day-month-year; the platform label is translated. (6) The four material controls sit in one grid with equal cells.

**HOW IT IS PROVEN, AND THAT THE PROOF CAN FAIL.** `scripts/verify-production-defects.mjs` reads the flag module against the env, walks `src` for any `toLocale*String` and for centred canvases, renders the real `StudentHome` with a Latin subject in an Arabic Continue sentence and checks the `<bdi>`s and the translated platform, drives the real `materialTopic` with the owner's own example (`xilvaroth-n11-20260810` is refused, a summary lead wins, an Arabic file name passes) and checks the isolates in a rendered Arabic question, and reads the material card's grid and the four buttons. On `main` it FAILED on all six; on the branch it passes. It is a step in the required `typecheck` workflow.

**WHAT WAS LOOKED AT.** The home, subjects, subject and settings previews at 375px and 1280px, Arabic and English, dark and light, on the Vercel preview; the Ask suggestions and the live Continue card are held by the proof, since both need a session.

### 2026-09-25 - the store assets are specified to the pixel and the first submission is ruled iPhone-only: #120 opens

**Appends one block; adds row #120. Outside this file: `docs/store/STORE_ASSETS.md` (new). No code. Rows 42, 69 and 81 are not touched, and Section 7 takes no deletions.**

**WHAT THE FILE IS FOR.** The owner designs the app icon and the store icon outside this repository, and ruled that nothing here draws one. What the repository can do is say exactly what each store accepts, so the files come back right the first time: the iOS icon (one 1024 px opaque square; iOS derives the rest and applies its own mask), the Play listing icon (512 px, 32-bit PNG with alpha, square, Play rounds it) and the adaptive icon layers (108 dp with a 66 dp safe circle), the Play feature graphic (1024 × 500, no alpha), the screenshot sets for both stores with the sizes read from App Store Connect Help and the Play Console Help on 2026-09-25, the listing text limits, and the order of screens to shoot from what this run shipped. Apple's HIG page could not be read by the tool this session and the file says so; the owner re-reads all three pages the week of submission.

**THE RULING: iPHONE-ONLY FIRST.** Capacitor's iOS template is Universal; the first submission sets `TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = 1`. Three reasons, in order of weight: review runs on what is declared, and a Universal app is reviewed on an iPad with a 13" screenshot set (2064 × 2752) that no screen in this run was designed or looked at for (the dashboard's `md` breakpoint puts an iPad into the desk layout); the audience is on phones by the market ruling of 2026-09-08; and adding iPad later is a target setting and a screenshot set, while removing it after a Universal launch means taking the app away from iPad users. Play gets the equivalent (phones only; tablet screenshots are required only when tablet support is declared). Reopen when a real iPad layout has been designed and looked at.

**WHAT THIS DOES NOT CLOSE.** #99 (the in-app mark) stays open: `MARK_PATHS` still serves only the web tile and the share card. The icon and the store icon are the owner's, and the record does not pretend otherwise.

### 2026-09-25 - the signed-in screens stop reading as empty: the approved feature list is written down at last, the four screens carry progress, a study kit, suggested questions and a way back from rows the app already writes, and what needs a migration or a paid call stops at the spec

**Appends one block; corrects #85 in place, quoting each sentence it makes false. Outside this file: `docs/design/SIGNED_IN_FEATURES.md` (new: what five leading apps show, what ships now, what waits for the owner), `src/lib/subject-stats.ts` and `src/lib/ask-suggestions.ts` (pure, proven), `SubjectsList.tsx` (rebuilt), `SubjectHeader.tsx` and `MaterialCard.tsx` (new), the subjects page and the subject page, `KBSelector.tsx` (`?kb=`), `ChatBox.tsx` (suggested first questions), the agent page (material names), `StudentHome.tsx` and the home page (the Continue card), three preview routes (`/[locale]/preview/subjects`, `/subject`, `/upload`), four dictionary blocks per locale, a new proof `scripts/verify-signed-in-features.mjs`, and one step in `.github/workflows/typecheck.yml`. Rows 42, 69 and 81 are not touched, and Section 7 takes no deletions.**

**THE LIST THAT WAS NEVER WRITTEN.** #85 recorded that the owner approved "new in-app features" to fill the signed-in pages and that nine work items waited on them; the list existed only in conversation. It is now `docs/design/SIGNED_IN_FEATURES.md`, in three parts, and part 3 is the honest half: flashcards with spaced repetition, quiz mastery, notes, exam dates, other question kinds, audio, mind maps, regeneration, reminders and sharing each need a migration, a paid call or data the app does not collect, and each stops there with what it needs named.

**WHAT THE LEADING APPS SHOWED, AND THE BAR IT SET.** StudyFetch, Turbo AI, Knowt, Mindgrasp and NotebookLM were read from their public pages and one independent review (not signed into, and the record says so). Four things repeat: a material is shown with the set of things made from it; the library says how far along each unit is; the ask screen never starts blank; and every one of them has retrieval practice with scheduling, which KnowFlow lacks and which is Phase 6.

**WHAT SHIPS, FROM DATA THE APP ALREADY HAS.** A subject card now says materials, summarised, quizzed, a bar of summarised over materials (the home's measure), the last thing that happened in it, and offers Ask and Add material; an empty subject says so and offers only the latter. The subject page opens with the same counts and an Ask button, and each material is a card with its status in the student's language and a STUDY KIT line: summary ready or not, quiz ready or not. The Ask screen, when empty, offers three questions built from the subject's material names in the student's language; a pressed one is the student's own message through the unchanged route and the same daily cap, and nothing is sent until pressed. The home shows a Continue card for the most recent conversation. Four reads were added, all `Promise.all`ed, all RLS-scoped: `quizzes(document_id)` twice, `conversations(kb_id, created_at)`, `documents(id, kb_id, filename)`.

**HOW IT IS PROVEN, AND THAT THE PROOF CAN FAIL.** `scripts/verify-signed-in-features.mjs` drives the real `subjectStats` (a failed material counts as a material and never as progress; two quizzes on one material count once; the newest conversation beats the newest material) and the real `askSuggestions` (three at most, extensions stripped, every placeholder filled, a subject with no materials still asked about), renders the real `SubjectsList`, `SubjectHeader` and `MaterialCard` with `react-dom/server` (the 75% bar, the `?kb=` Ask link, the checklist, the status in words, no Ask on an empty subject), and reads the wiring and the spec. On `main` it FAILED (no modules, no components, no spec); on the branch it passes. It is a step in the required `typecheck` workflow.

**WHAT WAS LOOKED AT, AND WHAT COULD NOT BE.** The subjects and subject previews, inside the real chrome, in both locales and both themes at phone width, on the Vercel preview. The Ask screen's suggestions could not be looked at without a session, because `KBSelector` reads conversations through Supabase on mount; they are held by the proof. The summary and quiz sections are unchanged and were not re-witnessed.

### 2026-09-25 - an upload shows real bytes, then an honest estimate that never claims done, and says what to do if it runs long: #113 closes

**Appends one block; corrects #113 in place and closes it, quoting its status. Outside this file: `src/lib/upload-progress.ts` (the measured estimate, the stages, the 90% cap), `src/components/upload/DropZone.tsx` (XMLHttpRequest with upload progress, the filename, the seconds, the bar, the sentences), six dictionary keys per locale, a new proof `scripts/verify-upload-progress.mjs`, and one step in `.github/workflows/typecheck.yml`. Rows 42, 69 and 81 are not touched, and Section 7 takes no deletions.**

**WHAT A STUDENT SEES NOW.** The file's name and, while the bytes leave the phone, a bar that is those bytes (`XMLHttpRequest`'s `upload.onprogress`; `fetch` cannot report it, which is the one reason the transport changed). Then the server's wait: a bar that advances against the measured rate and stops at 90% until the answer arrives, a counter of seconds, and a sentence that changes with the time: "Reading your file…", "Preparing it for questions…", and past the estimate "Still working on it…" with the note that large files can take a minute or two and that leaving the page is safe, because the service owns the final status and the material appears after a refresh (#113's own remedy, now in both locales).

**WHY THE ESTIMATE IS SAID AS "USUALLY".** The service reports no stages (B6's shape, unchanged), so the only numbers the browser has are bytes sent and time elapsed. The rate is #50's measurement, 4 MB in 80.5 s on production, with a floor for the fixed cost of a small file; it is stated as "usually about N seconds", never promised, and the bar cannot reach the end before the server does.

**HOW IT IS PROVEN, AND THAT THE PROOF CAN FAIL.** `scripts/verify-upload-progress.mjs` imports the real estimate module (a 4 MB file lands near 80 s, a tiny file keeps a floor, the stages turn over in order, the fraction never passes 0.9), reads `DropZone.tsx` for the transport, the checks #50 and #111 shipped, the filename, the seconds and the live region, and reads both dictionaries. **WHY IT IS A SUBMISSION ITEM AND NOT A FEATURE.** Apple guideline 4.8 (PIVOT_PLAN.md §5, 2026-08-24) requires an app that offers a third-party login such as Google to offer Sign in with Apple beside it. The record carried that sentence in §5 and §7 and had no register row, and `src` had Google alone (the 2026-09-23 audit). A store submission with Google alone is a rejection.

**WHAT SHIPS.** `startOAuth(provider)` is the whole start for both providers: the browser client's `signInWithOAuth`, PKCE verifier in a cookie, return to `/api/auth/callback`, a refusal returned rather than thrown. The Apple button follows Apple's Human Interface Guidelines for this control, which is a requirement: the logo, the words "Sign in with Apple" / "Continue with Apple", 44px, a solid fill with the logo and text in the opposite colour. It is painted `bg-foreground text-background`, so it is Apple's white button on the dark theme and Apple's black button on the light one with no theme branch. The callback needed no change: a provider's `access_denied` and every other refusal already come back to it the same way Google's do.

**WHAT DOES NOT SHIP, AND CANNOT FROM HERE.** Apple does not answer yet. Until the Services ID, the key and the Supabase provider switch exist, Supabase refuses the start with *“provider is not enabled”* and the button shows its sentence, which is the honest state. Every step is a console the agent has no credential for, and each is listed in #119 with what it produces.

**HOW IT IS PROVEN, AND THAT THE PROOF CAN FAIL.** `scripts/verify-apple-signin.mjs` calls the real `startOAuth` against a recording Supabase stub (provider `apple`, redirect to `<origin>/api/auth/callback`, a refusal returned), renders the real Apple button with `react-dom/server` (wording, logo in the text colour, 44px, the foreground-on-background fill), reads both pages for the button under Google's, and reads both dictionaries. On `main` it FAILED: no `oauth.ts`, no `AppleButton.tsx`, no Apple keys; on the branch it passes. It is a step in the required `typecheck` workflow.

### 2026-09-25 - the light theme is real and a student can choose it: one cookie, one boot script before paint, a toggle in every chrome; the 404 goes dark, `color-scheme` is set, and Arabic loses the last tracking utility

Outside this file: `src/lib/theme.ts` (the cookie, the inline boot script, `applyTheme`), `src/components/layout/ThemeToggle.tsx` (segmented and icon forms), `[locale]/layout.tsx` (`data-theme` on `<html>`, the script in `<head>`), `DashboardShell.tsx` (no hardcoded theme), `Sidebar.tsx`, `MobileNav.tsx` and `SiteHeader.tsx` (the icon toggle), `[locale]/not-found.tsx` (new), `globals.css` (`color-scheme` in three scopes, `tracking-wide` neutralised under RTL), three dictionary keys per locale, a new proof `scripts/verify-theme.mjs`, and one step in `.github/workflows/typecheck.yml`. **THE RULING.** The owner ruled on 2026-09-25 that the light theme is not deferred: design it across every screen and ship a real toggle, dark by default. The dark base and the single gold accent stand (#101).

**HOW THE CHOICE IS CARRIED, AND WHY IT IS A SCRIPT.** The choice is one cookie, `kf-theme`, one year. An inline script in `<head>` reads it, or a `?theme=` query that also writes it, and sets `data-theme` on `<html>` before first paint, so every token in `globals.css` resolves to the chosen palette and a student who chose light never sees a dark flash. It is a script and not a server read because every marketing page is prerendered static and must stay so; `<html>` carries `suppressHydrationWarning` for exactly this one attribute. The toggle is a client control that reads `<html>`'s attribute after mount, which is the only place the truth is, and starts indeterminate rather than guessing.

**WHAT HAD TO GO.** `DashboardShell` (the chrome extracted for #83) hardcoded `data-theme="dark"`. #85 had recorded that attribute as "the entire light half of the toggle"; with a real toggle it would have pinned the whole app dark whatever the student chose. It is gone, and the proof fails if it returns.

**THE THREE ITEMS #101 HELD.** `color-scheme` is set on `:root` and in both themed blocks, so the UA's own surfaces, scrollbars and form controls follow the theme. `[locale]/not-found.tsx` exists, paints on the tokens inside the locale layout, and is bilingual by design because `not-found` receives no params and a lost student may be on the wrong locale. `tracking-wide` (16 sites, all under `[locale]`) is neutralised under `[dir="rtl"]` the way #101 did `tracking-tight`, and the proof walks `src` so a new tracking utility cannot reach Arabic unreset.

**HOW IT IS PROVEN, AND THAT THE PROOF CAN FAIL.** `scripts/verify-theme.mjs` executes the real `THEME_BOOT_SCRIPT` against a fake document six ways (no cookie, light, dark, garbage, `?theme=light` writing the cookie, `?theme=dark` over a light cookie) and checks the shell, the layout, the stylesheet and the 404. On `main` it FAILED with twelve findings; on the branch it passes. It is a step in the required `typecheck` workflow.

**WHAT WAS LOOKED AT.** Both themes, both locales, at 390px, on the Vercel preview: the student home inside the real chrome, the landing, pricing, login and the 404. The light palette is #85's, unchanged in value; what this change did was make it reachable on every screen and look at every screen in it.

'ar' : 'en'`. They now collapse anything unknown to Arabic. Every shipped client sends its locale, so the arm fires only for a hand-made request; the direction is the ruling's.

### 2026-09-24 - #114: a summary the model did not finish is never saved, and its length is set by the prompt with a ceiling sized to hold it in Arabic

**Appends one block and adds row #114. Nothing else in the file changes, and Section 7 takes no deletions.** Outside this file: `src/app/api/summarize/route.ts` (word budget, derived ceiling, refusal of any stop but `end_turn`), a comment-only correction in `src/app/api/quiz/generate/route.ts`, two new proofs under `scripts/`, two steps in `.github/workflows/typecheck.yml`, and `supabase/manual/20260924_reset_cutoff_summaries.sql`, which has not been run.

**WHAT WAS FOUND.** The 2026-09-24 cost measurement asked for one Arabic summary of a 13,421-character document. The model hit the 1,024-token ceiling mid-word (`stop_reason "max_tokens"`) and the route saved it, because it never read the stop reason. A saved summary is final (#26), so the student was locked out of a whole one. The ceiling was not the only cause. The prompt set no length at all, so no ceiling could have been right for every document.

**WHAT CHANGES.** The prompt asks for at most 600 words, covering every major part briefly and ending on a complete sentence. The ceiling is derived from that budget in the costliest language: 600 words × 3.7 Arabic tokens a word (measured) × 1.5 headroom = 3,330 tokens, about $0.102 per summary at the 120,000-character worst case. Any stop but `end_turn` is refused with a 502, logged `[summary-incomplete]`, and not saved. The quiz route is unchanged in behaviour: a cut-off quiz already cannot parse, so it is never saved, and that is now proven at every one of 607 cut positions.

**HOW IT IS PROVEN, AND THAT THE PROOF CAN FAIL.** `scripts/verify-summary-cutoff.mjs` fails on `main`'s unchanged route with 9 problems, reproducing the production defect exactly, and passes 4 of 4 on the branch. Its control (a finished summary is saved once) passes on both, so it is not a check that fails everything. `scripts/verify-quiz-cutoff.mjs` passes on both, and a lenient parse, applied and reverted, made it fail with 463 of 607 cuts saved. Neither script touches a network, a credential or a database. **Not yet proven on production:** that needs one fresh Arabic summary after merge (about $0.02 and one daily summary credit), whose line must read `end_turn` and `truncated false` and match the Console.

### 2026-09-23 - the queued record: the §5 measurement lands, per-account counting is decided, the usage lines get their home, and two lessons from tonight's witnesses are written down

**THE §5 MEASUREMENT IS DONE, AND SIZE BINDS FIRST.** Owner-run on production: a 4 MB text file, the largest the app now accepts, ingested in 80.5 s (200, 1,845 chunks). The service's health address answered in about a quarter of a second before and after, ten minutes apart, so it does not sleep, and the 80.5 s is a warm service. That time could not succeed under the 10 s ceiling, so Fluid compute is on and the ceiling is 300 s: a 3.7× margin at the limit. Consumed: one upload credit, about a million Voyage tokens (accepted beforehand as up to $0.18), one permanent study-event row. The material was deleted, confirmed by a fresh load of the documents table.

**WHAT THE RUN ALSO FOUND.** For the whole 80 s the drop zone showed one static line, `جارٍ المعالجة...`. That is #113: a design gap that loses users, not a defect. **Two lessons for whoever witnesses next, written while fresh:** first, the browser records a request's timing only once its reply body is consumed, and the delete control returns on a 200 without reading the body, so the delete left no timing entry, and the material disappearing from the list, which only a 2xx triggers, was the proof instead; second, an empty network log is not a zero. In the #189 witness the extension's log recorded nothing at all, not even the page's own requests, and it was set aside for the page's own Resource Timing record, which is complete since the last full load, holds 250 entries, and can be checked for both.

**THE REST OF THE QUEUE.** #112 records the owner's decision to count summaries and quizzes per account, which #80 named as the owner's call and did not decide; the ceiling itself is still to be set, from measured costs. #94 becomes the home of all three `kf-usage` lines, extended by #185 to summaries and quizzes through one shared rate module, and all three stay unwitnessed until the owner reads them in Vercel. #110's pointer to #80 now points to #94. #111 carries the copy item the owner ruled tonight, the Arabic unit ميغابايت, which rides with that row's fix and is not changed here.
