Nothing else in the file changes, and Section 7 takes no deletions.**

**THE CEILING WAS THE PLATFORM'S, AND IT COUNTS HEADERS.** The record said ~4.5 MB. Measured on a preview, with requests refused before any database call, the platform refuses a request once its body and headers together pass about 4,500,000 bytes, and the browser sends the file raw inside a 402-byte-plus-filename envelope, so the largest file that passes is about 4,490,000 bytes, 4.28 MB as Windows counts. The owner's ruling covered exactly this case (*“set the limit where a student's file actually passes”*), so the limit is 4 MB (4,194,304 bytes), which leaves about 300 KB for headers. Files between 4 and ~4.28 MB, which uploaded before, are now refused.

**WHAT SHIPPED (#189, `cf4eba4`).** One constant in `src/lib/upload-limits.ts`, read by the browser check, the line under the drop zone and the route's own guard. One sentence in `src/lib/limit-messages.ts`, in both locales, composed by the browser and the route alike. The drop zone reads replies as text, so the platform's plain-text 413 no longer surfaces as *“Unexpected token 'R', "Request En"... is not valid JSON”*, and `uploadFailed` became a sentence that does not claim the upload failed when, after a timeout, it may have arrived.

**THE WITNESS, AND AN INSTRUMENT THAT FAILED.** The owner performed it in `/ar` on the production deployment's own address. The line under the drop zone read *“المدعوم: PDF, DOCX, PPTX, XLSX, TXT, MD (حتى 4 ميجابايت للملف الواحد)”*, and a 4,404,020-byte file was refused at once with *“حجم هذا الملف 4.2 ميجابايت، وأكبر حجم يمكنك رفعه هو 4 ميجابايت.”* The pass condition was zero requests to `/api/ingest`. The browser extension's network log returned nothing at all for that tab, not even the subject page's own requests, so its empty result was not counted as a zero. The page's own Resource Timing record was: it is complete since the sign-in page loaded (one navigation entry, 55 entries of a 250-entry buffer, no service worker), it lists the subject page's own requests, and it holds no request to `/api/ingest`.

**RULINGS ON THE REST OF #50.** Uploads stay synchronous, and (b2) is not needed. Decision 5 is no longer blocked by 1 and stays open; the Railway service has no size check of its own. The §5 duration measurement stays owed and owner-run, at the new ceiling. Decisions 2, 3, 4, 6 and 7 do not depend on size. **#111** opens, on the owner's ruling, for the route's refusals still in English under `/ar`, rather than folding them into #50.

**FLAGGED, NOT EDITED.** Three pointers into files #189 touched were already stale before it: #42's `DropZone.tsx:50` (the `as any` was on line 51, now 61), #69's `route.ts:199` (the guard was at 202, now 211) and #81's `limit-messages.ts:259-260` (the sentences were at 272-273, now 279-280). It does not close #17, #18, #49, #83 or #25. It does not touch #16 or #92, which this arc honoured and re-proved but did not change. And it does not add a §6 rule for the header fields, for the reason given in the next paragraph.

**ONE RULE CHANGE WAS CONSIDERED AND REFUSED, AND THE PREMISE BEHIND IT WAS WRONG.** The proposal was that §6's maintenance list fails to name *Last updated*, which is why nothing catches it going stale. **§6 does name it** — *“… and bump Last updated”* — so an eight-day-stale date is a compliance failure, not a rule gap, and the fix is to comply, which this PR does. The field §6 genuinely omits is *Active branch / last landed*, and the header's own 2026-09-11 post-mortem already says so: *“§6's maintenance list names *Last updated* and does not name this field, which is why nothing caught it.”* **That gap was already closed, and closed better than a rule would have:** both *Active branch / last landed* and *Main tip at authoring* were demoted to dated annotations that state they are not live pointers and name `git rev-parse origin/main` as the live answer. Adding a §6 rule to bump them would re-create the maintenance burden the demotion removed, and give the file two more fields that can go stale. **The rule stays as it is.**

### 2026-09-15 - the share card loses its band and the claim that justified it, the mark lands in cream by a rule rather than a preference, and both locales are witnessed by decoding what the domain serves

**Appends one block; edits two sentences in Section 4.** Register **#100** opened and closed for the card, and **row #99 corrected IN PLACE twice** - its status line said the card still remained, and its closing line said the colour was not ruled. Both were true when written and false within the day. The superseded text is quoted in both places, the pattern rows 94, 96 and 97 already set. This is the first docs entry in this arc that is not insertions-only, and the deletions are exactly those two superseded sentences.

**THE BAND'S JUSTIFICATION WAS FALSE AND THE COMMENT WENT WITH IT.** The source said the band was "what still reads as KnowFlow after the wordmark has stopped being legible" at a ~200px thumbnail. Measured: the wordmark is legible at 200 and at 120 without it. Deleting the band and keeping the comment would have preserved the claim in the one place a future reader would trust it.

**THE COLOUR WAS DECIDED BY A RULE THAT ALREADY EXISTED, AND THE AGENT REVERSED ITS OWN LEAN TO FIND IT.** `markSvg` refuses a default `fill` because there is no correct one - the mark has no colour of its own. It wears the foreground of whatever ground it sits on: `--accent-foreground` on the gold tile, `currentColor` inheriting `--foreground` in the app. The card sits on `--background`, so the mark wears `--foreground`, `#f5f0e8`. Gold was argued for on "the mark is the identity" and lost to the file that defines the mark.

**THE SURFACE THE OWNER NAMED AS DECIDING IT DID NOT DECIDE IT.** Rendered in both colours through the real pipeline, the delivered structure at 200px is identical to three decimals - 16x17px of ink, the gap clearing 100% to the ground, two connected components at every threshold - so cream is LOUDER (18.60:1 delivered against 9.26:1), not clearer. And cream does not keep gold uniquely on "Flow", because the 112x5 rule that survives is itself gold. Both premises of the question were measured and both were wrong.

**THE BEFORE-STATE WAS THE SHIPPED CARD, WHICH IS WHY THE FRESHNESS PROOF IS STRONGER THAN #165's.** The pre-build artifact was byte-identical to what production was serving, sha256 `274dc93941c8`, 20,568 bytes. A build starting 16:03:34Z wrote the new artifact at 16:03:50Z, 21,669 bytes, sha256 `d4e54a57370725bc`. New mtime, new hash, new size. `rm` was refused twice by the environment, so freshness rests on timestamp plus hash change rather than deletion.

**THE WITNESS IS BOTH LOCALES, DECODED, PLUS THE POINTER.** `/ar` and `/en` each return 200, 21,669 bytes, `Age: 0`, and sha256 equal to the build artifact **byte for byte**. Decoded: IHDR 1200x630 RGBA8 non-interlaced, corner `#14110d`, mark centre `#f5f0e8`, ink bbox `x[549..651] y[107..209]` = 103x103px against a derived 103.2px, 2,742 ink pixels, and **two connected components** sized 2,094 and 648. Both pages carry `og:image` and `twitter:image` at 1200x630 with `twitter:card` still `summary_large_image`.

**A CLAIM FROM THE PREVIOUS SESSION IS CORRECTED RATHER THAN LEFT STANDING.** The `og:image` query hash was called content-addressed. It is not: it moved with this change (`?d5f1e675ae952e5f` to `?f5a4c9693edca08c`), but a local build of identical source emitted `?d9c1dfe555ac83f0` while producing byte-identical images, and both locales share one hash within a build. The hash is per-module and build-specific. What survives is the part the cache argument needs - the URL changes when the card changes - not the stronger claim that it changes only then.

**WHAT THIS ENTRY REFUSES TO CLAIM.** No messaging client's render has been seen and none can be from here. The claim is bounded to the bytes served and the tags pointing at them. Their page-level preview cache may still hold the old card; a manual re-scrape is the available lever and was not measured this session.

### 2026-09-15 - the mark reaches the home-screen tile, a ruling made from a picture is overruled by a measurement, and the shipped tile is witnessed by decoding what the domain serves

**Appends, deletes nothing.** Register **#99** opened in Section 4 in this same PR, folded rather than split - the shape #162 settled. It opens **OPEN and does not close**: the mark holds one of the three surfaces it is meant to hold.

**A RULING MADE FROM A PICTURE WAS OVERRULED BY A MEASUREMENT, AND THE REGISTER SAYS SO IN THOSE WORDS.** The standing reading was that the chevron and the diamond spoke two different geometric languages, and that the contrast was a feature. The paths disagree: chevron arms at `atan(42/40)` = **46.40 degrees**, diamond edges at `atan(14/14)` = **45.00**. **1.4 degrees is sub-pixel at every size this ships at**, so the described fault does not exist and the two elements share one idiom. What separates the diamond is the **10.00-unit gap** - a distance, not a difference of language. The owner asked for this to be recorded as an overruling rather than softened, and it is the second such correction in one week.

**THE HOME-SCREEN TILE WAS TAKEN FIRST BECAUSE IT IS THE ONE SURFACE NOBODY HERE GETS TO DESIGN.** Its ground, its size and its mask are fixed by iOS. The **125px** artwork size is therefore derived and not picked: the farthest ink sits **56.57** units from centre, so at 125 the mark spans a **141.4px diameter inside a 180px tile**, and simulating the superellipse mask (n=5) puts **zero** ink pixels outside it. The mask simulation was not requested; it is the reason the number is defensible rather than plausible.

**THE PRODUCT NOW CARRIES TWO MARKS, BY WRITTEN DECISION RATHER THAN DRIFT.** A 32px favicon is shown at **16** in a tab strip - about 14px of ink, demanding a **12-to-14-unit** gap to keep two elements apart, three quarters of the mark's own arm width. At the sizes `apple-icon` is seen at, the same measurement gives a floor of **4**. So `icon.tsx` keeps the letter K, which was drawn for that size and measurably beats the mark there.

**A GREEN BUILD IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR THIS CHANGE AND WAS NOT USED AS ANY.** Satori drops SVG it cannot handle **silently** - `tsc` passes, the route prerenders, and a blank gold tile ships. The control is a negative one: an SVG whose only content is an unresolvable external reference renders **byte-identical to an empty tile**, sha256 `4e56328371fffb9c`. The artwork is therefore filled paths only, carrying none of `<style>`, `class`, `currentColor`, `<use>`, `<image>`, `<text>`, external references, filters, masks, clip-paths, gradients, patterns, opacity or `stroke`.

**THE WITNESS IS THE DECODED BYTES OF WHAT THE DOMAIN SERVES, NOT A STATUS LINE.** `GET https://tryknowflow.com/apple-icon` returns **200**, `image/png`, **1,460 bytes**, `X-Matched-Path: /apple-icon` - the header that matters, because this route was a **404 before #159**, `/` redirects to `/ar`, and only a deployed request exposes the locale router's exclusion. Decoded: IHDR **180x180** RGBA8 non-interlaced, corner `#d4a548` at **alpha 255** (a transparent tile is composited onto black by iOS), **2,717 ink pixels**, block **101x101** at `x[40..140] y[40..140]` centred on 90,90, and **two connected components at 8-connectivity**. **The component count is the load-bearing number:** the letter K is one component, so two is what proves the mark shipped and that the 10-unit gap survived rasterisation at the tile's real size.

**A SIMPLIFICATION FELL OUT OF THE SAME CHANGE.** With the K gone there is no text on this route, and Satori renders it with **no `fonts` option at all** - verified byte-identical output with and without. That deletes the `process.cwd()` read `loadBrandFont` performs, so `brand.ts`'s warning about these routes having to stay prerendered no longer applies to this one. `icon.tsx` and the share card still carry type and still carry the font.

**THE GEOMETRY IS A MODULE AND NOT A COMMITTED `.svg`, FOR A REASON WORTH NOT RE-PROPOSING.** A client component cannot read the filesystem, so a `.svg` would feed the image routes and nothing else, and the in-app mark would become a second hand copy of the same geometry - the failure `brand.ts` already names for the palette, with no check behind it. Colour is a required parameter rather than artwork: `BRAND.onGold` `#14110d` on `BRAND.gold` `#d4a548` at **8.31:1**, interpolated at render time, and `currentColor` must never reach Satori.

### 2026-09-14 - the six theme-only tokens get an unthemed ground, and the inertness claim is held against the served bundle rather than the source

**Appends, deletes nothing.** Register **#98** opened and closed in Section 4 in this same PR, folded rather than split - the shape #162 settled.

**A FAILURE CLASS CLOSED BEFORE IT COULD FIRE.** `--accent`, `--accent-foreground`, `--accent-hover`, `--accent-subtle`, `--faint` and `--raised` existed only inside the two `[data-theme]` blocks. Outside a themed subtree they are UNDEFINED, and a declaration referencing an undefined variable is dropped at computed-value time: green build, page renders, styling gone. Nothing used them outside a theme, so nothing was broken - this is cover for the moment landing and login are themed.

**THE VALUES DEGRADE INTO `:root`'s OWN PALETTE RATHER THAN IMPORTING ANOTHER.** The four accent tokens alias the primary family, `--raised` aliases `--muted`, and `--faint` is the only new value - derived, not picked, as `--muted-foreground` lightened along its own hue, landing at 3.23:1 between the dark theme's 3.25 and the light theme's 3.18.

**ONE OF MY OWN ARGUMENTS WAS THROWN OUT IN THE OPEN.** I first rejected the light-theme values on contrast. That was wrong: light `--raised` sits 1.11:1 against `#f7faf9` and this palette's own `--muted` step is 1.08:1, so the step is comparable and the ratio carries no rejection. The rejection rests on hue, and on a second identity colour appearing on emerald pages.

**A GREEN CHECK IS NOT EVIDENCE FOR A CSS CHANGE, AND WAS NOT USED AS ANY.** An undefined variable passes the build by construction - that IS the defect. Three measurements instead: a parse diff of every scope; a real browser cascade returning empty change sets inside both themed subtrees, with the before-state showing `var(--accent)` resolving to `rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)`; and then the same claim held against **what is actually served**, which the first two could not do because they used source and production ships minified.

**THE LIVE READ IS THE ONE THAT EARNED ITS PLACE.** `[data-theme=dark]` sha256 `79fe4a21cbd8e63f` and `[data-theme=light]` sha256 `7224fcb625419b66` are **byte-identical before and after** in the served bundle, while `:root` went 19 to 25 vars. It also settled something no source reading could: the minifier does **not** inline `var()` inside custom properties, so `--accent:var(--primary)` survives verbatim and the aliasing is live rather than frozen at build time. Read with the selector unquoted, because the minifier strips the quotes - the trap already recorded twice in this file.

### 2026-09-13 - the citation header was decoding UTF-8 as Latin-1, one expression fixed it, and the decoder is witnessed in the shipped bundle while the render is not

**Appends, deletes nothing.** Register **#97** is opened in Section 4 in the same PR as this entry - deliberately NOT the #159/#160 shape, where the row already existed and the docs PR corrected it. Here the row IS the docs PR, so splitting the changelog into a third round trip would have bought nothing.

**THE FIRST REAL QUESTION EVER ASKED THROUGH THE NEW ANSWER PATH FOUND A DEFECT THAT WAS NOT IN THE ANSWER PATH.** The answer was correct, the arithmetic was shown, retrieval returned eight citations and the 600-token cap did not truncate. The citation labels were mojibake.

**ONE EXPRESSION, AND THE SERVER WAS NEVER WRONG.** `Buffer.from(JSON.stringify(citations)).toString('base64')` defaults to UTF-8 and always did. The client called `atob(header)` and handed the result to `JSON.parse`. **`atob` returns a BINARY STRING - one character per byte, each in U+0000..U+00FF - which is Latin-1 by definition**, so every non-ASCII character crossed the `X-Citations` header as several bytes and arrived as that many characters. **Storage and the database are innocent**; the corruption lived in exactly one transport hop, which is why the fix is four lines of code and nothing upstream moved.

**IT SURVIVED BECAUSE THE TWO BRANCHES ON THAT LINE DISAGREED WITH EACH OTHER.** The `Buffer.from(header, 'base64').toString()` fallback beside it defaults to UTF-8 and was always correct, so the bug was **browser-only** and no server-side test could have reproduced it. It took a phone.

**THE BUG REPORT UNDERSTATED THE BLAST RADIUS AND THE ROW CORRECTS IT.** It was never only Arabic. UTF-8 and Latin-1 coincide exactly below U+0080, so ASCII was untouched; **everything above it was corrupted** - accented Latin, Cyrillic, Hebrew, CJK and emoji alike.

**PROVED BEFORE THE PR OPENED, WITHOUT THE APP, WITHOUT THE NETWORK AND WITHOUT SPENDING, AND TIED TO THE SHIPPED SOURCE RATHER THAN RETYPED.** The harness asserts both expressions present in the files and the old `atob` decode absent, then round-trips six filename shapes. ASCII came back byte-identical old versus new, so nothing regressed; a filename outside the BMP round-tripped, because the payload carries UTF-8 bytes and `TextDecoder` reconstructs the surrogate pair.

**MERGED `ece215d` 23:31:46 UTC, Vercel Production `6427785115` success, served build id `Nlmv_Qff9W5kw4qbRTfU_` -> `i9sgRK143KxJtUSH57jCz`.**

**AND THE DECODER ITSELF IS WITNESSED ON PRODUCTION WITHOUT ASKING A QUESTION** - a thing worth recording because it was not obvious it could be done. The agent route's client chunk (`page-2cafc0688189a924.js`, the only fetched script containing `X-Citations`) tests POSITIVE for `Uint8Array.from(atob(`, `TextDecoder` and `charCodeAt`, and NEGATIVE for the old `JSON.parse(...atob(` shape. **That is a witness of the shipped CODE, not of the RENDER, and the row does not blur the two.**

**STILL UNWITNESSED, STATED SEPARATELY FROM EVERYTHING ABOVE:** no corrected Arabic pill has been seen on a real answer, because that needs a streamed response - a paid call and a production write. **The cheapest occasion is the next question paid for anyway**, which already owes a `layout-shift` trace for the streaming-instability item; one question discharges both. Also not claimed: the lone-surrogate `U+FFFD` replacement is pre-existing SERVER behaviour, and the 2,232-byte header for eight citations is an observation, not a defect.

### 2026-09-13 - the two levers with no quality cost are cut, the app starts recording what a question actually costs, and the embedding switch is refused a place in the same PR

**PR opened, NOT MERGED.** Registers **#94** (this change) and **#95** (the voyage-4 migration spec) opened.

**THE POINT OF THIS PR IS THE LOG LINE, NOT THE SAVING.** The app read `response.usage` nowhere, so every cost figure this project has quoted — including the ones the price was set on yesterday — came from source constants and an assumed answer length. One structured `kf-usage` line per answered question now carries input/output/cache tokens, `stop_reason`, a truncation flag and the USD cost. No question or answer text is logged.

**LEVER 1** cuts `max_tokens` 2048 → 600, because output is priced 5× input and the cap is the tail. **LEVER 2** puts a 1,200-token budget on replayed history, which previously had a message count and no size bound at all. Projected: worst case **$0.02493 → $0.00691**, spread **4.3× → 1.28×**.

**THE ESTIMATOR IS SCRIPT-AWARE AND WAS TESTED BY RUNNING THE SHIPPED FUNCTION**, not a replica: English 3.92 chars/token, Arabic 2.09. A single chars/4 rule would have under-counted Arabic by more than half and blown the budget for the audience the budget exists to protect.

**THE INERT CACHE MARKER IS REMOVED RATHER THAN REPAIRED**, and the reason is written into the file so it is not re-added: nothing stable in this request reaches Haiku 4.5's 4,096-token minimum, and Lever 2 now guarantees the history never will.

**LEVER 4 WAS REFUSED A PLACE IN THIS PR.** Query embedding is **0.1%** of a question's cost, so it cuts nothing this PR is for, while being the only lever needing a schema change, a dual-write, a production backfill and a cutover. voyage-4 is **also 1024-dimensional**, so the column silently accepts a mixed corpus — a dimension change would have thrown. #95 specifies the dual-column sequence that makes a mixed state unrepresentable.

**THE UNCOMFORTABLE PART, RECORDED BECAUSE IT GOVERNS EVERYTHING ABOVE:** register #91 puts the embedding key's last use at **2026-08-10**, now 34 days ago. This PR installs a measurement that produces **nothing until somebody asks a question**. The blocker on pricing is no longer cost arithmetic — it is that there is no traffic to measure.

**BOTH POSSIBLE REGRESSIONS DISPROVED.** The Latin terminal line still resolves to `ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, …`, so the terminal aesthetic survived the scoping. `/en`'s three `tracking-tight` elements still compute `-0.6px / -1.2px / -0.5px`, so the Latin design is untouched; `/ar`'s three now compute `normal`, where they measured `-0.6px / -1.2px / -0.5px` on production before the merge.

**ONE BAD READ, CAUGHT AND RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY FIXED.** A first grep of the served CSS reported **0** occurrences of the RTL rule and would have read as a failed deploy. The minifier strips the attribute quotes — the bundle carries `[dir=rtl] .tracking-tight,[dir=rtl] .tracking-tighter{letter-spacing:normal}`, 1 occurrence against 0 in the old bundle. The pattern was wrong, not the CSS. This is the fourth time a tool has answered a question other than the one being asked; it is written down so the fifth is cheaper.

**THE RESIDUAL IS NAMED, NOT INFERRED.** The `MessageBubble` arm is shipped and **unwitnessed**. All 8 existing conversations on the live account were opened — read-only, confirmed from source before navigating (`KBSelector.tsx:36-56` performs no write on load) — and **none contains a single `<code>` element**, so there is nothing on production to measure. Producing one would mean sending a question, which writes to the production database, and that was refused rather than rationalised. The witness owed is the next real chat answer containing inline code.

### 2026-09-12 - Arabic on the landing hero was rendering in a fallback face, 23 Latin-default tracking values stop reaching /ar, and two of the four publishing blockers stop misdescribing the code

**Three corrections, one PR, no decision required from anybody.** Register **#92** opened; **#48** and **#49** corrected in place, each quoting the wording it supersedes. **No locale file is touched**, so there is no string-parity count to hand-check; `tsc` and `db-types` remain the only gates, and no build or deploy configuration is altered.

**THE DEFECT WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSERTED.** Read from production before any edit: the hero terminal’s Arabic resolved to `ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Monaco, Consolas, "Liberation Mono", "Courier New", monospace` — **Rubik nowhere in the stack** — while `document.fonts.check()` confirmed Rubik was **loaded and covering those glyphs**. The face was there and unused. Widths at identical size: **315.73px rendered, 245.49px in Rubik**, 207.87px in generic monospace. That 28% gap is the before-witness, and the after-state target is rendered == Rubik.

**TWO FIXES, NOT ONE, AND THE ASYMMETRY IS THE POINT.** The hero string is fixed and ours, so it is **scoped** to `font-sans` — deterministic everywhere. `MessageBubble`’s inline `<code>` holds arbitrary model output, so it **drops `font-mono`** instead. A theme `mono` stack was considered and rejected in writing: Rubik before the generic costs Latin its monospace on Android, the Phase-8 target; Rubik after it lets Windows’ Courier New — which carries Arabic — win instead of ours. A fallback stack is a guess about other people’s machines; scoping is not.

**23 TRACKING VALUES RETIRED BY TWO SELECTORS.** `tracking-tight` ×23 across 15 files (`tracking-tighter` ×0), stock `-0.025em`, no `letterSpacing` key in the theme — a Latin display default nobody chose, crowding a joined script. One descendant-qualified rule in `globals.css` outranks the utility 0,2,0 to 0,1,0 with no `!important`, no component churn, and `/en` unchanged. Accepted and written down rather than discovered later: it also neutralises Latin runs inside `dir="ltr"` subtrees on `/ar`.

**A FALSE COMMENT CORRECTED WHILE STANDING IN FRONT OF IT.** `globals.css` claimed a `.dark` block would override the tokens *“with NO utility changes”*. **43 colour utilities across 18 files bypass the tokens entirely**, 36 of them the danger/error family that sits on delete-account confirmation and form validation — on a dark ground `bg-red-50` is a near-white block and `text-red-700` is unreadable. The claim is replaced with the count. **No palette, no colour system and no dark work ships here**; the comment simply stops asserting that dark is free.

**CAIRO IS CLOSED, NOT DEFERRED.** Google serves Rubik’s **arabic** subset at `font-weight: 300 900` — identical to its latin subset — and the codebase uses only four weights (`font-medium` ×61, `font-semibold` ×54, `font-bold` ×27, `font-normal` ×3), leaving 300, 800 and 900 already loaded and unused. The weight hierarchy the new design rests on needs no font change, so `layout.tsx:9–11` is never reversed and **#85**’s warning never has to be discharged.

**NOT MERGED AND NOT WITNESSED ON PRODUCTION AT THE TIME OF WRITING.** A green deploy is not a witness; the after-measurement is taken against the PR’s preview deployment, and the production witness is owed at merge.

### 2026-09-12 - PR #149 merged and the privacy rewrite witnessed on production in both locales

It merged legally because `main`'s protection sets `required_approving_review_count: 0`; GitHub also forbids self-approval, so on a single-account repo that approval could never have existed as an artifact. The authorisation was the instruction, not a review.
