### 2026-09-12 - student notes were inside a vendor model-training grant, the grant is closed by an action in the live dashboard, and the privacy policy stops being false about where the text goes (#90, #91 opened; #18, #45, #61 corrected)

(**Four files and the record**: `privacy/page.tsx`, `dashboard/settings/page.tsx`, and one new key in each locale. No route, no schema, no migration, no dependency, no workflow, no production write.

**THE FINDING, AND IT WAS NOT A WORDING PROBLEM.** `services/ingestion/main.py:134` posts the **chunked text of every uploaded document** and **every question a student types** to `api.voyageai.com`. Voyage’s published default is that customer data is stored and used for model training unless an account-level opt-out is enabled, and the account’s own Terms of Service page read **“Opted In”**. The live privacy policy, meanwhile, said uploads were *“only accessible to you and the AI agents you explicitly authorize”*. **Neither Voyage nor the phrase sub-processor had ever appeared in this repository in any privacy sense.** It was found by deriving the vendor list from three independent sources — env-var names, dependency manifests, and every outbound call in `src/` and `services/` — rather than by asking for one, which matters because the owner could not place Voyage from memory: `VOYAGE_API_KEY` exists **only** under `services/`, invisible from the Next.js app.

**CLOSED GOING FORWARD BY ACTION, 2026-09-12, all read back on screen (`owner-attested`):** billing set to a **personal account**, country Morocco, billing email `support@tryknowflow.com`; card added and set default; a budget alert at **5 USD**; and the toggle flipped **Opted In → Opted Out**, confirming *“we will not save your data”*. Register **#90** carries it.

**THE COST OBJECTION WAS MEASURED AND DISMISSED, AND I RULED AGAINST WAITING.** The advice was to hold the toggle until Voyage confirmed the free allowance survived, because *the difference between zero and a permanent bill is too large to assume*. **Right as a rule, wrong here, and the numbers are why.** The account’s own Free Token page shows **`voyage-3-large` — the model pinned at `main.py:19` — with a remaining free allowance of ZERO**, matching the pricing page, which prices it at **$0.18/M with no free allowance** while the 200M free tokens attach to other models. **There was nothing to void.** And the spend is noise: ×1.143 chunk overhead at $0.18/M gives **$0.0014 per document once**, **$0.0000054 per question**, a **Free lifetime ceiling of $0.07 EVER**, a **Pro lifetime ceiling of $14.40 EVER** (#80(c)’s 50×200), and **$0.32/month** at Pro’s 2,000/day question cap — **0.053% of #80’s $615/month figure.** **The asymmetry ran the opposite way to the advice:** waiting cost student content entering a grant the toggle can never retract (Section 3 is forward-only), and acting cost cents. **Embedding does not enter the price decision at any tier**, which is the answer to whether this changes #81.

**THE PAGE NOW DESCRIBES WHAT ACTUALLY HAPPENS.** Removed: *“Your documents are stored securely and are only accessible to you and the AI agents you explicitly authorize”* (false), *“We use industry-standard security measures”* (unverifiable, and removed under the same rule that forbade writing new claims of that shape), and the last of **#18**’s retired vocabulary on this page — *knowledge bases*, *intelligent agents*, *documents* — each now grepping **0**. Added: a **Who We Share It With** section naming all eight providers in a student’s words, with the sentence **“Anthropic and Voyage AI receive the text of your materials and your questions. They do not receive your name or your email address”**, which is established from the code — the Voyage payload is `input`/`model`/`input_type`/`output_dimension` and nothing else, and no identifier appears in any model prompt. Added: Apple 5.1.1(i)’s **equivalence confirmation**, and a **How Long We Keep It** section. **Data Storage and Security now says only what was read from the migrations** — the `documents` bucket is `public = false` (`002_storage.sql:2`), storage policies key on `auth.uid()::text = (string_to_array(name, '/'))[1]`, and row-level security with `auth.uid()` covers `profiles`, `knowledge_bases`, `documents`, `conversations`, `messages` and `chunks`. **Nothing claims encryption or auditing**, because neither was read.

**THE VOYAGE SENTENCE IS TIME-SCOPED, AND THE DATE IS LOAD-BEARING.** It reads *“content sent from 12 September 2026 onward is deleted after it has been processed and is not used to improve their models”*. **Unscoped, that sentence would be false for anyone who uploaded earlier** — Section 3 makes the opt-out forward-only — and publishing a false reassurance is the exact defect this change exists to remove. The fuller disclosure about pre-opt-out content was considered and not written, on the ground that it is unbounded until Voyage answers; **that is the owner’s call to escalate and #90 records it as open, not settled.**

**RETENTION, RE-CHECKED AGAINST THE SCHEMA AND THE DELETION PATH RATHER THAN CARRIED FROM THE DRAFT.** No `pg_cron`, no `cron.schedule`, no TTL and no expiry column exists in any of the 16 migrations, so **nothing is ever deleted automatically**; `orchestrate.ts` sweeps `{userId}/` storage, erases the waitlist row, cancels Paddle, then one `deleteUser` whose cascade takes eleven tables. **The page names what survives**, because `account_deletion_orphans` holds `user_id` and `email` and is **deliberately not a foreign key** — the migration says CASCADE *“would erase the incident on recovery”* — and because the vendors keep their own records.

**THE APPLE LINK, AND THE ONE ARABIC STRING IN THIS CHANGE.** `git grep` found **exactly one** link to `/privacy` in the entire product — the marketing footer — so a signed-in student had no path to it and the Phase-8 Capacitor shell would have had none. A link now sits in `dashboard/settings/page.tsx`. Its label is user-facing, so it needed both locales: `dashboard.settings.privacyPolicy` is **“Privacy Policy”** and **“سياسة الخصوصية”** (`U+0633 U+064A U+0627 U+0633 U+0629 U+0020 U+0627 U+0644 U+062E U+0635 U+0648 U+0635 U+064A U+0629`), which reuses the existing `footer.privacy` token `الخصوصية` rather than inventing a term. **Parity hand-counted, since `tsc` proves keys and not wording: 12 settings keys in each locale, same names, same order.** The three legal pages stay hardcoded English — **#17**, open, not translated here.

**WHAT STAYS OPEN, ALL THREE RECORDED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED.** **(a)** Content sent **before** 2026-09-12 remains inside the grant, reachable only via `legal@voyageai.com`; the strongest argument is the **DPA**, which is auto-incorporated and bars using Customer Personal Data *“for any purpose other than providing the Services”* — but its precedence over the training grant is **not stated**. **(b)** The 5 USD limit is an **alert, not a cap**. **(c)** The key has **never been rotated** despite July’s public exposure — **#45** corrected today, because that row’s remediation rotated `INGESTION_TOKEN` and left `VOYAGE_API_KEY` untouched, and the account page shows the **same key from 2026-05-01 still active**.

**AND ONE MEASUREMENT ARRIVED FOR FREE THAT #80 HAS WANTED SINCE IT OPENED — REGISTER #91.** That key was **last used 2026-08-10**. Every Ask embeds a query and every upload embeds a document, so **no student has asked a question or uploaded a material in 33 days.** #80 records account usage as *“UNKNOWN … establishing it needs a Supabase credential this session deliberately did not take”*; this is that number arriving from a third party’s timestamp at no cost. **It is a lower bound on inactivity, not a count of accounts**, and it cuts both ways: it shrinks every cost figure in #80 to the hypothetical, and it makes #81’s price decision harder, because there is still no usage distribution to set one from.

Scope: `src/app/[locale]/privacy/page.tsx`, `src/app/[locale]/dashboard/settings/page.tsx`, `src/lib/i18n/locales/{en,ar}.ts`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`. **ZERO routes, zero schema, zero migration, zero dependency, zero workflow; no production write, no secret or env read, no Supabase or Paddle credential, no key rotation, no DNS change.** The Vercel preview was **not** read — it 302s to SSO. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 rows **#18**, **#45** and **#61** are edited in place, each quoting what it supersedes, and **#90** and **#91** are new.)

### 2026-09-12 - the privacy policy names the app Play requires it to name, and this row's Play clearance is corrected a SECOND time by writing the three-condition test out instead of summarising it (#61)

(**One page file and the record.** `src/app/[locale]/privacy/page.tsx`, plus §4 row **#61**. No route, no schema, no dependency, no workflow, no DNS change, no production write, no credential.

**THE DEFECT WAS FOUND IN THIS REGISTER’S OWN EDIT, ONE DAY OLD.** Yesterday’s entry cleared Play’s deletion-resource requirement with *“`/en/privacy` and `/ar/privacy` are public and now carry a working request route — **present**”*. **That checked one of three conditions and called the requirement met.** Read from Play’s own page on 2026-09-12, the weblink must be **functional**, **relevant in scope** (*“the pathway to request account deletion should be prominently featured and easily discoverable on the page”*) **and reference the app or developer name** (*“as it appears on your store listing in Google Play”*). **Two of the three failed**, measured on the page as served: `KnowFlow` appeared **zero times in the rendered `<body>`** of `/en/privacy` — five times in `<head>` only, and a `<title>` is not the policy naming the app — and the deletion pathway sat in the second paragraph under a heading reading *“Your Rights”*, with **no heading naming deletion anywhere on the page**.

**WHAT SHIPPED, AND WHY BOTH HALVES WENT TOGETHER.** An opening paragraph now reads: *“This Privacy Policy applies to KnowFlow, the study service at tryknowflow.com. KnowFlow is operated by an independent developer, and the contact address given below reaches the person responsible for it.”* And the deletion pathway gained its own heading, *“Deleting Your Account and Data”*, lifting it out from under *“Your Rights”*. **They are two conditions of ONE requirement on ONE page; shipping half would have earned #61 a THIRD correction**, which is the pattern this entry exists to stop rather than continue. **A side effect worth naming:** the page used **we** six times and never once said who *we* was — it does now.

**THE MISSING LEGAL ENTITY IS NOT A BLOCKER, AND THAT IS A READING OF THE POLICY, NOT A CONVENIENCE.** Play’s User Data policy: *“The entity (for example, developer, company) named in the app’s Google Play store listing must appear in the privacy policy **or** the app must be named in the privacy policy.”* **It is an OR**, so naming the app clears it and an owner with no registered company is not blocked. **No company name, no personal name and no postal address was invented** — none exists to state truthfully, and Play does not ask for one here. The same policy also wants *“developer information and a privacy point of contact”*: the contact is `support@tryknowflow.com`, twice on the page; the identity is answered as far as it truthfully can be, *operated by an independent developer*, and no further.

**TWO FORWARD CONSTRAINTS ON PHASE 10, because the page is live and the listing does not exist yet.** **(1)** The rule is that the policy match the name *“as it appears on your store listing”* — the policy now says **KnowFlow** and so does `SITE_NAME` (`src/lib/site.ts`), so **the listing must be KnowFlow or begin with it**; the page shipped first and the listing has to match it, not the reverse. **(2)** Play publicly displays a **developer name** chosen when the account is created, and until that account exists there is no verified name to publish — **publishing a guess now would create the very contradiction (1) prevents**. Reconcile the two in the same sitting the account is made.

**WHAT IS EXPLICITLY NOT A PLAY PROBLEM, RULED FROM THE POLICY TEXT.** The three legal pages serve **English inside `dir="rtl"`** on `/ar` — measured on production 2026-09-12, `/ar/privacy` renders **1,246 Latin characters to 21 Arabic** — on a product whose routing default is **Arabic** (`src/middleware.ts:5` `defaultLocale = 'ar'`; `getLocale` sends every non-`en` browser to `/ar`). **Play’s deletion-resource requirements contain no sentence about language, localisation, translation or locale.** So it is register **#17**’s scope and a **real market defect** for the Moroccan and Gulf students this product is aimed at — **and it is not a publishing blocker.** Asserting otherwise would be the mirror of the error this entry corrects: claiming a store requirement the store does not state.

**SCOPE CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED.** `terms/page.tsx` and `refund/page.tsx` are **not reached** — Play’s naming rule attaches to the privacy policy and the deletion resource, and neither page is either — and it is **moot regardless**, because both already name the app (`terms` ×3, `refund` ×1, counted 2026-09-12). *Apple’s guidelines were not fetched for this change, so an equivalent Apple naming rule is **unchecked rather than cleared**.* **`DeleteAccountCard` was re-verified as WIRED, not merely present** — imported and rendered at `src/app/[locale]/dashboard/settings/page.tsx:84` — because this row has now been wrong twice about things it asserted without re-reading.

**NO ARABIC COUNTERPART, STATED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `tsc` proves key parity and not wording parity, so every locale change this week was hand-counted. **There is nothing to count here:** the privacy page is hardcoded English throughout (register **#17**, open — it takes `locale` only to set `dir`), the dictionaries hold no key for it, and **no Arabic string was added, changed or removed**, so there is none to print with codepoints. Translating it is #17’s branch and was not started.

Scope: `src/app/[locale]/privacy/page.tsx` and `docs/PROGRESS.md`. **ZERO i18n files, zero routes, zero schema, zero migration, zero dependency, zero workflow; no DNS change, no production write, no secret or env read, no Supabase or Paddle credential; no test mail sent.** The Vercel preview was **not** read — it 302s to SSO. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 row **#61** is edited in place, quoting **both** of its superseded clearances.)

### 2026-09-11 - a domain belonging to another company comes off the legal pages, every public address becomes one, and the refund page stops promising a window it was never able to enforce (#18 part-closed; #61 corrected; #89 opened; #81 gains the deferral)

(**Four page files and the record.** `privacy`, `terms`, `contact`, `refund`. No route, no schema, no dependency, no workflow, no DNS change, no production write, no credential.

**THE HEADLINE IS NOT A TYPO. `knowflow.ai` IS ANOTHER COMPANY.** Measured from public DNS and registry: registered **2023-07-05** at GoDaddy (this product’s `tryknowflow.com`: **2026-04-12**, Name.com, Vercel nameservers), registrant privacy-shielded, **no MX and no TXT of any kind**, serving a live Portuguese page for a different “KnowFlow” — *“Lançamento em breve … Copyright © 2025 KnowFlow – Todos os direitos reservados”*. **The privacy policy’s deletion route and the terms’ legal contact were addresses on it.** Mail to them bounces (*inference — no MX, so delivery falls back to A records serving a website; no SMTP probe was made, deliberately*), and if that company ever adds MX, a student’s deletion request or legal notice reaches a stranger. **Occurrence sweep before the edit: three source lines and nothing else** — `privacy/page.tsx:25`, `:28`, `terms/page.tsx:32`; **zero** in metadata, `README.md`, any config or any store-facing file, and none in either locale dictionary. **After: `git grep knowflow.ai -- src` returns zero.** The only remaining mentions are the two register rows that record the defect, which is what the record is for.

**EVERY PUBLIC ADDRESS IS NOW ONE STRING.** `git grep` over `src` returns **eight occurrences of `support@tryknowflow.com` and no other address at all**: `privacy/page.tsx:25` (href + text) and `:28`, `terms/page.tsx:32`, `contact/page.tsx:17` (href + text), `refund/page.tsx:17` and `:23`. `contact`’s `hello@` went with them. **Nothing breaks, and that was checked rather than assumed:** these strings are **rendered literals only** — no test, script, workflow or component reads them, `git grep` over `scripts`, `.github` and `src` for any reference to the three legal pages returns nothing, and **neither locale dictionary contains an email address**, so there is no key to keep in sync. **The local part was a clarity choice, not a technical one:** ImprovMX holds a catch-all `*@tryknowflow.com`, so every local part already received (**#89**).

**THE PARITY HOLE DOES NOT APPLY HERE, AND THAT IS WORTH STATING RATHER THAN ASSUMING.** `tsc` proves key parity and not wording parity, so every locale change this week was hand-counted. **This change has no Arabic counterpart to count:** the legal pages are hardcoded English throughout (register **#17**, still open, and `privacy`/`terms`/`refund` take `locale` only to set `dir`), and the dictionaries hold no address. **No Arabic string was added, changed or removed, so there is none to print.** Translating these pages is **#17**’s branch and was deliberately not started here.

**THE REFUND PAGE LOSES A WINDOW IT COULD NOT ENFORCE AND A ROUTE THAT LED NOWHERE USEFUL.** Superseded, quoted: *“We offer a 7-day refund window from the date of first charge. To request a refund, contact us at support@tryknowflow.com …”* and *“Section 3: No Refunds After 7 Days — After 7 days from the charge date, refunds will not be issued.”* **Both were defects regardless of any number, and the grounds are Paddle’s own published policy.** Paddle is merchant of record: it holds transactions *“non-refundable and non-exchangeable”* unless law requires otherwise, **may refund at its own discretion within 14 days**, and states that *“if local consumer protection laws or a Supplier of a Product provides you with additional or non-waivable rights, the highest level of rights will always apply”*. **So a seller policy can only ADD rights, never remove them** — which makes *“refunds will not be issued”* a representation about a third party’s behaviour that we are in no position to make. And Paddle **approves most refunds itself** (automatically when the account is verified, the amount is ≤ 400 USD and ≤ balance) with buyers directed to **paddle.net** — so the old page sent a student to an address that could not decide. **Section 2 now names Paddle as merchant of record, routes to `paddle.net`, offers to escalate on the student’s behalf, and links Paddle’s policy rather than restating its numbers, so it cannot go stale.** **Section 3’s denial is replaced by something true and already shipped:** cancel any time from Settings, future charges stop, Pro runs to the end of the paid period — the exact behaviour register **#70** proved (`effectiveFrom: 'next_billing_period'`, PR #106). **The “Last updated” dates on all three legal pages were bumped**, because a policy whose substance changed under an old date is itself a false statement; `refund` had read **April 2025**.

**THE RULING ON A VOLUNTARY WINDOW, BECAUSE ONE AGREED ANSWER WAS ASKED FOR AND NOT TWO VIEWS: NO WINDOW ON THE PAGE FOR NOW, AND THE OBJECTION IS ANSWERED RATHER THAN OVERRIDDEN.** The worry is that a page with no window reads as hesitancy against competitors advertising one. **What the comparators actually publish cuts the other way for a MONTHLY plan:** Coursera gives 14 days but **explicitly excludes monthly subscription charges**; Chegg’s 7-day window requires *“a good enough reason”*; Quizlet generally does not refund a missed cancellation. **And the decisive argument is sequencing, not philosophy:** a published window is a **binding grant** under Paddle’s highest-rights clause, so it is harder to retract than the price — and it would attach to a price **#81** has not settled and a free tier **#80** has not settled. **Hesitancy is silence; this page is not silent.** It now describes a mechanism — who decides, how to ask, that we escalate, and that cancelling is one click and preserves the paid period — which is more information than *“7 days”* ever carried. The choice between none, a short window and ~two weeks is recorded in **#81** with what each costs, **and the number is deliberately not proposed** — it is the owner’s, and it belongs with the price and the trial as one decision.

**REGISTER #61’s GOOGLE PLAY ARGUMENT IS CORRECTED, AND THE POSITION IS RE-STATED FROM SOURCE.** It read: *“Play’s superset also wants a web-link resource, **satisfied by the privacy page’s `privacy@knowflow.ai` route**”*. **A store-compliance claim rested on a mailbox at another company’s domain.** Where it stands now: *in-app deletion* — `DELETE` at `src/app/api/account/route.ts:32` with `DeleteAccountCard.tsx` rendering it, **present**; *web resource* — `/en/privacy` and `/ar/privacy` are public and now carry a request route that actually receives, **present**. **Not a new open item:** declaring that URL in the Play Console Data-safety form is a submission act already owned by §1’s **Phase 10** row.

**RECORDED, NOT FIXED — REGISTER #89, AND THE SECOND HALF IS A STANDING SECURITY ITEM.** The domain’s mail configuration had **no entry in this record at all**, which is how `knowflow.ai` survived on two legal pages for the life of the product. Measured from DNS: **ImprovMX receives** (free tier, $0, forwarding only, catch-all held by the owner) and **Resend sends** from the EU region — a correct split, not a defect. **Two stale Brevo records are not:** `_dmarc` is `p=none` with `rua=mailto:rua@dmarc.brevo.com`, an account `b1-verification-protocol.md:163` records as **expired 2026-05-19 with zero credits** — **the one signal that would show mail failing or being spoofed is posted to a dead address**; and `brevo1`/`brevo2._domainkey` still publish live keys, so **a lapsed third party can still send mail that passes DKIM as `tryknowflow.com`** on a domain that carries password-reset and signup-confirmation mail, with `p=none` telling receivers to do nothing. **Three deletions and one edit, all free, none performed here** — this session may not change DNS. **Do the `rua=` edit first:** it is the one that makes the next mail problem visible instead of silent.

Scope: `src/app/[locale]/{privacy,terms,contact,refund}/page.tsx` and `docs/PROGRESS.md`. **ZERO i18n files, zero routes, zero schema, zero migration, zero dependency, zero workflow; no DNS change, no production write, no secret or env read, no Supabase or Paddle credential; no test mail sent to any address.** The Vercel preview was **not** read — it 302s to SSO. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 rows **#18**, **#61** and **#81** are edited in place, each quoting what it supersedes, and **#89** is new.)

### 2026-09-11 - the landing page stops timing anything it cannot time, and the fix that closes register #20 is to delete a word rather than translate it (#20 closed; #17 corrected and STILL OPEN; #84 gains the one claim an edit cannot repair)

(**Seven strings across three files** — `src/app/[locale]/page.tsx`, `src/lib/i18n/locales/en.ts`, `src/lib/i18n/locales/ar.ts` — plus the record. No route, no schema, no dependency, no workflow, no production write, no credential.

**THE DEFECT, RESTATED FROM THE LAST ENTRY AND THEN ACTED ON: no timing claim on this page named the act it timed.** *“Ready”* appeared twice meaning two different things (ingestion, setup) and *“seconds”/“instantly”* a third (answering). Four claims were found; **three change, one stays.** <br>**(A)** `page.tsx:72` `[OK] Ready in 0.4s.` → **`[OK]`**. **(B)** `hero.disclaimer` loses its third clause: `“No credit card · Free to start · Ready in seconds”` → `“No credit card · Free to start”` (`en.ts:18`), and the Arabic the same (`ar.ts:20`). **(C)** `cta.note` loses its third clause: `“Free plan · No credit card · Set up in a minute”` → `“Free plan · No credit card”` (`en.ts:48`, `ar.ts:50`). **(D)** `howItWorks` step 3: `“…, instantly.”` → `“…, in seconds.”` (`en.ts:42`) and `“… فوراً.”` → `“… في ثوانٍ.”` (`ar.ts:44`). **LEFT ALONE:** `hero.title` *“Any question. In seconds.”* — it times **answering**, which is a streamed call and genuinely takes seconds, and `layout.tsx` derives `title`, `og:title` and `twitter:title` from it, so editing it would change the site’s search and social identity for no truth gain.

**(A) THE INSTRUCTION FOR THIS CHANGE WAS *“drop the number and localize it”*, AND THE SECOND HALF IS OVERTURNED, DELIBERATELY.** The mock’s container carries **`dir="ltr"` by design** (`page.tsx:70`) because it depicts a terminal, and the line above the status is an unlocalized shell command, `> upload ./biology-notes.pdf`. **The Arabic that already ships inside that block is the QUOTED USER INPUT on the next line** — `ask "ما الفرق …"` — content the student typed, which is exactly right and is the model the block already follows: **English tool verbs, Arabic user content.** `[OK] Ready` is **tool output**; translating only that word produces `[OK] جاهز` sitting above an English command, which is a half-translated shell and reads worse than an untranslated one. **So the line is reduced to `[OK]`** — the only form that is true (the upload succeeded), claim-free (no duration at all) and **language-free**, so register **#17**’s class stops applying to it rather than being converted into a new bidi surface someone has to maintain inside an LTR block. **Bidi was checked rather than waved at:** in an LTR paragraph a strong-RTL run renders correctly but trailing neutrals resolve to the paragraph direction, which is precisely the class of bug that would have been introduced by adding `جاهز.` there and would never have been caught by a gate.

**(B) AND (C) GO TOGETHER, AND THE REASON IS A GATE THAT SHIPPED THREE DAYS AGO.** These were treated as one contradiction — *“Ready in seconds”* against *“Set up in a minute”*, two numbers for one act on one page — and **in Arabic the contradiction is exact, not approximate: both used the identical word جاهز (“ready”)**, one with “in seconds” and one with “in a minute”. **But aligning them on a number would have been wrong, because BOTH are false.** `signup/page.tsx:50` states it outright: *“Confirm email is ON for this project, so GoTrue withholds the session”* — the signup confirmation gate shipped 2026-09-08 and was witnessed on production. **A student is not ready in seconds or in a minute; they are ready when an email they do not control arrives and they click it.** Neither figure is ours to promise. **So both clauses are DROPPED rather than aligned, and nothing is put in their place** — replacing a false timing claim with a different timing claim is how this set got to four members.

**(D) IS A WORD, NOT A NUMBER, AND ITS SENTENCE STILL CARRIES A CLAIM THIS CHANGE CANNOT REPAIR.** *“instantly”* is wrong about a streamed response and becomes *“in seconds”*, matching `hero.title`; the Arabic reuses `في ثوانٍ` **byte-identically** to the fragment already in `ar.ts:15`, so the two sentences now use one vocabulary rather than two. **The same sentence also promises *“the right information”*, and that is NOT touched here — recorded against register **#84** instead.** It is not known to be false; it is **unfalsifiable**, because #84 measures that rank and similarity are computed, streamed once in `X-Citations` and discarded, so retrieval quality has never been assessed at all. **The dependency therefore runs the unusual way round: the COPY fix is blocked on the DATA fix**, and editing the claim now would only swap one unverifiable promise for another. **It is the one claim left on the landing page this repository cannot defend.**

**THE SWEEP, SO THIS CLOSES A SET RATHER THAN THREE OF FOUR.** Both dictionaries and every public page file were searched for durations and immediacy (`second`, `minute`, `hour`, `instant`, `immediat`, `fast`, `quick`, `moment`, `real-time`, `right away`, and فور/ثوان/دقيق/لحظ/سريع/حالا). **Everything found is ruled, not left hanging.** **Changed:** A, B, C, D above. **Left, each for a stated reason:** `hero.title` (times answering; drives the metadata); `usecases[0]` *“Understand hard topics **fast**”* (describes the student’s outcome, not system latency — no measurable claim exists to be false); `pricing.checkout.unavailable` *“try again **in a moment**”* (an error-recovery instruction, not a performance claim); `auth.forgotRateLimited` *“wait about **a minute**”* (a rate-limit disclosure about GoTrue, hedged by *“about”*, and nobody here has read the configured interval — **named so the hedge is a decision rather than an accident**); `settings.deleteAccount.billingNote` *“cancelled **immediately**”* and `privacy/page.tsx:24` *“immediately cancels”* (both TRUE — `account-deletion/paddle.ts:159` passes `effectiveFrom: 'immediately'`, and in-app/legal pages are not marketing surfaces anyway).

**REGISTER #17 DOES NOT CLOSE, AND THAT WAS MEASURED BECAUSE THE BRIEF ASKED WHETHER IT SHOULD.** Read on `main` @ `e61fe9b`: **no `legal:` key exists** in `en.ts`; `terms/page.tsx` and `refund/page.tsx` reference `t.` **zero** times and `privacy/page.tsx` **once**; **“Unlock your knowledge” is still hardcoded** at `login/page.tsx:67` and `signup/page.tsx:93`. **Every instance #17 actually lists is still live.** What did change is that #17’s instance list was **incomplete**: the landing hero string it never named is now gone, and the row is corrected to say so. **A row does not close because a neighbouring row did.**

**THE PARITY HOLE FOUND ON 2026-09-11 WAS GUARDED BY HAND, AS IT NOW HAS TO BE.** `tsc` proves key parity and nothing about array length or wording (measured in the previous entry: English cut to three bullets, Arabic left at four, `tsc` exit 0). So both locales were counted and read: `hero` and `cta` carry the **same keys and the same number of `·` separators in both files after the edit** (hero 2 each, cta 1 each), and every changed Arabic string was printed with its Unicode codepoints and checked for Arabic-Indic digits, em/en dashes, tatweel, bidi control marks and NBSP — **none present**.

Scope: `src/app/[locale]/page.tsx`, `src/lib/i18n/locales/{en,ar}.ts`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`. **ZERO routes, zero schema, zero migration, zero dependency, zero workflow; no production write, no secret or env read, no Supabase or Paddle credential; the Vercel preview was NOT read — it 302s to SSO — and nothing here claims otherwise.** §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 rows **#20** (closed), **#17** (corrected, still open) and **#84** (extended) are edited in place, each quoting what it supersedes.)

### 2026-09-11 - the pricing page stops lying in three places, and the remedy this register prescribed for one of them would have published a number the project already intends to break (#81 part-closed; #2 corrected)

(**Six strings, two files, `src/lib/i18n/locales/{en,ar}.ts`.** No route, no schema, no dependency, no workflow, no production write, no credential. The record changes are §4 rows **#81** and **#2**, edited in place.

**THE THREE FALSE LINES, EXACTLY.** **(1)** Pro `“10 Subjects” → “Many more subjects”` (`en.ts:77`) and `“10 مواد” → “مواد أكثر بكثير”` (`ar.ts:79`). **(2)** Free `“10 materials” → “10 materials per subject”` (`en.ts:66`) and `“10 ملفات” → “10 ملفات لكل مادة”` (`ar.ts:68`). **(3)** Pro `“Priority support”` (`en.ts:80`) and `“دعم ذو أولوية”` (`ar.ts:82`) **deleted**.

**THE PRESCRIBED REMEDY FOR (1) WAS WRONG, AND THE RULE THAT CATCHES IT IS ONE THIS FILE ADOPTED THE DAY BEFORE.** Register #81 said the defect *“needs no pricing decision at all”* and implied the obvious repair: print the real **50**. §5, ruled 2026-09-10: *“Unsettled ceilings do not [publish], because publishing one publishes a number we already intend to break.”* **`knowledge_bases: 50` is an unsettled Pro ceiling**, and **#80(c)** holds it open *precisely because it multiplies the per-subject document cap into 50 × 200 = 10,000 documents*. Printing 50 would have swapped a false number for a doomed one and satisfied every reviewer. **So the Pro line became non-numeric, matching the two beside it.** **The app was already right and nobody had looked:** `dashboard.newKb.errorLimitFree` says *“Upgrade to Pro **for more**”* with no number, while `errorLimitPro` interpolates the real 50 — **a ceiling may be shown IN-APP to someone who has already hit it; it may not be PUBLISHED as a promise to someone deciding whether to pay.**

**(2) AND (3) WERE FOUND BY RE-READING THE BLOCK WHOLE, NOT THE LINE THE REGISTER NAMED, AND THEY ARE DIFFERENT SHAPES.** **(2) understates by 5×:** `checkDocumentLimit` filters `.eq('kb_id', kbId)` (`limits-server.ts:53`), so the cap is **per subject** — the app’s own wall says *“This subject already holds its limit of 10 materials”* (`limit-messages.ts:259-260`) — and a free student actually gets 10 × 5 = **50**. Free figures are settled and publish under §5, so it was **qualified, not renumbered**. **(3) is not a number at all:** there is **no support mechanism of any kind**. The contact form is deliberately inert (`contact/page.tsx:22-24`, register **#19**); the only paths are `mailto:hello@tryknowflow.com` and a GitHub link, **identical for free and Pro**; a `git grep` over tracked source finds no ticketing or helpdesk surface. **The false word was “priority”, not “support”** — `footer.support` links to `/contact`, so the channel exists and is honestly labelled. **A register row about NUMBER sync was structurally incapable of catching (3)**, which is why #2 now carries the conclusion that *“bump by hand if the limits ever change”* is **the wrong trigger**: not one of the three was caused by a limit changing, so that instruction would have fired **zero** times in three defects.

**PROVEN BY RUNNING IT, AND IT CONTRADICTS SOMETHING THE RECORD LEANS ON.** `ar.ts` is annotated `: Translation` and `Translation = typeof en`, and `en.ts` has **no `as const`** — so `features` infers **`string[]`, not a tuple**. **Measured before the real edit:** English Pro cut to three bullets, Arabic left at four, `npx tsc --noEmit` **exits 0**. The annotation guarantees **key parity, not array parity**: a locale could go on advertising “دعم ذو أولوية” after English stopped, with every gate green. Register **#1** calls that guarantee *“enforced by `ar.ts`’s `: Translation` annotation and therefore by `tsc`”* — true for keys, and this is its edge. Both locales were edited in the same commit and **counted by hand (4/4 free, 3/3 pro) because nothing else counts them.**

**RENDERING SETTLED FROM SOURCE, NOT FROM A SCREENSHOT.** Both cards are `flex flex-col`; both feature lists are `<ul class="… flex-1">` rendered by `.map((feature, idx) => <li key={idx}>…)` with **no index access, no length assumption and no fixed-height container**; the Free CTA follows the `flex-1` list and the Pro CTA sits in `<div class="mt-auto">`; the grid is `md:grid-cols-2` with default `align-items: stretch`. **So one fewer Pro bullet redistributes whitespace INSIDE the card and both CTAs stay bottom-aligned at equal card height.** Nothing else reads `features`.

**EVERY OTHER SURFACE WAS SWEPT, AND ONE REAL FINDING CAME BACK — A DIFFERENT FALSE CLAIM, DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED HERE.** `git grep` over **tracked** files for these three claims returns only the six strings themselves plus one code comment quoting the in-app message (`limits-server.ts:46`, correct). **Clean:** page metadata derives from `hero.title`/`hero.subtitle` and carries no pricing claim; `README.md:18` says only *“PRO tier with usage limits”*; the settings/cancel/delete strings say *“contact support”*, which is true because `/contact` exists; **no store-listing document exists in `docs/` yet**, which is exactly why these strings matter before Phase 10 copies them. **NOT CLEAN, AND RECORDED RATHER THAN FIXED because it is a different claim:** the landing hero still renders **`“[OK] Ready in 0.4s.”` hardcoded in English at `src/app/[locale]/page.tsx:72`** — register **#20**’s false performance claim, which is *also* outside the dictionary so it never localizes (**#17**’s class). **And it is not alone:** `hero.disclaimer` ends *“Ready in seconds”*, `hero.title` is *“In seconds.”* and `howItWorks` step 3 ends *“instantly”*. **#20 is specifically about INGESTION**, so only the hardcoded `0.4s` is plainly its defect; the other three describe ANSWERING and need a judgement about which act each names. **That is four strings across two locales, not one line** — recorded here so the next branch scopes from a count rather than a memory.

**THE COST OF THIS FIX, STATED RATHER THAN BURIED.** The Pro card now carries **no number at all** against a Free card with three exact figures, so a student comparing them cannot see what paying buys. **Accepted as temporary and deliberate** — honesty now outranks a number the project intends to break — and logged in **#81** as the price decision’s problem, with the sharp form of it written down: **there is currently NOT ONE settled, publishable Pro figure.** `knowledge_bases: 50`, `documents: 200` and `conversations_per_month: 2000` are all open under #80(b)/(c), and daily `query: 2000` is #80’s **$615/month** exposure. Whatever price is set must be set **together with at least one Pro ceiling the model can fund**, because that ceiling is what the card will finally be allowed to print.

**THE FREE DAILY CAP IS NOT PUBLISHED, AND THE REASON MATTERS MORE THAN THE DECISION.** The free card publishes *“100 conversations/month”* and is silent on the **10 questions/day** cap (`rate-limit.ts:42`, cut 30→10 by PR #141) — two different meters, and the tighter one is the one a student meets first. **The proposed reason for waiting was that the daily cap is planned to become monthly, so publishing now means editing twice. That reason is REJECTED:** this file has ruled twice (**#61** at nine days, **#74** at thirty-six hours) that a defect is not deferred for the convenience of a future edit, and adopting that argument here would quietly overturn both. **It is deferred on a different ground:** printing *“10 questions/day”* beside *“100 conversations/month”* without saying how the two meters relate **adds** confusion rather than removing it, and choosing which unit a student is told about is a product decision living inside **#80(a)**’s open monthly-cap work. It is an **omission, not a contradiction**, and it is now written in **#2** so the next reader inherits the finding instead of the silence.

Scope: `src/lib/i18n/locales/en.ts`, `src/lib/i18n/locales/ar.ts`, `docs/PROGRESS.md`. **ZERO routes, zero schema, zero migration, zero dependency, zero workflow; no production write, no secret or env read, no Supabase or Paddle credential.** Arabic is Modern Standard, short, Western numerals, no em dash — `scripts/dashes_scan.py --staged` inspected **both** locale files and passed. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 rows **#81** and **#2** are edited in place, each quoting what it supersedes — including **this register’s own prescribed remedy**, which is the first time a superseded clause here was a FIX rather than a FINDING.)

### 2026-09-11 - the webhook is WITNESSED IN PRODUCTION and the deployed secret is IDENTIFIED, and the same run exposes a live destination delivering three of the eight events the route handles (registers #86-#88 opened; B3, #2, #70, #80, #81 corrected)

(**NO CODE AND NO COPY CHANGED. `docs/PROGRESS.md` is the only file touched**, and nothing in this entry was measured by an agent: every Paddle fact below is `owner-attested`, read off the LIVE dashboard by the owner on 2026-09-11 and reported here. No agent held a Paddle credential, a Supabase credential, or an env file at any point in this session.

**WHAT WAS RUN.** Destination `ntfset_01kprw8mnymg1tyrk5kqrt90ed` (active) had its usage type changed **Platform → Both**; a single-event simulation `ntfsim_01m28s0mnzyyvsmh1fbc3c1efn` ("KnowFlow secret probe", `subscription.created`, default payload) was run **once**; the response was **`200`** with body `{"received": true}`; the payload was Paddle’s demo object — subscription `sub_01hv8x29kz0t586xy6zn1a62ny`, status `active`, subscription-level **`custom_data: null`**; the usage type was then restored to **Platform** and confirmed in the destination list. Separately, the inactive destination `ntfset_01kp513exd62615n2m33nj0h9j` was found to offer **no delete** in its Actions menu and was renamed **"DEAD - do not use - old KnowFlow webhook"**. The simulation object remains saved and inert.

**THE READING IS CONFIRMED, AND IT WAS TOO MODEST.** Confirmed as stated: the HMAC verified, so the deployed `PADDLE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is destination `…90ed`’s; the route was reached, parsed and answered inside Paddle’s window; and with `custom_data` null the handler took the zero-row `UPDATE` branch, so no row was written. **That reading stands and is not overturned.** But a `200` from this route licenses more than it was credited with, because `{received: true}` is returned at **exactly one place** (`route.ts:182`), reachable only by running the whole `try` without throwing, and **every** branch of `classifyWebhookFailure` returns 400/401/500/503 and never 200. So the same observation also proves: **(1)** `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are both set and non-empty in that runtime — `getSupabaseAdmin()` sits **inside** the `try` and `@supabase/supabase-js@2.103.0` throws on a falsy value; **(2)** the demo payload carried a non-null `current_billing_period.ends_at` — status was `active`, so the **S1 hard obligation** at line 92 would have answered **400**, which means that guard was evaluated in production and not taken; **(3)** the service-role client **reached the production database** and the statement returned no error — the `else` branch answers **500** on any `updateError`, so PostgREST was reachable, `subscriptions` exists, and the key may update it. **Two corrections of emphasis, neither of them nitpicks:** *“nothing was written”* is an **inference** from the source plus the payload and **not a measured post-state** — nobody read the table before or after; and **the run was not read-only in intent** — a write statement was issued against production with the service-role key and matched nothing. **Register #70 does NOT close and must not**: a simulation cannot mint a `subscriptions` row, so it can never be the witness that row demands, and the five things still unwitnessed (genuine `custom_data.user_id`, the overlay and the quoted price, money moving, a renewal advancing `current_period_end`, the entitlement flip on a real row) are carried into #70’s re-drawn boundary verbatim.

**THE HEADLINE IS NOT THE SECRET. IT IS WHAT READING THE DESTINATION ROW EXPOSED: A FIX THAT SHIPPED TO THE CODE AND WAS NEVER APPLIED TO THE SYSTEM IT WAS ABOUT.** That destination subscribes to **three** events — `subscription.created`, `subscription.updated`, `subscription.canceled`. `route.ts:73-80` handles **eight**. The commit that grew the set from three to eight is **`dff93fc` (2026-06-28)**, and `PIVOT_PLAN.md:373` records the defect it closed as *"only handles `created`/`canceled`/`updated`. Missing `past_due`, `paused`, and resume."* **The live destination is still configured exactly as that sentence describes, 75 days later**, and §3’s B3 row has said **✅ done** the whole time. Register **#86**, and B3 corrected in place with the superseded status quoted. **The exposure is ranked rather than shouted**, because two of the five are inert and three are not: `past_due` is **effectively covered and not by luck** (the route makes it entitling on purpose, so the mirrored and unmirrored cases produce the same entitlement and both settle at period end) — which is *exactly why nothing noticed*; `paused`/`resumed` are **real but bounded and unreachable from the app** (there is no pause path in `src/`, so only the dashboard can pause — but an unmirrored resume is a customer being charged who resolves to `free`, register #72’s founding sentence arriving through configuration); and `activated`/`trialing` are **INERT**: the price’s trial setting **was** read — the only live price shows **Trial `-`**, no trial — so there is no trial→active conversion to miss. **THE ROW IS GRADED 🟠, NOT 🔴, AND THE FIRST DRAFT OF THIS ENTRY GOT THAT WRONG.** It graded the gap by its **size** (five of eight) instead of by its **reachable consequence** — which is register **#80**’s own corrected error, *wrong in KIND, not in size*, committed a second time in the same file. **The argument that decides all five at once, and which the first draft buried under per-event prose:** `route.ts:73-80` is **ONE SHARED BLOCK**; every arm mirrors `status` + `current_period_end`. An undelivered event costs nothing unless it carries a transition **no subscribed event also carries**, and none of the five does. **Nothing reachable through this product today is missed.** What keeps it a row rather than a note is that **three silent triggers arm it** — adding a trial to a price, pausing a subscription in the dashboard, adding a second plan — and **not one of them touches `src/`**, so nothing here can see them coming. **The recommendation survives the downgrade and is separate from it:** subscribe the five anyway, and **exactly** the five, because the `switch` has **no `default` case** and any type without an arm is accepted with a 200 and no log — register **#87**’s failure shape in a second place in the same file. Priority is **hygiene, not incident**: it should not jump ahead of #81’s live “10 Subjects” falsehood. **And the configuration was never wrong when it was set** — the destination’s ULID dates it **sixty-eight days BEFORE `dff93fc`**. Three events was correct for the code of that day; a commit made it stale, and nothing was ever going to notice.

**A SECOND DEFECT, FOUND BY READING THE ROUTE AND THEN FOUND TO HAVE RUN.** Register **#87**: when `custom_data` is absent the handler updates by `paddle_subscription_id`, and **a zero-row `UPDATE` is not an error** — so it answers `200`, logs **nothing**, and Paddle records a successful delivery it will never redeliver. The sibling `FK_VIOLATION` case is the proof this is an omission and not a style: it is *also* a correct do-nothing and it is given a `console.warn` **because** *"this is the only record that a billing event outlived its account."* The attribution-less arm has no equivalent, **and the route’s own design comment is false on it** — *"a missed `created` self-heals on the next `updated`"* is the **upsert**, which this branch never reaches. **On 2026-09-11 that branch executed in production**, which makes the simulation’s real yield larger than its purpose: the first production execution of this route demonstrated the one path that cannot report itself. The fix is named so it is not redesigned (`{ count: 'exact' }` plus a warn on zero) **and it is explicitly not a 500**, because an unattributable subscription is correctly a no-op. **The branch is also the one branch of this route the project’s own harness does not cover**, which is register **#71**’s class rather than a coverage statistic: `verify-webhook-failure-classification.mjs` runs the real handler over **ten** cases and all ten take the `if (userId)` arm, while its `eventBody()` helper already builds the `custom_data: null` shape and no call site ever asks for it — *“not whether something was executed … but who chose what to assert”*. The coverage fix is one `check()` call, worth adding before the `count` fix so the new warn has a failing test first. **The field this all rests on had never been written down:** `custom_data` is set on the **TRANSACTION** (`checkout/route.ts:53`) and read from the **SUBSCRIPTION** (`webhook/route.ts:84`) — two different Paddle objects — and **`custom_data` appeared in ZERO files under `docs/` before this entry**. Paddle documents the bridge (*"Where a transaction has custom data against it, that data is copied to the created subscription"*, `developer.paddle.com`, read 2026-09-11), so the row is `vendor-documented` rather than a live hazard — which is precisely what §5’s *"fails closed is a claim about the PRODUCER’s guarantees"* rule asks to be written next to the claim instead of assumed.

**THE PRICE IS NOW MEASURED, AND #81 IS SHARPENED RATHER THAN WEAKENED.** One live price, `pri_01kp4v99rr3k0qcd6db7hm61cj`, **$49.00 monthly, no country overrides** — so the `$49` in `en.ts:74` / `ar.ts:76` is **not** stale marketing beside a different Paddle number; the copy and the catalogue agree, and no override differentiates a Moroccan or Gulf student. The defect was never a mismatch: it is that the agreed figure is the pre-pivot HR/enterprise price `PIVOT_PLAN.md:45` retired at the pivot. **Two things remain, and they are different kinds:** the **business question** (gated on three tests, of which only the gross price is now answered — **#80**’s "WHAT THIS MODEL DOES NOT CONTAIN" is corrected to say so), and the **"10 Subjects" vs 50** falsehood, which **needs no pricing decision** and has been waiting on one anyway. **Three things that read did NOT settle, recorded so they are not assumed settled:** the price’s **tax mode** (which is #81’s own VAT gate); **`PADDLE_PRO_PRICE_ID`**, unreadable here — though one live price bounds it usefully: that variable either names that price (**the student is charged $49**) or names one that does not exist live (**checkout fails outright**), and **it cannot silently charge a different amount**; and a **trial period** — **which was in fact read: Trial `-`, none**, and it is what retires two of #86’s five as inert. Register **#2** gains one clause: one of its hardcoded strings is not merely unsynced, it is **false**.

**WHAT WAS READ ON BOTH SIDES OF THE PROBE — CORRECTED IN PLACE ON THIS BRANCH, BEFORE THIS ENTRY LANDED, WHICH IS THE ONLY WINDOW IN WHICH §7 ALLOWS IT.** The first draft of this block said the `"No notifications yet"` signal on `…90ed`’s log was *“not read — not before the simulation and not after”* and might be *“unrecoverable”*. **Both halves were wrong, and the provenance belongs in the record:** that sentence was written from the 2026-09-11 brief’s own instruction, *“It was not checked; say so”*. **It had been checked.** The log was read **twice before** the simulation and **once after** it — after the usage type was restored to Platform — showing `"No notifications yet"` every time, **zero rows, Events 3, status Active**. **THREE THINGS FOLLOW, AND THEY ARE THE BEST EVIDENCE THIS ENTRY CARRIES.** **(1)** A simulated delivery is recorded **only** against the simulation run and **never enters the destination log** — the open UNKNOWN of the first draft, now measured. **(2) NO GENUINE NOTIFICATION HAS EVER BEEN DELIVERED TO THIS DESTINATION SINCE IT WAS CREATED** — and its creation instant is **2026-04-21 20:38:46Z**, decoded from the destination’s own ULID with **no Paddle access at all** (Paddle ids are ULIDs; the first ten characters are the millisecond timestamp in Crockford base32). That is **142 days** of empty log, and it converts register **#70**’s oldest inference into a positive measurement. The same decoding dates the dead destination `…t7xY` to **2026-04-14 03:38:31Z**, seven days earlier — corroborating “old KnowFlow webhook” from the identifier rather than from the label somebody typed. **(3)** The probe was **designed correctly**: the before-reading is what makes the after-reading conclusive. **One assumption is load-bearing and unverified** — that the log pane has no retention horizon; `"No notifications yet"` is a zero-state rather than a filtered range, which is why (2) is stated at full strength, and **#88** carries the caveat. **THE DISTINCTION THIS ENTRY MUST NOT BLUR:** the *route* has been reached and answered, by a simulation. A *genuine notification* has never been delivered. The first is new; the second is now measured instead of assumed, and it moves register **#70**’s boundary not one inch. §5 gains **one** rule from this and no more, and it is **the inverse of the one first drafted**. That first draft — *“a probe against a live system can consume the evidence it was run to test”* — was **born from an event that did not occur**, which is the record-versus-fact class this file exists to catch, appearing in §5 of all places. **It is neither kept with a disclaimer nor deleted:** a rule carrying its own falsification is dead weight and teaches a reader to distrust the citations on the rules beside it, while deleting it would throw away a lesson the run genuinely earned. The rule now reads — **when a probe could write to the signal you are relying on, read it BEFORE and AFTER; only the pair can tell contamination from a genuine null.** The after-reading alone proves that simulations do not log; it is the **before** pair that makes *no genuine notification has ever arrived* admissible, and had the probe written to that log, nothing after the fact could have reconstructed it. That is register **#71**’s class handled correctly rather than repeated. #88 also carries the operational inventory a rotation will need: `…90ed` is the deployed secret and **a future rotation copies from it and nowhere else**; `…t7xY` is dead, **cannot be deleted**, is renamed DEAD, and the one verb its menu still offers is **Activate**; and the saved simulation is inert **only** while `…90ed`’s usage type stays Platform.

**TWO HEADER FIELDS WERE STALE AND ARE FIXED.** *Last updated* read **2026-09-09** on a file whose newest §7 entry was dated **2026-09-10** — the one header field §6 explicitly requires bumping, missed by the commit that added that entry; it now reads 2026-09-11. *Active branch / last landed* still named `feat/61b-account-deletion` (PR **#91**) eight days after `main` moved past it to PR **#143**; it is **annotated, not rewritten**, with the same "scoped to the moment of writing, not a live pointer" caveat the line below it already carried and this one lacked — and the reason it rotted is recorded: **§6’s maintenance list does not name that field.**

**ONE EARLIER §7 ENTRY IS SUPERSEDED BY THIS ONE AND IS DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED**, per this section’s own immutability rule — named here so the next reader is not left to find it. The **2026-09-07** block (*the cancel path SHIPS and is DEPLOYED*) states *"the webhook has **never fired at our endpoint**, and our `subscriptions` table holds no row for that subscription."* It was true when written; its second half still is. **It is the ONLY one** — a sweep of every *"never fired"* / *"never delivered"* in this file shows the other two are unrelated (the 2026-08-10 block’s *"`15` was requested and never delivered"* is a cron interval; the 2026-07-10 block’s is Phase 5’s `material_uploaded` emit). §4’s **#70** carries the correction, which is where a reader looking for current state is sent.

Scope: `docs/PROGRESS.md` only. **ZERO files under `src/`, `services/`, `supabase/`; zero workflow changes; no migration, no dependency, no production write, no secret or env file read, and no Paddle or Supabase credential taken.** §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**, per this section’s append-only preamble; §3’s B3 row, §4 rows **#2**, **#70**, **#80**, **#81** and the two §1 header fields are edited **in place**, each quoting the claim it supersedes; **#86**, **#87** and **#88** are new rows. The repo↔live **vendor-configuration** lesson is deliberately carried inside **#86** and cross-referenced to **#39** rather than copied into §5, because a second copy of a rule is how rules stop being read.)

The live `$49` is **RECORDED, NOT EDITED**: the price is the owner's undecided call, and a session that quietly picked one would be doing the thing this entry exists to stop.
