**THE HEADLINE: `tryknowflow.com` QUOTES STUDENTS `$49`/MONTH, AND `PIVOT_PLAN.md:45` RETIRED THAT FIGURE AT THE PIVOT.** `en.ts:74` and `ar.ts:76` both carry `price: "$49"` - the pre-pivot HR/enterprise price - shipped to production in `73e0df3` like every deploy before it, against a market ruled **MOROCCO AND THE GULF** and a working assumption of **$9-11 net**. The plan lists that exact number under *"HR-specific / must change - Drop Enterprise entirely; restructure to one cheap consumer Pro tier."* **Register #2 tracks the FEATURE ARRAYS on that same page and says nothing about the price**, so the one number that takes money had no row while the numbers beside it did. The same block promises Pro **"10 Subjects"** while `limits.ts:18` grants **50** and `errorLimitPro` interpolates the real 50. Register **#81**.

**REGISTER #80'S `summary`/`quiz` FIGURE WAS WRONG IN KIND, NOT IN SIZE, AND IS CORRECTED IN PLACE WITH THE SUPERSEDED TEXT QUOTED.** It read *"`summary`/`quiz` at 100/day each = 3,000/month each = $105 + $151/month"* - a **generate-once** cost booked as a **recurring monthly burn**. `/api/summarize:94` returns a cached summary **before** `enforceLimit:128`; `/api/quiz/generate:206` returns a cached quiz **before** `enforceLimit:245`; there is no regenerate path (#26) and `unique(document_id, lang)` (#28/#31). **The daily caps bound the RATE of first generation; the document ceiling bounds the TOTAL** - and #80(c) already computed that ceiling. True exposure is **one-time and lifetime** (Free <= ~$6.78 ever; Pro <= ~$1,356 ever) against a superseded reading of **$256/month**. **The decision consequence inverts:** summaries and quizzes are **noise**; the entire recurring Pro exposure is the **$615/month questions** figure, which stands because questions are not cached. Carried forward uncorrected, that row would have cut the two cheapest features hardest for no reason. **#80's model gaps are now enumerated in the row itself** - no Paddle fees, no VAT, no per-market table, no heavy-user profile, no revenue model, no ingestion cost - under the heading **"WHAT THIS MODEL DOES NOT CONTAIN"**, ending *"No price may be set from this row as it stands."*

**THE OWNER'S DECISIONS, RECORDED AS HIS, DATED 2026-09-10.** **Registers #46-#49 are PUBLISHING BLOCKERS**, not deferred design-quality items; all four rows are corrected and each quotes the classification it supersedes. **The first visual proof is ACCEPTED** (landing hero and student home; Cairo for `/ar`, Rubik for `/en`, warm ground, gold accent) **with one reservation: the signed-in pages read as empty and too simple to be a paid product.** The extended signed-in proof is **not yet judged**. **New in-app features are APPROVED** to enrich those pages. Register **#85** - which also carries a source finding the owner should see first: **Cairo is not in this repository at all**, and `/ar` renders today in **Rubik with `subsets: ['latin', 'arabic']` for BOTH locales**, a choice whose own comment says it *"fixes the prior fonts, which were Latin-only and left Arabic in an unstyled browser fallback."* Splitting the family is the owner's call to make; **it is a REVERSAL, not an addition.**

**RECORDED AS PENDING, NOT SIGNED, WITH THE AGREED RECOMMENDATION AND ITS EVIDENCE.** **Phase 6 hold, going 5 -> 7** (register **#82**): flashcards and spaced repetition are what free NotebookLM already ships, and §1 already records Phase 7 as the **GATE before Phase 8** with both stores the PRIMARY GOAL since 2026-08-24. **The word NotebookLM appeared nowhere in this repository before this commit.** **§1 IS DELIBERATELY NOT EDITED** - writing an unsigned reorder into the phase table would make the table assert something nobody ruled. **Price and every Pro ceiling** stay PENDING under #80/#81, gated on Paddle's under-$10 custom-pricing floor (`owner-attested`, external, **needs a re-read date** per this section's own 2026-09-08 ruling), VAT per market, and the heavy-user profile.

**THREE GAPS THAT EXISTED ONLY IN CONVERSATION, NOW ROWS.** **#83 - locale:** the middleware decides the language **once** from `Accept-Language` and **stores nothing**; **no locale-flip control exists anywhere in `src/`**; and `defaultLocale` contradicts itself - `middleware.ts:5` says `'ar'`, `i18n/index.ts:6` says `'en'`, and five call sites use `'en'`. #46 already asked for the buttons **without stating that the mechanism behind them is absent**. Placed with the marketing shell. **#84 - citations:** rank **and** score are computed, streamed once in the `X-Citations` header, and discarded - the assistant turn persists `{conversation_id, role, content}` and nothing else, so a reloaded conversation loses its citations **and retrieval can never be tuned**, leaving `MATCH_COUNT = 8` and `match_threshold = 0.3` unfalsifiable. **`X-Citations` appears in no document in this repository.** Its reading path is a scheduled workflow on the `deletion-orphan-watch` pattern, **which transfers in SHAPE ONLY** - three outcomes not two, state in issues - because **that workflow's anon key buys "exactly one bit" and retrieval statistics are aggregates over user content**, while the same file rejects the service-role key as *"a grotesque trade"*. **#47 <-> #80(c) coupling recorded:** they are the same gap from two sides, and the materials copy merged in `73e0df3` **depends on the delete path not existing** - if #47 ships, that copy is wrong and must change in the same commit.

**`react-markdown` (#5): THE SEVERITY IS WITHDRAWN; THE MOVE IS NOT.** The row claimed *"a pruned prod install could drop it and break chat."* That break **cannot occur on this deployment**: the import is client-side and compiled into the browser bundle at build time, and `next.config.ts` sets **no `output: 'standalone'`**. **The declaration is still wrong and the move still stands**, because the guarantee is the host's and not ours - it breaks under `standalone`, under an `--omit=dev` install performed BEFORE the build, or on any host that prunes pre-build.

**§5 GAINS ONE RULE AND EXTENDS ANOTHER; IT DOES NOT GAIN A DUPLICATE.** **New:** *no usage number reaches a public surface until it has been checked against the cost model, and a number the model cannot yet justify is not published at all* - ruled on three instances in one session ($49 live, "10 Subjects" vs 50, and #142 one review away from printing Pro's 2,000/day). **Extended, not re-stated:** the *"when a remedy ships, update the row that named it"* rule (#61, 2026-09-03) now also requires grepping for rows that merely **CITE** the defect. **This is its THIRD instance and the first of that shape:** #80's closing clause called the 429 body *"English in every locale"* after PR #142 made it false - **a false sentence in a row nobody was editing, about a defect nobody was working on, under a subject naming a different register.** A second copy of a rule is how rules stop being read, so the existing one was amended rather than duplicated.

Scope: `docs/PROGRESS.md` only. **ZERO files under `src/`, `services/`, `supabase/`; zero workflow changes; no migration, no dependency, no production write.** §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**, per this section's own append-only preamble; §4 rows and §5 rules are edited **in place**, which is what §5's own *"update the row that named it"* rule requires and which §7's preamble explicitly scopes away from by saying it *"changes nothing else in this section."* Every superseded claim is quoted inside the row that replaced it.)

### 2026-09-10 - the Google OAuth project is PUBLISHED and IN PRODUCTION, which retroactively re-scopes every measurement this arc took

**THE OWNER COMPLETED THE GOOGLE CLOUD CONSOLE WORK ON 2026-09-10 AND CONFIRMED PUBLISH.** Branding is
filled in (home page, privacy, terms), **`tryknowflow.com` is on the authorized domains**, and **no logo
was uploaded, deliberately**, because uploading one triggers Google's verification review and nothing in
this product needs it. Publishing status now reads **"In production"** and the **test-user list is gone**.

**WHY THIS IS ITS OWN ENTRY RATHER THAN A LINE IN A REGISTER ROW.** Until this change, the Google button
worked for **exactly the addresses on an allow-list**, and every measurement in the link-vs-create arc was
taken under **Testing**. That includes the 2026-09-08 production run recorded in the (now closed) PR #125
and the 2026-09-10 `exchange ok` reading that closed register **#79**. Those measurements are **not
invalidated** - the OAuth consent mode governs *who may authorize*, not what GoTrue does with the identity
once it has - but any future reader who assumes the arc was measured against a public button would be
wrong about the conditions, and there is no way to tell from the code. **The button's audience changed
without a single line of this repository changing**, which is exactly the class of fact that has no other
home.

**WHAT ACTUALLY CHANGES, STATED NARROWLY.** Before: an address not on the test-user list got Google's own
"app has not completed verification" screen and never reached our callback at all. After: any Google
account reaches it. So the population that can hit `/api/auth/callback`'s code arm went from **one
allow-listed address** to **the public**, and the first genuinely unfamiliar user of this app's auth
surface is now possible. **Nothing in the app was built for a smaller audience** and nothing needs
changing - but the register **#79** recipe for producing a live `password_replaced: true` is now reachable
by an ordinary member of the public arriving in the ordinary way, rather than only by a deliberate
in-house test. It stops being a thing we would have to stage and becomes a thing we should expect to
observe.

**NO DASHBOARD SETTING WAS CHANGED BY ANY AGENT.** This entry records the owner's action, read back from
his own report of the console. No agent in this session has ever had, or used, access to the Google Cloud
console or the Supabase dashboard.

### 2026-09-09 - cross-device password recovery is WITNESSED ON A PHONE, and BOTH of the agent's predictions about the mail were wrong: it reached the INBOX, and `{{ .TokenHash }}` still carries a `pkce_` prefix

(**WHAT SHIPPED, over three changes.** PR **#128** closed register #74, which recorded that this application had **no password reset at all** while every account on it is password-gated. PR **#129** made `type=recovery` authoritative over the `kf_recovery` cookie, because that cookie lives only in the browser that asked for the mail and would otherwise have sent a cross-device user to the dashboard, signed in, with the forgotten password still in place. Then the owner **flipped the recovery email template** by hand to `{{ .SiteURL }}/api/auth/callback?token_hash={{ .TokenHash }}&type=recovery`. The signup template was deliberately **not** touched: it is the only positive deliverability signal this domain has, and changing both would have made the next reading uninterpretable.

**THE SUCCESS CRITERION WAS PRE-COMMITTED, ON PURPOSE.** The agent stated in advance that it did **not** expect the template change to clear the spam verdict, and the owner recorded that so a persisting spam result could not read as failure of a change that worked. Success was defined as **the link reaching the reset form from a SECOND DEVICE**, and nothing else. That discipline is the reason both errors below are legible as errors rather than being quietly absorbed.

**WHAT PRODUCTION EXECUTION WITNESSED.** One mail, sent from `tryknowflow.com` at 02:03:23Z, opened by the owner **on his phone** - a device that had never touched the flow and held no PKCE verifier. **He landed on the set-a-new-password form, not the dashboard.** That closes the cross-device limitation recorded against #128, and it closes the one seam #129 could not execute: the line passing `type` into `landing()` after a `verifyOtp` that actually SUCCEEDS. Both are now measurements rather than arguments.

The delivered link, verbatim:

`https://tryknowflow.com/api/auth/callback?token_hash=pkce_29fc8b7fabc803a06a32b7b62bff2a96279a0a68510f017b82e6c465&type=recovery`

Host is the brand domain, no `/auth/v1/verify` hop, no double slash from a trailing Site URL, and the ampersand survived unescaped.

**PREDICTION ONE WAS WRONG: IT REACHED THE INBOX.** No spam folder, no phishing banner, and Gmail left the anchors live - which independently confirms the earlier finding that link-stripping was an EFFECT of a phishing verdict rather than its cause.

The agent had argued the opposite from what looked like a controlled comparison: the signup mail links to the SAME Supabase host and reaches the inbox, therefore the link host cannot be the discriminator, therefore moving it would remove a signal that is not the deciding one. **The observation was true and the inference was invalid.** Spam classification is **additive scoring against a threshold**, not a single discriminating feature, and the two arms of that comparison were never comparable: a password-reset message carries a far higher baseline score than a signup confirmation. The same host mismatch was **survivable in the low-risk message and decisive in the high-risk one**. Holding one variable constant across two messages with different baselines isolates nothing. **The transferable lesson: a signal that is demonstrably harmless in one message class can be sufficient on its own in another, and "it does not spam A, so it is not what spams B" is not an argument.** The related claim that reputation was therefore the operative cause is retired as overstated - a content change was sufficient here.

**PREDICTION TWO WAS WRONG: THE `pkce_` PREFIX SURVIVED `{{ .TokenHash }}`, AND `verifyOtp` ACCEPTED IT ANYWAY.** This one matters more than the first, because the prefix was load-bearing in the cross-device reasoning and that reasoning was stated imprecisely enough to mislead a future reader.

**The prefix marks how the flow was INITIATED, not how the link is delivered.** `resetPasswordForEmail` is called from `createBrowserClient`, which `@supabase/ssr` pins to `flowType: 'pkce'`, so GoTrue records the recovery flow as a PKCE flow and prefixes the stored token hash accordingly. `{{ .TokenHash }}` renders that stored value, prefix included.

**The verifier was never a property of the token. It was a property of the ENDPOINT.** `/auth/v1/verify`, on seeing a `pkce_` token, completes the flow by issuing an authorization **code**, and it is the **code exchange** that requires the verifier cookie. Going straight to `verifyOtp` with the hash **bypasses code issuance entirely**: GoTrue validates the hash and issues a session directly, so no verifier is involved, prefix or no prefix. The conclusion drawn earlier (cross-device would work) was right; the mechanism given for it ("`pkce_` links need the verifier") was wrong. **The accurate rule is: WHICH ENDPOINT CONSUMES THE TOKEN decides whether a verifier is needed, not what the token looks like.** Anyone who reads `pkce_` in a link and infers "this cannot work on another device" will be wrong.

A harmless consequence, recorded so it is not mistaken for something worse later: the PKCE code-verifier cookie left in the requesting browser is now **orphaned**. Nothing consumes it and it expires on its own.

**COPY CORRECTED IN BOTH LOCALES, because the flip made it false.** `forgotSameDevice` ("Open the link on this device, in this browser. It will not work anywhere else.") is renamed to `forgotAnyDevice` and now reads "You can open the link on any device." The key was **renamed rather than reworded in place**, because a key named for the opposite of what it holds is exactly the stale artifact this project keeps finding. Its pre-submit instance is **deleted**: a caveat that no longer exists does not need pre-warning, and the reassurance is kept only in the sent state where it is useful. `resetNoSession` no longer blames the browser.

**ONE STRING WAS CHANGED THAT THE OWNER DID NOT NAME**, disclosed rather than slipped in. `noticeSigninRequired` also carried same-device advice ("open the link on the same device and browser you asked from"). Under the new template a recovery failure routes to `link_expired` and never reaches that notice, so the sentence had become unreachable advice for the only case it described. It is shortened to "We could not finish that link. Please sign in below.", which is true for the cases that DO still reach it: OAuth returns, and links minted before the flip. Trivially revertible if the owner disagrees.

**STILL OPEN, unchanged by this.** The abandonment window from #128: reaching the reset form means a live session exists on that device before the password is replaced, and closing the tab leaves it. Inherent to Supabase recovery, not fixed. Register **#75**, whether a repeat `signUp` re-sends confirmation mail, remains unverified. And PR **#125**, the Google link-or-create harness, remains unmerged with the unconfirmed-email branch of that question still unmeasured.

Scope: `docs/PROGRESS.md`, both locale files, and the two recovery pages. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 is untouched; the `Last updated` header advances to 2026-09-09. **Zero** changes under `services/` or `supabase/`; zero workflow changes; no dashboard setting or DNS touched by the agent; no mail sent beyond the single authorized verification send.)

### 2026-09-08 - the signup confirmation gate SHIPS and is then WITNESSED ON PRODUCTION end to end: the three elements that merged unproven are now executed, and the `pkce_` token settles which callback arm actually fires (registers #74, #75 opened)

(**WHAT SHIPPED.** PR **#126** (merge `b955e2e`) closed a live defect on the main signup path, found by reading source and confirmed by an owner dashboard read: **Confirm email is ON**, `signUp` returns `{ user, session }` with `session` **null** until the address is proven, and **nothing read it**. `router.push('/dashboard')` ran unconditionally, the middleware bounced it, and **every new password signup landed on Sign In with nothing on screen.** The account was fine; it looked like the signup had failed. Three further faults sat on the same path: the `profiles` upsert ran session-less against a policy of `using (auth.uid() = id)` and was therefore **always refused**, its `42501` swallowed by a `code !== '23505'` console filter; **neither locale carried a single string** about confirmation, inbox or verification (sixteen `auth` strings each, symmetric, none of them this), and the signup page had **nowhere to put one** — its only message slot was a red error box, so "check your inbox" would have arrived dressed as a failure; and `signUp` passed no `emailRedirectTo`, so the mail link defaulted to the **bare Site URL** and dropped the user on the marketing homepage, still signed out, with **no confirmation-landing route in the application at all.**

**THE MERGE WAS EXPLICIT ABOUT WHAT IT HAD NOT PROVEN, WHICH IS THE REASON THIS BLOCK EXISTS.** #126 recorded three open elements. The signup notice had **never been seen on screen**, because rendering it requires creating a real user on the production database and that run was forbidden from writing to production. **Which format the confirmation mail carries** was unknown, because the email template is a dashboard setting no agent could read — so **both arms were built rather than one chosen**, `token_hash` through `verifyOtp` and `code` through `exchangeCodeForSession`. And whether `emailRedirectTo` **survived into the delivered link** was built but never observed.

**WHAT PRODUCTION EXECUTION THEN WITNESSED.** Owner-executed on `tryknowflow.com`, 2026-09-08, using `fettah.tornido123@gmail.com`.

**(1) THE NOTICE RENDERS AND THE BOUNCE IS GONE.** After Create Account on `/en/signup`: *"Check your inbox. We sent a confirmation link to your email address. Open it to activate your account."* The user **stays on the signup page**. No push to a dashboard, no bounce to login. That element is no longer unproven.

**(2) MAIL IS DELIVERED TO AN ORDINARY GMAIL ADDRESS**, from `noreply@tryknowflow.com`, sender name **KnowFlow**, subject **"Confirm Your Signup"**. Resend SMTP reaches a non-team address, which is what took the built-in-mailer failure mode off the table rather than merely making it unlikely.

**(3) THE DELIVERED LINK, VERBATIM:** `https://wnpqdafdkbuvwecksrjj.supabase.co/auth/v1/verify?token=pkce_ce066efac3fce176c15d71f4b663e12a0304fb5496f02202bbb7b96d&type=signup&redirect_to=https://tryknowflow.com/api/auth/callback`

**`emailRedirectTo` SURVIVES INTO THE DELIVERED LINK** — the one hop in the chain that could only be built and never observed. And **the token is `pkce_`-prefixed**, which settles the arm question outright: `/auth/v1/verify` consumes that token itself, confirms the address, and redirects to `redirect_to` carrying a **`code`**. **The code-exchange arm is the one that fires; the `token_hash` arm does not.** Both exist because the template was unreadable to the agent that wrote them, and this is the measurement that says which one is live. **It is on the record here rather than left in an agent's head**, so nobody deletes the wrong arm as dead code or debugs the wrong one when this path next misbehaves.

**(4) CLICKING IT LANDED SIGNED IN ON `/en/dashboard`** with the account email in the sidebar. The ruling that a confirmation landing should **sign the user in and continue**, rather than deposit them on login with a success message, is **what actually happens** and not merely what was argued.

**ONE CONSEQUENCE THAT IS NOT A DEFECT AND MUST NOT BE DISCOVERED TWICE.** Because this template uses the `verify?token=pkce_...` form, the code exchange requires the **PKCE verifier cookie**, which exists only in the browser that performed the signup. **A link opened on a different device cannot complete that exchange** — sign up on a laptop, read mail on a phone, which is ordinary rather than exotic. That path is **already handled and is not a failure**: `/auth/v1/verify` confirms the address *before* redirecting, so the account is fine and only the session is missing; the callback falls to `/login?notice=signin_required` and the user signs in normally. It was designed from the **measured** `pkce_code_verifier_not_found` error, whose own message names the cross-device case. **So `signin_required` is the ordinary cross-device path, not a rare edge.** The lever, should same-device-only ever become unacceptable, is switching the email template to `{{ .TokenHash }}`, which activates the currently dormant `token_hash` arm and removes the verifier dependency entirely. **That is a dashboard change. It is recorded here as a known lever and was NOT taken.**

**TWO ITEMS RECORDED, NOT FIXED.** **Register #74** — there is **no password reset in this application at all**, which is the more severe sibling of the defect just closed. **Register #75** — whether calling `signUp` again for an unconfirmed address re-sends the mail is **unverified**, and the `link_expired` copy shipped in #126 already instructs the user to do exactly that.

Scope: `docs/PROGRESS.md` only. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**; §4 gains **rows #74 and #75**; and the `Last updated` header field, stale at 2026-09-04 while two 2026-09-08 entries sat below it, is advanced to 2026-09-08. **ZERO files under `src/`**, zero under `services/`, `supabase/`; zero workflow changes.)


### 2026-09-08 - the Apple Developer account is APPROVED AND ACTIVE, not awaiting approval; the target market is ruled MOROCCO AND THE GULF; EU trader status is deferred

(**THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES ONE CLAIM IN THE 2026-08-24 ENTRY BELOW**, which records *"The Apple Developer Program fee is PAID, the account complete and awaiting approval — `owner-attested` (§2.6.6), NOT machine-verified"*. The fee and the provenance stand. **"Awaiting approval" is false and is corrected here**, the way this section prescribes: a new entry, never an edit.

**WHAT THE OWNER READ, 2026-09-08.** Signed into **App Store Connect** under his own name: **Apps, Business, and Users and Access all available, and the Add Apps button live.** The Apple Developer Program account is **APPROVED AND ACTIVE**, and has been for some unknown length of time. **No app is registered** — App Store Connect shows **No Apps** — and that half of the record was accurate.

**THE FAILURE IS NOT THE PROVENANCE LABEL; IT IS THE ABSENCE OF A RE-READ.** `owner-attested` correctly marked a fact no agent had verified, and that discipline is right and stays. But **a label does not expire**, and nothing in this repository was ever going to notice when an external system changed state. The sentence sat unchallenged from 2026-08-24 to 2026-09-08, and **two sessions sequenced real work around a pending enrollment that was not pending** — most recently deferring Sign in with Apple behind it and planning to ship Google alone. **An owner-attested fact about an external system that can change needs a RE-READ DATE, not merely a label.** That is the transferable lesson and it is the reason this block exists at all.

**WHAT IT UNBLOCKS.** App ID, **Services ID**, private key and Team ID are reachable today, so **Sign in with Apple is no longer blocked and ships ALONGSIDE Google rather than behind it.** That is the better order on its own terms: guideline **4.8** — quoted verbatim in this session against Apple's live page — requires a qualifying alternative login service the moment a third-party login such as Google Sign-In is offered for the primary account, and exempts an app that *"exclusively uses your company's own account setup and sign-in systems."* KnowFlow is in that exemption **today** and leaves it the instant Google ships. Shipping both together retires a submission-blocking debt instead of incurring one.

**TARGET MARKET RULED, AND IT IS STANDING: MOROCCO AND THE GULF.** Europe is **deferred, not cancelled**. It bites in two places already on the record. It puts the product **outside the United States storefront** — the only one where 3.1.1(a) permits buttons or links to a purchasing mechanism other than in-app purchase — so the Phase 8 billing-surface gate is **binding rather than theoretical**. And it makes the **EU trader-status** banner in App Store Connect a **deferred item, not a blocker**: it gates EU distribution only, and does not gate registering an app, obtaining sign-in keys, or submitting to non-EU storefronts. **Recorded so nobody sequences around it.**

Scope: `docs/PIVOT_PLAN.md` §5 and §9, and this block. **ZERO files under `src/`**, zero under `services/`, `supabase/`, zero workflow changes. §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**, and the superseded sentences are struck through in place at their §5 and §9 sites rather than deleted.)

### 2026-09-08 - a predicted deletion defect was measured and did NOT exist; Tier 2 executed in substance; verifiablyCanceled is exported and both its cases are now real executions

(**THIS BLOCK SUPERSEDES ONE SENTENCE OF THE 2026-09-07 ENTRY BELOW**, which states that `verifiablyCanceled` *"is unreachable by any test and had to be transcribed rather than executed."* That was true when written and is false as of this change. §7 entries are immutable, so this corrects it the way the preamble prescribes — a new entry, not an edit.

**A DEFECT WAS PREDICTED IN SHIPPED CODE AND THE PREDICTION WAS WRONG.** Shipping PR #106 made a state reachable that had never existed before: a subscription carrying a pending `scheduledChange`. Account deletion calls `cancel(id, { effectiveFrom: 'immediately' })` and demands `status === 'canceled' && !scheduledChange`, and earlier the same day we had **measured** Paddle throwing `subscription_locked_pending_changes` against an already-scheduled subscription. The reading was that a customer who cancelled and then deleted their account would be **unable to delete it** — an Apple **5.1.1(v)** hard requirement, and the very requirement cleared last session to unblock publishing. It was recorded as **INFERRED rather than measured**, because the lock had been observed only for `next_billing_period`.

**THE MEASUREMENT OVERTURNED IT.** `cancel(immediately)` against that exact state **RESOLVED** — `status: 'canceled'`, `scheduledChange: null`. **An immediate cancel OVERRIDES a pending scheduled change** rather than colliding with it, so the lock does not apply. The catch-then-re-read arm never executed, `cancelUserSubscriptions` returned `{ ok: true, outcome: 'canceled', canceled: 1 }`, and deletion proceeds past the irreversible boundary to `auth.admin.deleteUser`: **HTTP 200**, never the 409 or the 500. **No defect. Nothing to fix.** The error was generalising a lock measured on one call shape to a call with different semantics, and the generalisation was labelled as an inference before it was tested, which is the only reason it cost a subscription rather than a wrong repair next to an irreversible step.

**THE PROBE WAS DESIGNED SO THE DESTRUCTIVE OUTCOME WAS THE SAFE ONE** — a throw would have consumed nothing and proved a defect; a success would spend the subscription and prove there was none. It succeeded. **`sub_01m1yqapt1zxhjvamk4hzjmsfr` is spent**, and the sandbox now holds **two canceled subscriptions and nothing live**, so every further billing experiment costs the full re-mint sequence (`docs/BILLING_SANDBOX_TEST_ORDER.md` rule 3). That was a known coin-flip, stated in advance, and it landed on the destructive side.

**TWO THINGS WERE GAINED.** **TIER 2 IS NOW EXECUTED IN SUBSTANCE** — the immediate cancel, through our own deletion code, against a real subscription, consuming it — so the sandbox test order has run top to bottom. And **`verifiablyCanceled` IS EXPORTED**: it returned **true** here against a genuinely `canceled` subscription, and Tier 1 had already shown **false** on a period-end success state, so **both cases are now real executions** rather than one execution and one transcription. That is the last transcription in this arc, closed.

This change is **one line under `src/`** — adding `export` to a function whose body is untouched — plus `docs/`. Zero under `services/`, `supabase/`; zero workflow changes; §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**.)

### 2026-09-07 - the cancel path SHIPS and is DEPLOYED: period end never immediately, every branch of it executed against real Paddle through our own code, one defect found by running it, and register #70 still does not close

(**A PAYING CUSTOMER CAN NOW STOP BEING BILLED WITHOUT DESTROYING THEIR ACCOUNT.** PR **#106** merged as **`a85711b`**, closing the product gap register **#70** opened on 2026-09-04: until today `subscriptions.cancel()` appeared **exactly once** in the codebase, inside the deletion orchestrator, so the only exit from billing was to delete every byte of your data. `POST /api/account/subscription/cancel` + `src/lib/subscription/cancel.ts` + a Settings card that sits **above** the delete card now pass `effectiveFrom: 'next_billing_period'`. **PERIOD END, NEVER IMMEDIATELY, AND THAT IS THE DECISION.** The customer has already paid for the period and this codebase has no refund code — a refund is a separate `adjustments` call it has never made — so `immediately` would mean keeping their money and withdrawing the service. The deletion path keeps `immediately` and stays right for the opposite reason. Same API, two call sites, two different right answers, neither the other's default. **NO DDL, NO MIGRATION AND NO DATABASE WRITE, AND THE ABSENCE OF THE WRITE IS THE DESIGN:** Paddle leaves the status `active` and the row already carries a future `current_period_end`, which is exactly what `getEntitlement` needs to keep the customer Pro for the time they paid for and lapse them **by the clock**. Writing `status = 'canceled'` would revoke access instantly and take away the very time this exists to protect; that trap is named in the code so nobody adds it.

**EVERY BRANCH OF THE MODULE HAS NOW EXECUTED AGAINST REAL PADDLE, THROUGH OUR OWN CODE.** Everything before this arc proved the **SDK**; this is the first time `src/lib/subscription/cancel.ts` itself ran. Against `sub_01m1yqapt1zxhjvamk4hzjmsfr`: `scheduleSubscriptionCancellation` returned `{ ok: true, outcome: 'scheduled', scheduled: 1, effectiveAt: '2026-10-07T20:00:38.204Z' }` — **the value Paddle holds**, not one we computed — and `scheduledChange.effectiveAt` equalled `currentBillingPeriod.endsAt` **EXACTLY**, which is this feature's whole design assertion executed rather than argued. `readScheduledCancellation` read that timestamp back with the change attached and returned `null` after the reset, never throwing. **THE PREDICATE PAIR IS THE FINDING THAT SHAPED THE MODULE AND IT IS NOW MEASURED ON A LIVE OBJECT:** `verifiablyScheduledToCancel` returned **true** while the deletion module's `verifiablyCanceled` returned **false on BOTH of its terms** — status is `active` not `canceled`, and a scheduled change *is* attached. Sharing that predicate would have **reported failure on every success**, telling a customer their cancellation had failed while Paddle had scheduled it, and inviting them toward the delete button. **THE DOUBLE-CLICK ARM RAN TOO, AND THE MODULE'S OWN COMMENT WAS VINDICATED RATHER THAN WRONG:** it said an already-scheduled subscription *"plausibly throws"*, `plausibly` was doing real work, and it **does** throw — `subscription_locked_pending_changes` — so the catch-then-re-read arm executed and a customer who clicks twice is correctly told the cancellation is in place.

**ONE DEFECT WAS FOUND BY RUNNING IT, RULED ON, AND FIXED BEFORE THE MERGE.** With two subscriptions where the first succeeded and the second failed, the library returned an honest `{ ok: false, scheduled: 1 }` **against a partial success that was real in Paddle** — and the route discarded `result.scheduled`, answered a flat `CancelFailed`, and the card rendered *"That did not work and nothing was changed."* **The customer was told nothing changed while one of their subscriptions was genuinely scheduled to cancel.** A control with two bogus ids proved the loop also **stopped at the first failure**: the second id was never attempted, so a customer with three subscriptions kept being billed by the third. **THE OWNER RULED THAT THE LOOP CONTINUES** — every id in that list is the customer's own, read with `.eq('user_id', userId)`, and they asked for all of them to end, so an early return buys nothing but a live subscription still drawing money from someone who asked to stop. Fixed in **`8262dd7`**: the failure arm carries `failed` and `total`, the route sends a **stable code** plus counts with the detail kept in the log — the shape `paddle-errors.ts` (#109) and `paddle-webhook-errors.ts` (#110) established, and **this was the last undifferentiated Paddle failure in the codebase** — and the card tells the truth in both languages with a support reference, because a **permanently dead id would otherwise loop a customer through "try again" forever**.

**GETTING A SUBSCRIPTION TO TEST AGAINST COST FOUR BLOCKED ATTEMPTS AND IS WRITTEN UP IN `docs/BILLING_SANDBOX_TEST_ORDER.md`.** A browser checkout was refused throughout; it was minted **by invoice**. The path is **not** one call — Paddle validates the status transition against the **pre-update** state (`bad_request`, *"transaction needs to have ready status"*) — and it is **not** purely API-driven: `addresses.create` **and** `addresses.list` both returned `forbidden` until the sandbox key was granted Addresses permission. A checkout-created customer was **rejected** for manual collection where an API-created one was accepted, and an address carrying only a `countryCode` was rejected until completed **in place** with `firstLine`/`city`/`postalCode`/`region`. The reset loop — `update({ scheduledChange: null })` — was proven repeatable **2 of 2** before anything else was spent against the subscription, which is what let a single subscription serve the whole tier.

**DEPLOYED, AND CONFIRMED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** GitHub deployment **`6317416446`** records environment **Production**, sha **`a85711b`**, state **success**; the Railway deployment of the same sha also succeeded, and the deployed URL answers **200**. `tsc`, `db-types` and `ingestion-image` are green on the merge commit, alongside the Vercel and Railway statuses. **Merged with `--subject` and `--body` passed explicitly**, per the §5 rule #93 earned.

**REGISTER #70 DOES NOT CLOSE, AND THE SENTENCE THAT KEEPS IT OPEN IS UNCHANGED: "Nothing here may be described as production-verified."** The key was a **sandbox** key, the Supabase client was a **stub** so no database was read or written at any point, the webhook has **never fired at our endpoint**, and our `subscriptions` table holds no row for that subscription. The owner's finding that production `subscriptions` holds **zero rows** is recorded in the row **attributed and explicitly NOT independently verified**, because `.env.local` is unreadable to the agent under a deny rule and no count was taken — writing someone else's unmeasured claim as a measured one is the failure register #71 exists for. **WHAT WOULD MOVE IT:** one real production subscription with a row written **by the webhook**, then this same sequence run against it — which required this path to be deployed, which is why it merged. **STILL OPEN AND NAMED:** Tier 2 of the sandbox order (the immediate cancel, terminal, deliberately unrun); whether `subscriptions.update` **requires** status `active`, shown to work while active and never shown to require it; and `verifiablyCanceled` being **module-private**, so the predicate whose misuse this feature exists to prevent is unreachable by any test and had to be transcribed rather than executed. This change touches **`docs/PROGRESS.md` only** — zero under `src/`, `services/`, `supabase/`, zero workflow changes — and §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**.)

### 2026-09-04 - the deletion arc is EXERCISED: the Paddle cancel branch runs against a real cancellation, the most heavily verified change of the arc is reversed on evidence, and the arc costs roughly double an estimate that was Claude's

(**THE BRANCH THAT #91 WAS HELD FOR IS NO LONGER UNEXERCISED.** `src/lib/account-deletion/paddle.ts` — the module whose own comments call its end-state assertion the single most important decision in it — has now been run against a **real Paddle sandbox subscription** created through the product's own checkout and cancelled through the product's own deletion path. **BEFORE:** `sub_01m1mrte7wdpfewsbvmbztkwwg`, `status: "active"`, `scheduledChange: null`, `nextBilledAt: 2026-10-03T23:14:17.307915Z`. **AFTER:** `status: "canceled"`, `scheduledChange: null`, `canceledAt: 2026-09-03T23:20:10.244Z`, `nextBilledAt: null`. `verifiablyCanceled` asserted a real end state and `DELETE /api/account` returned **200**. **THE ASSERTION WAS EXERCISED; THE HAZARD IT GUARDS AGAINST WAS NOT REPRODUCED, AND CANNOT BE ON THIS PATH — AND A FUTURE READER MUST NOT READ THE GREEN RESULT AS PROOF THE HAZARD WAS REAL.** The comment's claim is about Paddle's **default** (`next_billing_period`, returning a still-`active` subscription with a pending `scheduledChange`); the code passes `effectiveFrom: 'immediately'` explicitly and therefore never issues the defaulted call. What was measured is that the fix works, not that the danger exists. The extra field read before an irreversible act remains the right trade — it costs one comparison and stands between a wrong assumption and a subscription that can never be cancelled. **WHICH END STATE YOU ASSERT IS ITSELF THE FINDING:** on the cancelled subscription, `items[0].status` **remained `"active"`**. Had `verifiablyCanceled` asserted the item rather than the subscription, it would have read a successful cancellation as a failure and **aborted every deletion for every paying user**. The module chose the right field, and that is recorded as a near-miss rather than passed over. **THE COMMENT NAMED THE WRONG ERROR CODE AND THE DESIGN SURVIVED ANYWAY.** It predicts `already_canceled`; Paddle actually returns **`subscription_update_when_canceled`**, measured by calling `cancel()` on the already-cancelled subscription. `cancelAndVerify` took its **catch** path, the `get()` re-read rescued it, and the shipped `cancelUserSubscriptions` returned `{ ok: true, outcome: canceled, canceled: 1 }` with `canceledAt` **unmoved** — nothing cancelled twice. It survived precisely **because it never branches on the error code**, which is the strongest vindication of `436fe19`'s rule in the record: the author's specific prediction was wrong and the design did not depend on it. **A SECOND, INDEPENDENT WITNESS EXISTS OUTSIDE THE API:** the Paddle cancellation email arrived bearing the same timestamp as `canceledAt`, confirming the cancellation from the customer's side as well as from the read.

**THE MOST HEAVILY VERIFIED CHANGE OF THE ARC WAS STILL WRONG, BECAUSE IT WAS VERIFIED AGAINST THE WRONG REQUIREMENT.** `1c525f1` latched the delete control off after a 409 and was proven as behaviour, not as diff: interception established before any UI contact, the server's `DELETE` count held at its baseline, a deliberately wrong email kept in the field throughout, and **five attempts to defeat the latch — real mouse click, programmatic `.click()`, synthetic `MouseEvent`, Enter, Space — all refused**. Experiment (iii) then showed the requirement itself was false: step 3 succeeds on retry, steps 1 and 2 are idempotent by construction, and step 4 is a bare retry of `deleteUser` that either completes or fails again harmlessly. **Retrying costs the user nothing on any path and completes the deletion on at least one**, so the old copy told a frightened person "do not try again" while the latch disabled the only control that could finish what they had asked for. `000d2e2` reverses the latch forward and rewrites both locales — reassurance first, one instruction, no negation to parse — and was itself re-verified in the **Arabic RTL card**, where the open button reads `disabled: false`, clicking it reopens the panel, and the new string renders right-aligned across three lines. **`1c525f1` STAYS IN THE BRANCH HISTORY DELIBERATELY.** Thorough verification of the wrong requirement is a **distinct failure mode** from insufficient verification, and this repository now has a measured instance of it rather than an aphorism. It opens register **#66**. The per-mount bound — the latch died with the mount, so a reload re-armed the control the message forbade — is recorded there as **what `1c525f1` was**, moot from the moment the policy inverted and never fixed because it should not have been.

**THE ARC COST ROUGHLY DOUBLE ITS ESTIMATE AND THE ESTIMATE WAS CLAUDE'S.** It was scoped as: a sandbox signup, credentials, a price id, and one test-card subscription — "well under an hour", on the measured ground that a Paddle sandbox needs no approval. What it actually required, in the order it was discovered: a **sandbox account**; a **default payment link**, which no live account ever needs because it already has one; a **corrected payment-link domain**, after Claude advised `https://tryknowflow.com/en/pricing` and called its value "not load-bearing" when Paddle's own checklist requires it to match the domain under test (`http://localhost:3000`, since the field rejects a bare `localhost`); **two additional pull requests** (**#94** `.gitignore`, **#95** the browser environment) beyond the one that was planned (**#93**); and a **client-side code change nobody scoped**, because the environment switch had been built on the server and not in the browser. Claude also asserted it could complete Paddle's checkout itself and could not: the overlay is a cross-origin iframe from `sandbox-buy.paddle.com`, keystrokes never land, and the accessibility tree does not cross the origin boundary — the blocker was mechanical and the claim was made without testing the reach. **Stated as the record shows it, not as a lesson.**

**WHAT WAS EXERCISED, AND WHAT REMAINS NOT.** Pass A ran the whole ordering end-to-end on a throwaway with a **real 322-byte storage object written by the app's own ingest path** and a **real seeded waitlist row**, so the two paths no cascade reaches — the storage sweep and the waitlist erasure — both ran on non-zero data; afterwards `auth.users` returned **404**, every table read **zero rows** (measured directly by known id, not inferred from an absent parent), the storage prefix was empty, and Run 5 of `docs/deletion-preflight-read.md` returned **zero orphan rows** against `main PRODUCTION` under a byte-for-byte read-back gate. **Experiment (i) is NOT a re-run of Pass A and must never be recorded as one:** its account had no subjects, materials or files, so the sweep and the waitlist erasure both took their **zero paths** there. **Experiment (ii)** measured the import-time throw that PR #93 landed as an explicitly unmeasured claim: the throw reaches the log in full, with stack, source excerpt and the offending `page:`, and the blast radius is **exactly the three routes that import `paddleClient`** — `/api/account`, `/api/paddle/checkout`, `/api/paddle/webhook` — while six other API routes and every page kept serving. One `/api/ingest` 500 in that run **looked like a fourth failing route and was a malformed probe**, caught by re-measuring with a correct multipart body rather than reasoned away. **STILL NOT EXERCISED, AND NAMED:** the `deleteUser`-fails orphan branch, which needs fault injection that was refused at the start of the arc and refused again at its end; and **six of eleven cascade tables** (`chunks`, `quizzes`, `quiz_items`, `conversations`, `messages`, `study_events`), unreachable because local ingest is dead — register **#69**, issue **#92**.

**THREE STANDING HAZARDS OPEN, ALL FOUND BY EXECUTION AND NONE BY REVIEW.** Register **#66** (verification against the wrong requirement). Register **#67**: **`TaskStop` does not kill `next dev`** — observed five times; a server started hours earlier, carrying **live Paddle credentials from `.env.local` alone**, was found still holding **port 3000** during an arc built to avoid a real charge, having survived two of Claude's own explicit claims to have stopped it. Register **#68**: **three "complete" fixes each covered one side of a boundary** — `.gitignore` as two literal filenames rather than a glob (`3598a8f`), the Paddle environment switch as server-only (`b26ad57` then `4d5f46a`), and `/api/paddle/checkout` catching Paddle's `code`/`detail`/`documentationUrl` and returning a bare `Internal Server Error`, which is **still open** and is why diagnosing a self-describing configuration error required a server log the user cannot see. Register **#70** opens with commercial weight: **`subscriptions.cancel()` exists exactly once in the codebase, inside account deletion**, and there is no portal, downgrade or plain-cancel path — so **a paying customer who wants to stop being billed must destroy every byte of their data** (issue **#96**). **§5 GAINS A RULE:** `--body` must be passed explicitly when squash-merging, because `squash_merge_commit_message` is `COMMIT_MESSAGES` and the pull-request body — where the caveats are written, because that is where reviewers read — never reaches `main`. Caught on **#93**, whose commit carried a verification matrix and no caveat while its PR body carried the one open claim; the caveat survives in `b26ad57` only because it was passed by hand. **This change touches `docs/PROGRESS.md` only** — zero under `src/`, `services/`, `supabase/`, zero workflow changes — and §7 gains **one block with zero deletions**.)

### 2026-09-03 - account deletion ships: the ordering is the deliverable, one atomic statement sits after the irreversible act, and register #61 was stale for nine days

(**KNOWFLOW CAN NOW DELETE AN ACCOUNT.** `DELETE /api/account` + `src/lib/account-deletion/orchestrate.ts` + a Settings card + the privacy page corrected forward, closing register **#61(b)** — scoped here for the first time, since that row had explicitly declined to scope it. **Both stores require this**: Apple **5.1.1(v)**, and Google Play's requirement is a superset. **THE ORDERING IS THE DELIVERABLE, AND IT CHANGED TWICE DURING DESIGN, BOTH TIMES BECAUSE THE CODE CONTRADICTED THE PLAN.** It is now storage sweep → waitlist erase → **Paddle cancel (irreversible)** → `auth.admin.deleteUser`. **The sweep moved ahead of the cancel** because `storage.ts` states its own contract — the caller must abort on failure, since the `{user_id}/` prefix is the only handle on those objects — so a sweep failure now costs nothing irreversible at all. **The 'one transaction around the row delete and the waitlist erase' that had been agreed was withdrawn**: `supabase-js` speaks PostgREST and has no multi-statement transactions, so it would have required a Postgres function and therefore DDL, on a PR the 2026-08-24 re-scope ruled should be application code. Erasing the waitlist row *before* the account, while the email is still in hand, gives the same guarantee for nothing. **Exactly one atomic statement now sits after the irreversible boundary.** **THE ACCEPTED FAILURE STATE IS NAMED IN THE CODE, IN THE REGISTER AND TO THE USER:** cancel succeeds, delete fails, and the account survives with its subscription gone. Unrecoverable, and the smaller harm — the alternative orphans a live subscription that bills forever with no record left to cancel against. It gets an `[account-deletion-orphan]` log line and an HTTP **409** with its own code, so the user is told exactly what happened and told not to retry. **A durable, alertable trace is NOT built here** — that is #54's durable half. Its sibling, files outliving their owner, **is** query-detectable and ships today as Run 5 of `docs/deletion-preflight-read.md`. One is a query and one is an arc because one leaves evidence in the system and the other is a Postgres/Paddle divergence no query can see. **EVERY USER-FACING STRING SHIPS IN ARABIC AND ENGLISH, AND THAT IS ENFORCED RATHER THAN INTENDED:** `ar.ts` is annotated `: Translation` against `typeof en`, so a missing Arabic value fails `tsc`, which is a required check under `enforce_admins`. **REGISTER #61 WAS STALE FOR NINE DAYS AND NOTHING DETECTED IT.** Remedy (a) shipped in `2968ee5` on 2026-08-24; the row went on describing the corrected sentence as a live public falsehood until today, and that stale text was cited — by an agent that read the row instead of the page — as the reason to sequence this work ahead of #54. The decision survived on the corrected ground (both stores gate on deletion; #54 gates no store) but it was re-made rather than inherited. **It earns a line in #61 and a new §5 rule, not its own row**, on the ruling that kept `profiles_id_fkey` inside #62. **NO DDL, no migration, no workflow change.** Pre-existing and explicitly not fixed here: the privacy page is hardcoded English throughout, and `next lint` reports 9 errors on `main` — zero of them in the files this PR adds.)

### 2026-09-02 - the preflight read lands: Q8, Q10 and the function body are answered, the load-bearing result is a measured ABSENCE, and register #62 still does not close

(**THE THREE QUESTIONS THE 2026-08-24 LIVE READ LEFT UNRUN ARE NOW RUN**, against `wnpqdafdkbuvwecksrjj` at **2026-09-02T22:50:37Z**, PostgreSQL **17.6**, role proven `postgres` by an identity probe rather than read off a label — this build of the SQL editor renders no role selector, and a catalog read against the wrong database returning plausible rows is the worst outcome available. **THE LOAD-BEARING RESULT IS AN ABSENCE AND IT IS RECORDED AS A MEASUREMENT: ZERO delete-side triggers, ZERO rewrite rules, across `public` and `auth`.** Exactly one non-internal trigger exists — `on_auth_user_created`, `AFTER INSERT` on `auth.users`, executing `handle_new_user` (`SECURITY DEFINER`), enabled `O` — **and that is the first live confirmation it exists at all**, closing an open assumption `docs/b1-verification-protocol.md` §2.6.5 had rested on since it was written. **AN ABSENCE IS THE ONE CLAIM `supabase/migrations/` CAN NEVER SUPPORT**, which is why it is dated, query-cited, and written into the register rather than carried in a session. Building the deletion orchestrator on an unrecorded absence would have been register #62 committed a second time, by the arc opened to repair it. **`rls_auto_enable` IS BENIGN, READ FROM ITS OWN SOURCE RATHER THAN ITS METADATA** — an `event_trigger` function that enables RLS on newly created `public` tables and touches nothing existing; the repository previously knew only that its `search_path` was pinned, and a pinned `search_path` is not a reviewed body. Its body held no credential-shaped value and nothing was redacted. **All four CHECK constraints are present with the exact declared definitions**, so register #42's `Quiz.lang`/`StudyEventKind` exclusion holds and is now live-backed instead of assumed. **No unrecorded function or event trigger exists BEYOND THE ONE THIS REGISTER ALREADY NAMES** — 13 rows: six project functions, five declared in the migrations folder and `rls_auto_enable` declared nowhere in it; seven event triggers, of which six are Supabase platform machinery and the seventh, `ensure_rls`, is `rls_auto_enable`'s and is equally undeclared. **So the REVIEW half of divergence class (4) is discharged and the DIVERGENCE half is not** — a rebuild from `supabase/migrations/` still produces a database without either object. Reviewed is not recorded, and this block does not pretend otherwise. **THREE MISTAKES IN THE READ'S OWN DESIGN ARE RECORDED HERE, NOT SMOOTHED OVER, BECAUSE THE QUERIES ARE NOW CHECKED IN AND ANY RE-RUN INHERITS THEM.** The expected function set was wrong twice — pgvector installs into `public`, so the first census returned 131 rows; and `current_streak` was omitted although it has been in the folder since July. ### 2026-08-01 - ingestion failures become visible: the four defects behind a nine-day outage (register #54)

(**Production upload and Ask were broken from 2026-07-23 to 2026-08-01 and NOTHING RECORDED IT.** This PR does not fix the outage — that was a credential repair, done in the dashboards and described below — it fixes the four reasons **nobody could tell**. **THE OUTAGE, stated plainly so the record is not a reconstruction:** `INGESTION_TOKEN` differed between Railway and Vercel, so every production `POST /convert` returned **401**. Repair, executed 2026-08-01: a fresh 64-hex value set on Railway; an **authenticated probe against `/convert` returning 200** with one chunk and a 1024-float embedding, plus a **negative control** — the same value with one character flipped, sent well-formed as `Bearer <64-hex>` — returning **401 `{"detail":"Invalid token"}"` from the comparison at `main.py:58` rather than from the format check at `:56`; the same fresh value then set on Vercel Production and Preview; a Vercel production redeploy; and a real browser upload reaching **`status='ready'` with 2 chunks** followed by an Ask returning a substantive Arabic summary from the document's own content. **A REJECTED HYPOTHESIS IS RECORDED HERE RATHER THAN DELETED, because it was the leading one for most of the incident:** that the 07-23 rotation was never followed by a Vercel redeploy, leaving the running deployment on a stale value — Vercel env changes apply only to NEW deployments, so the mechanism is real. **It is refuted by the Vercel deployment list:** production deploys completed on Jul 24 (`140bc31`), Jul 26 (`eaad752`) and Jul 29 (`ee45958`), all after the rotation, so any correctly-written value would have gone live within a day. **The surviving explanation is that Vercel's value was never actually rotated on 07-23, or was written wrong** — which makes the root cause the ABSENCE OF A POST-ROTATION VERIFICATION STEP, not a missed redeploy, and which raises a documentation-integrity question this PR deliberately does NOT resolve: **the 2026-07-23 §7 block closes register #45 asserting the rotation was "verified by a real production upload reaching READY", and that assertion cannot be reconciled with an outage beginning the same day.** Under **#39**'s own standard a ✅ needs live-proof AND repo-reproducibility, and #45's rests on an attested upload with no artifact behind it. **Resolving that belongs to the next docs-only PR, as a NEW superseding entry — §7 blocks are immutable and #45's row is not edited here.** **WHAT THIS PR CHANGES — four files under `src/`, one register row, this block.** `src/app/api/ingest/route.ts`: the `!pyResponse.ok` branch now reads the upstream body (failure path only, `.catch()`-guarded so a body-read failure cannot mask the status), logs it, and writes `ingestion service returned <status>: <body, 500 chars>` into `error_message` — **truncated because it goes to the database**, and safe to record because `error_message` is written by this route and **rendered nowhere under `src/`** (grep-verified, not assumed). The user-facing `{success:false, error:'Ingestion failed'}` body and its 500 are **byte-unchanged**. `src/app/api/agent/route.ts`: the `embedQuery` catch now emits a greppable **`[retrieval-infra-failure]`** tag naming the cause; **the user still sees the ordinary "can't find that" answer — CHANGING THAT COPY IS EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE** and deferred, so the repair PR stays narrow. `src/lib/limits-server.ts`: `checkDocumentLimit` gains `.neq('status','error')`, so **our failures stop consuming the user's quota**; this opens no bypass because `enforceLimit`'s daily upload cap still gates every attempt before any work happens. `src/lib/ingestion.ts`: the module-scope `SERVICE_URL` const becomes an exported **`getServiceUrl()`** that throws when `INGESTION_SERVICE_URL` is unset **in production** and keeps the localhost fallback for development — and `route.ts`'s **duplicate** copy of the same fallback now calls it, so the two callers of this service cannot drift. A function, not a const, so the value is read at call time rather than import time. **WHAT THIS PR DOES NOT DO, listed because a half-fix that reads as a whole one is how this incident happened:** it adds **no deployment definition** (`railway.json`/`railway.toml`/`nixpacks.toml` still do not exist anywhere in this repo), **no `healthcheckPath`**, **no monitoring or alerting of any kind**, and **no verification step in the rotation runbook** — so the detection latency for the NEXT credential desynchronization is improved only to "the first log anyone reads", and the rotation that caused this one remains exactly as repeatable. Those are register **#54**'s open half and they are **not optional**. **Also recorded, unprompted by any doc:** a **Railway variable edit is a deploy trigger** — the 2026-08-01 token write rebuilt and shipped `main`'s head, real build, non-zero duration, which happened to be `ee45958` and therefore happened to be wanted. With empty Watch Paths an env tweak ships whatever is on `main` at that instant. **VERIFICATION HONESTY:** `tsc --noEmit` passes and is the only automated evidence here; `db-types` passes **vacuously** — this PR contains zero SQL and no migration. Neither check exercises a failure path, so **the logging added here is unproven until a real ingestion failure occurs**, and the first honest test of it will be an incident. **Scope:** four files under `src/`, plus this file. No migration, no DB change, no dashboard change, no branch-protection change, no change to `services/ingestion/`. `PIVOT_PLAN.md` untouched; §1 B5b stays ⬜; registers **#17**, **#51**, **#52**, **#53** all stay OPEN and untouched.)

### 2026-07-26 - (b1) PR A of 3: ingestion service gains /ingest and KEEPS /convert (register #51)

(**Enabler (b1) is shipping as THREE PRs, not one, and this is PR A — SERVICE-ONLY. `services/ingestion/` gains `/ingest` (convert → chunk → embed → persist chunks → write the terminal `documents` status) while KEEPING `/convert` byte-identical to its pre-(b1) contract. `src/app/api/ingest/route.ts` is deliberately UNTOUCHED and production Next still calls `/convert`.** **Why split what #70 had as one PR:** Railway auto-deploys `main` and Vercel deploys `main`, independently and in an order nobody controls. A single merge that renamed the endpoint AND repointed the caller therefore had a real skew window: if Vercel won the race, the new route's `/ingest` call hit an image that did not have it yet — a 404, `!pyResponse.ok`, and **every upload failing** until the Docker build landed. Restoring `/convert` closes the *other* direction (new image, old caller) and does nothing for that one. Landing the SERVICE first, with both endpoints live, is what makes the window zero-length: at this merge the old caller finds `/convert`; at PR B's merge the new caller finds an `/ingest` that is already running and already verified. **`/convert` is a shim with three deliberate omissions, each load-bearing:** no chunk/embedding length guard (the old contract truncates via `zip` and the old caller tolerates a missing `embedding` key — the guard is a better behaviour and a *different* contract); no `Form(...)` parameters (the old caller posts `file` alone, so an extra required field would 422 every production upload); and **no Supabase client and no persistence whatsoever** (the old caller does its own inserts — a write here would double-write every chunk for the shim's whole life). Response is `{markdown, filename, chunks[]}` with `chunks` produced by the *same* `_chunk_text` function `/ingest` uses, so a silent field mismatch is not constructible. **Accepted and TIME-BOXED regression:** the 9-13× return blob register **#50** measured is reachable again for the span of PRs A→C. That is the price of a zero-window sequence and it is **not** a design reversal — **PR C deletes `/convert` and is not optional**; if it is skipped, #50's blob endpoint stays live and the "loud 404 on version skew" property this design depends on is silently gone. **VERIFICATION HONESTY — this PR's green checkmark is not evidence.** The two required checks are `tsc` and `db-types`; this PR contains zero TypeScript and zero SQL, so **both pass vacuously**, and Railway's "Wait for CI" is OFF so the deploy does not even wait for them. The real Python evidence is the `ingestion-image` job added by PR #71 (register **#52**), which is **not** a required check. The only proof that this image works in *production* is an unauthenticated `GET /health` after the swap returning **`"supabase_configured": true`** — one probe that proves the container booted, the module-scope `supabase` import resolved on linux, and both env vars are visible to the process. **PR B MUST NOT be merged until that probe passes**, because the fast rollback (Railway dashboard → previous deployment, no rebuild, valid because production Next still calls `/convert`) stops being valid the instant PR B lands. **Also corrected here:** `docs/supabase-migration-runbook.md:101` and `:140` instructed the operator to *verify Railway holds no `SUPABASE_*` vars* — now false, since the service legitimately holds `SUPABASE_URL` + `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` (anon, **never** service-role — register **#45** blast radius). The same lines wrongly called the service a **separate repo**; it is `services/ingestion/` in this one. **Scope:** `services/ingestion/main.py`, `services/ingestion/requirements.txt` (adds `supabase==2.29.0`), `docs/supabase-migration-runbook.md`, `docs/b1-verification-protocol.md` (new — the pinned definition of "verified" for this sequence), and this file. **Zero files under `src/`.** No migration, no DB change; `backfill.py` untouched; `PIVOT_PLAN.md` untouched; §1 B5b row stays ⬜; register **#17** stays OPEN.)

### 2026-07-26 - (b1) PR A0 of 3: Python finally has a CI gate (registers #52, #53)

(**PR A0 is CI-ONLY and changes no service behaviour whatsoever — zero files under `src/`, zero files under `services/`.** It adds one workflow, `.github/workflows/ingestion-image.yml`, and this file. **THE GAP IT CLOSES (register #52):** the two required checks are `tsc` and `db-types`, and **neither inspects a single line of Python**. Neither carries a `paths:` filter, so both **run and report green** on a service-only PR — a **vacuous pass**, a checkmark that vouches for nothing. Railway's **"Wait for CI" is OFF** and **Watch Paths is empty**, so a merge to `main` starts the image build immediately and would not pause for those checks even if they meant something. Meanwhile `services/ingestion/main.py` imports `supabase` symbols at **module scope** (`:13`) and calls `tiktoken.get_encoding` at **module scope** (`:50`), so a bad resolve is a **boot-time crash**, and all four endpoints share **one uvicorn process** — a boot crash takes the **Ask path** down with the **upload path**. **WHAT THE CHECK PROVES:** it builds `services/ingestion/` through **Railway's own build context** (Root Directory `/services/ingestion`, empty Dockerfile Path — read from the dashboard 2026-07-26, not assumed), then **imports the module inside the built image** and **boots the container and probes `/health`** — which is strictly stronger than an import, because it catches ASGI/lifespan startup failures a bare import does not. **WHAT IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT PROVE:** that the Railway **environment** is correct. CI has no `SUPABASE_URL`/`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`, so its `/health` correctly reports **`supabase_configured: false`**, and the job asserts only `"ok":true`. That separation is load-bearing and must not be collapsed: this job is the CI twin of the **live boot probe**, and the **environment probe has no CI twin and cannot have one**. **THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS CHECK SHIPPED THE EXACT DEFECT IT EXISTS TO END, AND ITS OWN FIRST RUN EXPOSED IT — recorded here rather than quietly amended away.** Run **30218194135** on PR **#71** reported the `ingestion-image` job **GREEN in 38 seconds**, and its import step emitted **zero bytes of stdout**: no `ROUTES:` line, no `IMPORT OK`, no `IMPORT FAILED`. Cause, proven by direct experiment rather than inferred: `python -` reads its program from **stdin**, and `docker run` **without `-i`** does not attach stdin to the container, so python read EOF, executed an **empty program**, exited **0**, and the step passed having asserted nothing. **A workflow written to end vacuous green checks shipped a vacuous green check.** **Severity, stated accurately rather than dramatically:** the job was not proving *nothing* about the import — the boot probe transitively proves the module imports, because uvicorn cannot serve `/health` without importing `main:app`. What the dead step actually cost was the **route assertions**, the `ROUTES:` evidence line, and the **network-vs-`ImportError` diagnostic banner**. **TWO fixes, and the second is the one that matters:** (1) add `-i`; (2) pipe the step through `tee` and make a trailing **`grep -q "IMPORT OK"`** the thing that actually fails it. GitHub runs steps with **`bash -e` and NOT `-o pipefail`** (`shell: /usr/bin/bash -e {0}`), so `tee` would otherwise mask a non-zero `docker run`. Both paths were tested locally under `bash -e` before pushing: with `-i` the step exits **0** and prints `IMPORT OK`; with `-i` removed the step now exits **1**, so the regression cannot silently return. **The generalised rule, and the reason register #52 exists: a check step must assert on its own OUTPUT, not merely on its exit code.** **AUDIT OF THE REMAINING STEPS under that same lens, so the fix is not mistaken for a sweep:** `Build the ingestion image` is **not** exposed — its exit code and its effect are the same fact, since `COPY requirements.txt` / `COPY main.py` make a wrong context or a missing file fail the build loudly, and there is nothing further to assert on; `Boot the container and probe /health` already asserts on its **payload** (`grep -q '"ok":true'`), and that grep sits in the `if` **body** where `bash -e` applies, so a wrong payload does red the step; `Check out the repository` is self-verifying downstream; `Container logs (always)` is `|| true` by design and claims nothing. **The import step was the only exposed one. No other fix was invented.** **NOT A REQUIRED CHECK, DELIBERATELY, AND NOT ADDED TO BRANCH PROTECTION.** This job has **three** third-party network dependencies in its hot path — Docker Hub for `python:3.11-slim`, PyPI for `markitdown[all]` + `supabase`, and Azure blob for `tiktoken`'s `cl100k_base` (**#53**) — where `db-types` now has **one**, digest-pinned. **Register #43 is precisely this class:** it redded `main` **twice** on `docker: toomanyrequests` with **no commit to this repo**, and its residual (1) records that Docker Hub is **also** anonymous-rate-limited per IP with GitHub runners sharing NAT pools. #43's governing quote applies unchanged: *"A gate that reds `main` on a coin flip is worse than no gate."* **PROMOTION RULE, binding and recorded in #52:** required-check status is considered **only** after **20 consecutive green runs on `main` with zero re-runs**; **any red caused by a third party's network RESETS the counter to zero** and weakens rather than strengthens the case. Never being promoted is an **acceptable outcome**. **The base image is NOT digest-pinned in the workflow, deliberately:** `services/ingestion/Dockerfile` says `FROM python:3.11-slim`, and pinning in CI but not in the Dockerfile would make CI green on an image production never builds — a **silent false-green**, worse than the flakiness it would avoid. If the base should be pinned, it must be pinned in the Dockerfile so both paths move together; that is a service PR, not this one. **REGISTER CHOICES, argued rather than assumed:** **#52** is its own row rather than folded into **#51** because the vacuous-green gap **predates (b1) and outlives it** — #51 closes when PR C lands, and #52's promotion obligation must not close with it. **#53** is its own row because it is a **production** cold-start dependency, not a CI one, and it is **pre-existing on `main`** — recording it under a (b1) row would misattribute it as a (b1) regression, which it is not. **#51 is deliberately SKIPPED here and reserved for (b1)'s own row, which PR A adds** — PR **#70**'s body and the (b1) branch already name it #51, and renumbering it now would create drift with text that already exists. **RUNTIME IS NOW MEASURED, NOT ESTIMATED: 38 seconds** on run **30218194135** (build ~24s), against a pre-run projection of **5-9 minutes** — **wrong by an order of magnitude**, and the projection is recorded here rather than deleted so the next estimate is made with a known error bar. The reason it was wrong: every dependency in `markitdown[all]` resolves to a **prebuilt manylinux wheel** and GitHub's path to PyPI is very fast, so nothing is compiled from source. **Two riders on that number.** It was measured against **`main`'s `requirements.txt`, which does NOT yet contain `supabase==2.29.0`** — **PR A's run will be slower**, though on this evidence nowhere near the original projection. And it was measured on the **defective** run, whose import step was a no-op; the corrected step adds a real container start, so the true figure is slightly higher again. `timeout-minutes: 20` now carries far more headroom than intended, which is left as-is: a generous ceiling costs nothing and a hung third-party pull still fails loudly instead of burning the 6-hour default. **The first green run on `main` of the CORRECTED workflow is what satisfies the sequence's V11 condition** — the 38s figure above came from a run whose central assertion did not execute, so it pins the runtime and nothing else. **Costs nothing:** this repository is **PUBLIC** (`gh repo view` → `visibility: PUBLIC`), and GitHub bills Actions minutes only for private repositories. **Needs NO secret** — no registry credential, no Supabase key, not `INGESTION_TOKEN`; `permissions: contents: read` is the entire grant, so the job cannot reach, read, or mutate production. **Deliberately NOT in this PR:** `docs/b1-verification-protocol.md` (belongs to PR A), any change to `services/ingestion/`, any branch-protection change, any dashboard change, and the closing of PR **#70**. **Main tip verified this session** via the check-runs API rather than recalled: `tsc` and `db-types` both `success` on `140bc31`.)

### 2026-07-23 - B5b upload-hardening scoping recorded (register #50, docs/b5b-scoping.md)

(**B5b (deep upload content hardening) is now SCOPED, not started — register #50 opened, `docs/b5b-scoping.md` created; §1 B5b stays ⬜ because scoping is not progress on the gate.** The doc records this session's established + verified facts, its corrections, the split, and seven OPEN founder decisions. **Established (each verified against the code this session):** the real upload ceiling is Vercel's **hard ~4.5 MB edge cap** (platform constant, unraisable — `413 FUNCTION_PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` before the handler), **not** the 50 MB the app promises in **7 places** (`route.ts:51` check / `:52` hardcoded-English 413 message — register-#17 class, `DropZone.tsx:26`, and four i18n strings in `en.ts`/`ar.ts`); ingestion runs on **Railway** (`INGESTION_SERVICE_URL = knowflow-production.up.railway.app`), so the `route.ts`→`/convert` hop faces no second Vercel cap but a **9-13× return-path blob** — `/convert` returns each chunk's **1024-float embedding inline plus full markdown** (`main.py`: `EMBED_DIM=1024`, `CHUNK_TOKENS=512`, `CHUNK_OVERLAP=64`), and `await pyResponse.json()` materializes it in the Next function's memory; and **no `maxDuration` is configured anywhere** (grep-empty across `vercel.json`/`next.config.ts`/`package.json`/`src`), with Voyage embeds batched 128-at-60s and Supabase inserts batched 50, all sequential in one invocation — so the true user-facing ceiling is an **UNMEASURED duration limit**, not size. **Corrections recorded so they are not re-inherited:** the 4.5 MB cap does **NOT** mitigate decompression bombs (it bounds compressed bytes, never the expansion — 4.5 MB of crafted ZIP still exhausts the service); the pre-auth body-buffering concern is **bounded** by the edge cap, making the `getUser`-above-`formData` reorder a low-priority ride-along (`checkDocumentLimit` **cannot** move — it needs `kbId` from the body; `enforceLimit` **should not** — it would burn an upload credit on a rejected request, register #24); and **`PIVOT_PLAN.md:269`'s "Size cap (50 MB) already exists" is a documented FALSE claim** (the effective cap is the platform's ~4.5 MB), corrected here, `PIVOT_PLAN.md` deliberately untouched. **The split:** B5b-1 (request-path, doable now — magic-byte verification, a declared-size ZIP-ratio pre-check from the central directory, the auth reorder); B5b-2 (gated on **B6** — enforced expansion bounds, nested-archive depth, AV/content scanning, all needing actual expansion or a worker); plus enabler moves **(b1)** move chunk+embedding persistence into the Railway service (kills the 9-13× blob, relocates slow work to the only component without a duration ceiling — the enabler of B6, not B6 itself) and **(b2)** signed-URL direct-to-Supabase-storage upload (the only way past the 4.5 MB inbound cap; gated on the file-size ruling; #22's 5 GB/mo egress limits its payoff to ~100 uploads/mo at 50 MB). **Seven OPEN decisions (stated with consequences, none picked): (1)** max file size — ~4.5 MB vs. real 50 MB via (b2), gating the enforcement point and sync/async posture; **(2)** format list — keep six vs. drop the docx/pptx/xlsx ZIP trio that carries all bomb risk; **(3)** scanning scope — AV (nothing in the image today), PII, and **prompt-injection in extracted text** (flows unfiltered into Haiku via `<document>` interpolation across summarize/quiz/agent — unmitigated, needs at minimum a documented accepted-risk decision); **(4)** failure posture — reject-at-upload (415) vs. accept-then-quarantine; **(5)** enforcement point — Next, Railway, or both (Railway trusts its caller on a single bearer token, so Next-only is bypassed if `INGESTION_TOKEN` leaks); **(6)** the three ZIP numbers (expansion ratio, absolute expanded cap, nesting depth) — each measured from the spoofable declared central directory vs. actual expansion; **(7)** the signature table (`%PDF-`; `PK\x03\x04` + inner part-path for the OOXML trio; the txt/md non-signature UTF-8/NUL rule — txt/md have no magic bytes at all). **OUTSTANDING measurement:** the duration ceiling — plain-`.txt`-on-Preview procedure + a failure-signature table (`200`+chunk_count=pass; `413`+Vercel HTML=edge cap; `413`+app JSON=app check; `500`+"Ingestion failed"; `504`/none=duration), each upload burning a rate-limit credit and the #22 free tier. Related registers: **#45** (the duplicate ingestion service closed while scoping this), **#22**, **#24**, **#17**. **Docs-only: no code, no migration, no DB, no route/validation/size-constant change; only `docs/b5b-scoping.md` (new) and this file changed; `Phase 7` stays ⬜, §1 B5b row untouched, `PIVOT_PLAN.md` untouched, register #17 stays OPEN.**)

(**GATE-INTEGRITY ARC — one purpose, three register moves: the `db-types` REQUIRED check can no longer pass on a wrong result, nor red on a third party's traffic.** **(1) Register #40 CLOSED (PR #62, merge `d8c8fde`).** The gate's `002_storage.sql` skip was keyed on FILENAME, so a `public`-schema object added to a skipped file would be omitted from the generated types while `db-types` stayed GREEN — and the script *printed* the claim "Skipped migrations create no objects in the public schema" with nothing executing it. Honest limit kept in the row: a fail-closed **STATIC heuristic** with a documented undecidable case (`$$`/dynamic SQL → REJECTED), **not a proof of application**. **(2) Register #43 OPENED AND CLOSED (PR #63, merge `3378dd8`).** `gen types` shells out to `docker run public.ecr.aws/supabase/postgres-meta:v0.96.6` — a mutable tag on a rate-limited anonymous registry, the last gate dependency outside the script's digest-pinning discipline. Now pinned by index digest `sha256:a84cc713…`, pre-pulled from **Docker Hub** (byte-identical digest at both registries) and retagged to the exact ECR reference; the retag is REQUIRED, not defensive. In the SCRIPT, not the workflow, per `db-types.yml:57-60`. Proven on `3378dd8`: `toomanyrequests` **0**, `Pulling from supabase/postgres-meta` **0**, `Unable to find image` fired only for `PG_IMAGE`. (**Register #39 added — the repo↔live schema-parity STANDING INVARIANT: a schema change is NOT resolved until its migration is committed to the repo; "live-proven" and "repo-reproducible" are TWO SEPARATE CLAIMS and a ✅ requires BOTH.** Written because it was **violated**: PR **#43** shipped the per-language quiz route with **no migration at all**, and register **#31** carried a ✅ RESOLVED (alongside Phase 5's ✅ DONE) for a full day while a clean rebuild from `supabase/migrations/` produced a `quizzes` table the merged route **could not run against**. PR **#46** (merge `531b8a2`) closed the divergence; **#39** records the rule so it is not re-learned. **#39 extends register #23 to be BIDIRECTIONAL** — #23's standing rule guards **repo→live** ("never treat a written migration file as an applied one", which *presupposes the file exists*); this failure was **live→repo** (it ran; no file existed), the one direction #23 could never have caught. **A working preview is NOT evidence of repo parity** — the preview runs against the live DB, so an out-of-band change makes the app work **and** leaves the repo broken at the same time. The check is a **rebuild, not a preview**. **#23's row now carries a ⚠️ pointer to #39**, so the rule and its extension are read together. Enforcement remains **prose only, and prose already failed once** — a CI gate is logged as a **Phase 7** item.
